# points.md — ACTIVE part (currently entries #1416 onward)

**This is the real, currently-open tail of the single, canonical
points.md ledger — the file new entries get appended to.** Split
across multiple files purely because GitHub won't render a file over
~512KB in the browser; no entry content was changed, reworded, or
reordered. See `points/INDEX.md` for the full real map of which part holds
which entries, and `points.md` (repo root) for the short, canonical pointer
every session should still start from.

**Naming convention, for future-me:** this file keeps the stable name
`points_active.md` (no entry range in the filename) for as long as
it's still being appended to, so appending never requires a rename.
Once it approaches ~350KB, seal it — rename to `points_NN_XXX-YYY.md`
with its real final range, start a fresh, empty `points_active.md`
for continued work, and add the sealed file's own row to `points/INDEX.md`.

---

## #1416 (19 Nov 2026) -- THE LEDGER IS SEALED THROUGH #1415 (part 14), AND THIS FILE STARTS FRESH

*Asked by Alan (19 Nov, 20:58):* "yes seal and start a new one, it gets full fast" (answering my question after the status test failed on size: `points_active.md` was 465,537 bytes, limit 450 KB of `tests/test_status_files_v1.py`; the failure came with my entry #1415 and I had committed and pushed it before reading the test result: my mistake, noted).
*The seal.* `points_active.md` (#1236-#1415) is now `points/points_14_1236-1415.md`; this file starts fresh at #1416. Proof it is lossless: everything after the part's header is byte-for-byte the body of the old active file (the body, from the `## #1236` heading to the end, is 464544 bytes, sha256 `1b7e745aa08e97f238773ccdca6d8e15c26e0562623981a7b48edd61cb71b3e0`; the old whole file was 465537 bytes, sha256 `c29bd95ccb7413f4931e11206dc2fdb382d2dddf964d263e1db73d105d863e87`; checked by reading the new part back and comparing). Only a header was added, in the same words as part 13's with the range changed. No entry was edited, renumbered or reordered. The old file is also in git (the commit before this one).
*Pointers updated:* `points/INDEX.md` (a row for part 14, the open file's row, the "of N" note), root `points.md`, `current/START.md` (the lines that name the newest sealed part), `CLAUDE.md` (the ledger line and the sealed part to read), `current/latest.md`. Not changed: `tests/test_status_files_v1.py` and `_v2.py` both still exist (which one to keep is still Alan's call).
*Size note:* the open file filled to 455 KB over #1236-#1415 (180 entries in 31 days, about 15 KB a day with the width work); at this rate it reaches the 350 KB sealing mark in roughly three weeks, so sealing is likely to come up again.

## #1417 (19 Nov) -- Card: after a fresh load the FIRST run of each image is slow by a fixed amount and every later run is fast; confirms the reading of #1415 (learned durations order the queue)

**Run (Alan, 19 Nov, about 21:00; `cold_test_v1.sh`: bitstream reloaded before each group; `jobs_order_test2.json`, one block, NU=3 build; all 16 runs 9 of 9 correct).**

| group | run 1 | run 2 | run 3 | run 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A: load, all lw2 x4 | 48,260 | 35,491 | 35,492 | 35,493 |
| B: load, estimate x4 | 43,286 | 36,228 | 36,233 | 36,229 |
| C: load, all lw0, then all lw2 x3 | 117,087 (lw0) | 48,262 | 35,494 | 35,492 |
| D: load, all lw3 x2, then all lw2 x2 | 39,607 (lw3) | 39,612 (lw3) | 48,256 | 35,492 |

**Reading (measured).**
1. **The first run of an image after a load is slow, the next is fast, with no gradual settling:** all lw2 48,256 to 48,262 on its first run (three here, 48,261 in #1414, 48,262 in #1408: five values within 6 clocks) against 35,487 to 35,494 afterwards (12 runs over #1415 and #1417); the estimate's mix 43,282 to 43,289 on its first run (four values with #1408, #1414, #1415) against 36,228 to 36,267 afterwards. The "odd" run of #1408 and the 48,26x reference are the first-run values, reproducible to a few clocks.
2. **It is per width (class and width key), not per file or per load:** in D the lw3 runs show no penalty (39,607 and 39,612, the first no different from the second: with all jobs at lw3 they run one after another, so the order of the queue cannot matter), and the first lw2 run after them is slow again (48,256) although lw3 was already learned; in C the first lw2 run after a lw0 run is slow (48,262), the same as straight after the load in A (48,260).
3. **The 39,100 of #1415 run 1 is a different case:** a first lw2 run after other files had left their own values under the same keys; one run later it was 35,509. Not reproduced here.
4. **The predictions of #1415, checked:** "the first run after a load is not faster than later ones, later runs settle near 35,49x and 36,23x": yes. "In C the lw2 runs after lw0 may start from other values than A": no, the same. "If every first run equals the settled value the reading is wrong": it is not.
5. **The first-in-first-out model of #1411 matches the cold state for all lw2** (49,178 modelled against 48,26x: +1.9%) and for lw0 (115,807 against 117,087: -1.1%); it is 21% too fast for the cold wide-first mix (34,132 against 43,286: the same -21% as the wide-first run of #1407) and 12% too slow for lw3 (44,292 against 39,61x). In the warm state it is wrong for all lw2 (49,178 against 35,49x: +39%). The rough warm variant of #1415 (all jobs queued at once, highest predicted bucket first; not committed) gives 37,293 for all lw2 (+5%), 34,132 for the mix (-6%), 44,292 for lw3 (+12%), 102,339 for lw0 (-13%).

**What this means for use (reasoning from the above, not tested):** a card that has just been loaded runs each new (program number, width) combination slowly once. A warm-up run of the same batch removes the cost for all later runs; the cost is 12,800 clocks (36%) for all lw2 on this batch and 7,060 (19%) for the mix. Comparisons of widths must say whether the runs were cold or warm; the numbers of #1408 and #1409 are a mixture, and a fair rule is to compare the second run of each. Whether the host can seed the duration values before the first run (it cannot today: no command for it is known to me) was not checked in the host link.

**Not done.** A warm mode for the model (queue by bucket, `--estimate` choosing for the warm state) is not built: the offline variant was tried on one batch only; it needs the warm numbers of more batches (`jobs_order_test.json`, `jobs_many_small.json`, `jobs_curve_*`) taken twice in a row after a load. Open: why the cold wide-first mix is 7,060 clocks slower than the warm one (the model does not show it); the lw1 case with 4 or more jobs; the tie of #1407.

## #1418 (19 Nov) -- Own programs: a plain guide (`PROGRAMS.md`) and two new worked examples (`vmin`, `vxor`), checked on the host and in the whole-board simulation; not run on the card

**Asked (Alan, 19 Nov, 21:07 and 21:10):** the user of S-Core supplies the method as well as the data, unlike a fixed engine; the likely use is to make writing a program easy, and examples of how a program is formed will be needed. (My answer, in the conversation: three layers, method + data, a library that hides the method, and execution choices like width that are being automated; Alan: the second is closer to actual use.)

**What already existed (read, not new):** `programs/template.s` (the empty program with the rules in comments), four examples (`crc32`, `vgain`, `vmax`, `vprefix`) with Python answers in `programs/examples.py`, `progs.register()` which checks a program at load time (assembles, fits the code store, trial run on the interpreter, agrees with the Python answer), `jobs_own_example.json`, `test_progs.py`, `test_ownprog_sim.py` (whole board in simulation). What was missing was a guide a non-programmer can follow.

**Added.** `PROGRAMS.md`: the five parts of every program, the rules of the core (RV32I only, no multiply/divide, word loads and stores, the registers the library keeps, the code store limit), `vmax.s` line by line in words, how `vmin` and `vxor` were made from `vmax` and the template, the three steps to use a program (write it, write the answer in Python, name it in a job file), what the host then does (batching, widths, the estimate), a table of the examples. New: `programs/vmin.s` (smallest word; made from `vmax` by changing the start value, the comparison and adding an empty-input line), `programs/vxor.s` (each input XOR the job's `arg`; the template with one changed line plus reading `arg`), `programs/examples_v2.py` (their Python answers; `examples.py` is untouched), `jobs_own_examples2.json` (5 jobs: three vmin including an empty input and two all-ones words, two vxor), `test_own_examples_v1.py`.

**Checks (measured here, none on the card).** (1) `test_own_examples_v1.py`: 9 checks pass (registry load; the interpreter agrees with the Python answer on all five jobs; vxor with too little output room is refused with status 2 and nothing produced; the five jobs compile into one batch). (2) **Whole-board simulation, `python3 test_ownprog_sim.py jobs_own_examples2.json` (iverilog, the RTL cores):** all 5 jobs status 1 and the outputs equal the Python answers (vmin 5, 0xfffffffe, 0; vxor 0xfe 0xfd 0xfc 0xffffff00 and 10 20 30); batch of 34 script steps, code 82 of 128 words, shared 74 of 256 words, 25,997 board clocks; the SDRAM model reports no timing violations. (3) `scblocks.py --plan jobs_own_examples2.json --blocks 1` works (one batch, two kinds). The 71, 51, 38, 70 and 62 instructions of the five jobs are counted by the interpreter.

**Not done.** Not run on the card. The estimate uses the crc32 clocks per instruction for the two new kinds (no measured value; `cpi_run_v1.sh` covers only the four library kinds). The guide is untested on a person: whether Alan can follow it unaided is not known.

## #1419 (19 Nov) -- A ladder from one command to a program of your own (`LADDER.md`, four job files, `ladder_show.py`, `vcount`); host and whole-board simulation checked; not run on the card

**Asked (Alan, 19 Nov, 21:18):** "run from the simplest single set of commands, through to a small program". Read as: a graded walk-through with exact commands, from one job on the card up to a program of his own.

**What it is.** `s-core/LADDER.md`, six steps, each with the same three moves (look first with no card; run on the card with `--out`; compare): step 0 get the card ready (pull, load `sc_k7_fu2s8u8n3.bit`); 1 one job (`ladder_1_one_job.json`: vsum 1+2+3+4 = 10); 2 three jobs of different kinds in one file (`ladder_2_three_jobs.json`: vsum 60, vmax 99, vprefix 1 3 6 10 15); 3 more data with `{"seq": [start, count, step]}` (`ladder_3_more_data.json`: 5050, 134650, 242, 9649; the host splits it into two batches by itself); 4 how wide (the same file at `--lw 0`, `--lw 2` and `--lw auto --profile ... --estimate`, each run twice, compare the second, #1417); 5 a program of his own (`programs/vcount.s`: counts inputs bigger than `arg`, made from `vmax.s`; `ladder_5_my_program.json`: 3, 0, 50; then an exercise: copy it, swap one comparison to count the inputs SMALLER than `arg`, write the one-line Python answer, run it). New files: `ladder_1_one_job.json`, `ladder_2_three_jobs.json`, `ladder_3_more_data.json`, `ladder_5_my_program.json`, `ladder_show.py` (prints what a job file should give; with a result file made by `scblocks.py ... --out` it puts the card's numbers next to the right ones: MATCH or DIFFERENT, exit 1 if any differ), `programs/vcount.s`, `programs/examples_v3.py` (its Python answer; the earlier example files are untouched), `test_ladder_v1.py`.

**Checks (measured here, none on the card).** (1) `test_ladder_v1.py` 12 checks pass: the four job files give the answers the guide promises; all compile; step 3 needs two batches; `ladder_show` says MATCH for a right result file and DIFFERENT (exit 1) for a wrong number; **the exercise of step 5 works** (swapping `bgeu t5,t0,skip` for `bgeu t0,t5,skip` makes the program count the inputs below `arg`; the interpreter agrees with the Python answer). (2) Whole-board simulation (`test_ownprog_sim.py`, iverilog, the RTL cores): `ladder_5_my_program.json` all 3 jobs status 1 and equal to the answers (3, 0, 50), 2 batches, 26 script steps, no SDRAM timing violations; `ladder_2_three_jobs.json` all 3 jobs correct (60, 99, 1 3 6 10 15), 1 batch, 22 steps, 19,899 board clocks, no violations. (3) `scblocks.py --plan` works on all four, also with `--lw 2` and with `--estimate` for step 4 (the estimate chose lw2 for both batches of step 3).

**Not done.** Not run on the card. Steps 1 and 3 were not run in the whole-board simulation (host checks and `--plan` only; the vsum and vmax kinds are covered by earlier sim tests). The `--out` result file and `ladder_show` pairing was tested with a made-up result file in the format `scblocks.py` writes (read from the code), not with a real card output. Step 4 numbers were not measured. Whether Alan can follow it unaided is not known.

## #1420 (19 Nov) -- A front end for the job files: `score_shell_v1.py` (type jobs in, save/open, plan, run, compare; Tab completion; live colours; help file `SHELL.md`); tested with no card, `run` tested against a stand-in

**Asked (Alan, 19 Nov, 21:32):** "lets look at the front end system, add the commands into there, and maybe a way to save or create jobs by entry, include a help file, which lists the commands, maybe even coloured prompts as you type?"

**What existed (read, not new).** `sccli.py` (a `cmd.Cmd` shell for the Tang Nano 20K scheduler over a serial link: system files of named programs, `run NAME COUNT`, `hold`/`go`, `peek`/`poke`) and `scwin.py` (a window version). Neither talks to the Kintex card's job-file path (`scblocks.py jtag FILE.json`), which is the line the width work is on. Left unchanged; a new shell was written for the job-file path instead of editing a proven file.

**Built: `score_shell_v1.py`** (new file). The jobs you type are kept in the job-file JSON format, so `save` writes a file the other tools read and `open` reads one back. Commands (the list is in `SHELL.md` and in `help`): `add KIND DATA [arg=N] [lw=N|auto] [crit]` (numbers, hex, or `seq START COUNT STEP`; the right answer is shown at once; library programs come in by themselves), `list`, `show` (jobs with their answers), `remove`, `dup`, `clear`, `new`, `save`, `open`, `programs`, `register NAME FILE.s [expect=..] [out=..]` (your own program, checked at once), `settings` / `set` (blocks, lw, order, profile, estimate, margin, slices, unit-lw, port, out), `plan`, `run`, `check`, `profile`, `load`, `help [COMMAND]`, `quit` (warns once about unsaved jobs). Several commands on a line with `;`, `#` comments, files of commands with `-f`, `-c`, `-q`, commands piped on standard input. `plan` and `run` build the arguments and call `scblocks.main`, so the profile, estimate, order and widths behave exactly as on the command line; `run` then compares the card's results with the right answers (MATCH or DIFFERENT, via `ladder_show`).
**While you type:** Tab completes commands, kinds, settings, setting values, profile names and file names. With the optional package `prompt_toolkit` the line is coloured live: a known command green, an unknown one red, kinds cyan, numbers yellow, options magenta, and completions appear as you type; without it (or with `SCORE_SHELL_NO_PT=1`) the shell uses the standard `readline` Tab completion and coloured prompts and messages. `NO_COLOR=1`, `--plain`, or output that is not a terminal give no colour codes.
**Help file:** `SHELL.md` is generated from the same table the shell uses (`python3 score_shell_v1.py --write-help`); a test checks that the file equals what the shell generates, that every command has usage and help, and that every command and setting appears.

**Checks (measured here).** `test_score_shell_v1.py`, 43 checks pass: adding with hex, `seq`, `arg=`, bad kind, bad number, missing `seq` arguments and a width the compiler refuses are each an error with nothing added; `list`/`show`/`dup`/`remove`; **save/open round trip keeps the jobs exactly, and the saved file loads with `scbatch.load_jobs`**; quit warning; settings; `plan` and `plan` with the estimate (vsum lw2 and the vgain jobs lw0, vsum submitted first, as #1411); registering your own program; completion; colouring (a pure function, tested without a terminal); the help file. **Found and fixed by the test:** a job file saved outside the `s-core` folder could not find its program sources (paths in a job file are relative to the file); `save` now writes them relative to where the file is saved. **Real terminal, by pseudo-terminal:** with `prompt_toolkit` 3.0.53 the typed line came out with colour codes (command green, numbers yellow, an unknown kind red) and the job was added; with the fallback, Tab completed `vsu` to `vsum` and the job was added. Found and fixed: Tab did nothing in the fallback because this Python's `readline` is libedit and needs `bind ^I rl_complete`. The other host tests pass.

**Not done / not known.** `run` has not been used on the card: the test replaces `scblocks.main` with a stand-in and checks the arguments passed (`jtag`, blocks, submit order, `--out`) and the compare; the real call is the one `scblocks.py` already makes. `prompt_toolkit` is not known to be installed in Alan's nix shell (the shell works without it; `pip install prompt_toolkit` or a nix package would add the live colours); the fallback was tested with libedit here, not with GNU readline. There is no command for loading a bitstream (it needs the openXC7 tools; `load` prints the command), none for calibration (`sc_calibrate_v1.py` stays a separate program), and no editing of a job in place (remove and add again). Not tried on a person.

## #1421 (19 Nov) -- Card: the first run through `score_shell_v1.py` (3 jobs, 3 of 3 correct); the shell without `prompt_toolkit` shows the fallback banner

**Run (Alan, 19 Nov, about 21:40; shell started with `nix shell nixpkgs#openocd --command python3 score_shell_v1.py`; jobs typed in the shell: `add vsum 1 2 3 4`, `add vmax seq 5 80 3`, `add vxor 1 2 3 arg=255`, `save first.json`, then `run`; board version 12, 8 slices per block, one block; build not stated in the paste, an 8-slice build).** One batch of 3 jobs (vmax, vsum, vxor; code 106 of 128 words, shared 116 of 256), 22 script steps, 310 words loaded into far RAM in 0.6 s, finished in 0.08 s: **87,180 clocks = 1.74 ms**, **3 of 3 jobs correct**, results written to `shell_results.json`; the shell's compare showed all three MATCH (10; 242; 254 253 252).
**Reading (measured).** (1) The `run` path of the shell works on the card: this is the first time it ran for real (#1420 tested it against a stand-in). (2) An earlier attempt in a shell started WITHOUT the openocd wrapper gave the shell's error `LinkError: openocd not found: run nix shell nixpkgs#openocd first`; the shell stayed usable and the jobs were already saved; as designed, not a fault. (3) The three jobs ran at width 0 (no `lw` set); whether the run was cold or warm (#1417) is not known (not the first run of the session or load, as far as the paste shows, but it is not stated); the clocks are therefore not a width comparison.
**Noted by Alan:** the banner `Tab completion (install prompt_toolkit for live colours)`: that shell has no `prompt_toolkit`, so the live colouring is off there; Tab completion and the coloured messages are on. Not an error. Two ways to add it are not tested on Alan's machine (pip into the user's Python, or a nix shell with a Python that has the package); see the conversation.

## #1422 (19 Nov) -- `make`: build a new job kind from a recipe in the shell (`jobmaker_v1.py`); `prompt_toolkit` colours now working on Alan's machine

**Asked (Alan, 19 Nov, 21:49):** "ok now we have colours and tabbed prompts. now the build of new job types." The colours: Alan reports the live colours and tab prompts work after the nix command given in the conversation (the first nix command of mine was wrong: with `--expr` nix reads every argument as an expression; corrected to one `buildEnv` expression); which of the two routes he used was not stated. This closes the open point in #1421.
**Reading of the request (mine, stated so it can be corrected):** a way to make a new KIND of job without writing assembly.
**Built.** `jobmaker_v1.py` (new): a recipe is steps applied to every input word, optionally ended by one summing-up. Steps `add sub xor and or shl shr` (operand: a number, or `arg` = the job's arg word) and `not`; endings `then sum|xor|or|and|min|max` (one word out; without an ending one word out per word in). 32-bit wrapping; `shr` logical; shift by `arg` uses its low 5 bits; an ending over no inputs gives 0 (same as vmax/vmin). It produces (1) RV32I source in the shape of `programs/template.s` / `vxor.s` / `vmin.s` (constants that do not fit a 12-bit immediate sit in a2..a7, which the library never changes; `not` is `xori t0,t0,-1`), (2) a Python reference, (3) `verify()`: 12 runs on the reference interpreter (0, 1, 2, 5, 17 words; random data incl. 0, 1, 0xFFFFFFFF, 0x80000000; arg incl. 0, 1, 31, 32, all ones, random) compared with the Python answer. `score_shell_v1.py` (edited in place, logged here): commands `make NAME STEP...` and `code KIND`; made kinds are kept in `programs/made/NAME.s/.py/.json`, found again by `add` in later sessions, listed by `programs`, completed by Tab, coloured as kinds; a made name is never overwritten with a different recipe (same recipe = reuse; failure of any check deletes the files it just wrote). `SHELL.md` regenerated; `PROGRAMS.md` has a short section; `README.md` row; `made_demo_v1.score` (card demo: 3 made kinds, 5 jobs, `plan`, `run`).
**Measured (this computer, no card).** `test_jobmaker_v1.py`: 29 checks, all ok, including 80 random recipes (0..5 steps, random operands incl. 32-bit constants, negative numbers, `arg`, random endings) each built through the shell, assembled, fitted and agreed with the Python answer on the interpreter. The existing `test_score_shell_v1.py` still passes (43 checks). The interpreter run caught one real fault while building: the assembler has no `not` pseudo-instruction (the program failed to assemble; the shell refused the recipe and left no files); fixed by using `xori`. Found while building: `progs.register`'s own trial run uses 4 inputs and arg 0 only, so an arg-dependent program is not tested with a non-zero arg by it; `verify()` covers that for made kinds (the same gap exists for hand-registered kinds; not changed).
**Not known / not done.** (1) Not run on the card: the interpreter and the Python answer are my own pair (the interpreter is the independent RV32I reference, but the card result is the real test); `made_demo_v1.score` is for that. (2) Length estimate: a made kind has no measured CPI, so `--estimate` treats it like crc32 (same caveat as vmin/vxor/vcount). (3) Code size: each made kind costs about 15 to 30 words of the 128-word code store; batches that mix many made kinds can overflow (the compiler reports it). (4) Only "same steps on every word, plus one summing-up": no loops over neighbours, tables or early exit (those need a hand-written program). (5) `make` keeps files in the repo folder `programs/made/`; Alan's own kinds are his to commit.

## #1423 (19 Nov) -- Card: `made_demo_v1.score` (three made kinds, 5 jobs, 5 of 5 correct); two corrections to #1422

**Run (Alan, 19 Nov, about 21:54; `nix shell nixpkgs#openocd --command python3 score_shell_v1.py -f made_demo_v1.score`, started inside `s-core/` so the `cd s-core` of my instructions printed a harmless "No such file or directory").** Board version 12, 8 slices per block, one block. The shell made `demo_bump`, `demo_flip` and `demo_top16` (each checked on the interpreter), added 5 jobs, planned 2 batches (batch 0: demo_bump x2, demo_flip, vsum, code 110/128, shared 173/256; batch 1: demo_top16, code 52/128, shared 13/256), loaded 500 words in 0.6 s, finished in 0.08 s: **38 script steps, 69,287 clocks = 1.39 ms, 5 of 5 jobs correct**, all MATCH: 3928; 313080 (40 words, arg 3855); [4294967295, 4294967294, 4294967293, 0]; 32767; 1275.
**Reading (measured).** (1) Made kinds run on the card and give the Python answer: this covers `arg` in an xor, a shift left, an add with a small immediate, a 1-word reduction (`then sum`, `then max`) and a per-word map (`not`), with widths all 0 (no `lw` set). (2) Not covered by this run: the large-constant register path (a2..a7), `shl/shr` by `arg`, `min/and/or/xor` endings, and widths above 0; those are tested on the interpreter only. (3) The clocks are not a speed result: widths all 0, cold or warm (#1417) not known, and the two batches differ in size.
**Corrections to #1422 (new entry, #1422 left as written).** (a) #1422 says `test_jobmaker_v1.py` has 29 checks; the count is 30. (b) #1422 says the live colours are "now working" on Alan's machine, from his message "now we have colours and tabbed prompts"; the banner printed at the end of this run reads `Tab completion (install prompt_toolkit for live colours)`, i.e. THIS session had the fallback (no live colouring). So the colours were working in some shell Alan started (the nix command with the Python that has the package), not in this one, or the claim was about the coloured messages only; which, was not asked. Not established.
**Left over on Alan's machine:** `programs/made/demo_*` (made by this run) are untracked files in his checkout; harmless; his to commit or delete (a later `git pull` is not blocked by them unless the same names are committed).

## #1424 (19 Nov) -- Second card test for `make` prepared (`made_demo2_v1.score`, not run) and the write-up before the larger build (`WRITEUP_v1.md`)

**Asked (Alan, 19 Nov, 21:56):** "lets look at the second card test, then a full write up, before i kick off a larger build for testing in the morning".
**Test prepared (not run).** `made_demo2_v1.score`: 6 made kinds (`t2_big` add 0xDEADBEEF xor 0x12345678 sub 3000 and 0xFFFF00FF; `t2_shift` shl arg xor 0x55555555 shr arg; `t2_min` add 7 then min; `t2_and` or 1 then and; `t2_or` shl 3 then or; `t2_xor` not then xor), 12 jobs (the same 8-word `t2_big` job at lw 0, 1, 2, 3; `t2_shift` at arg 4 and arg 37 (low 5 bits = 5); a 17-word and a 1-word `t2_min`; `t2_and`, `t2_or`, two `t2_xor`), `show`, `plan`, then `run` twice (the second is warm, #1417). Checked here, no card: `plan` gives 2 batches (batch 0: 8 jobs, code 123/128, shared 200/256; batch 1: 4 jobs, code 117/128, shared 59/256) and the expected answers; two of them re-derived by hand (t2_xor over 0..16 = 4294967279; t2_shift arg 37 on 1 = 44739243). It covers what #1423 did not on the card: constants above 12 bits (the a2..a7 path), shifts by arg, the min/and/or/xor endings, widths 1 to 3 (answers only: not a speed test), a one-word job.
**Write-up.** `WRITEUP_v1.md`: what is measured / read from the RTL / reasoned, the 19 Nov findings, limits, the second test, a checklist for the larger build, commands, open items. The size of the larger build is not known to me (left blank in the file for Alan). The profile and the estimate in the repo are fitted to the 2x8, 3-unit build and are stated as not valid for another build until re-measured (reasoned from how they are built, not tested on another build).

## #1425 (19 Nov) -- The browser window for the Kintex card: `scwin_v2.py` (the shell as a prompt, live cores, jobs, plan, last run); tested against a FAKE board only, not on the card

**Asked (Alan, 19 Nov, 21:59):** put the prompt system in the HTML front end that shows the cores, and update that front end for the newer features. Answers to my two questions: drive the **Kintex card (JTAG)**; panels **cores, jobs, batches, widths** (not the fusion-unit and cold/warm extras beyond what is listed below).
**Built.** `scwin_v2.py` (new; `scwin.py`, the Tang serial window, is unchanged): a local web server on 127.0.0.1:8766 (`python3 scwin_v2.py`; with the card: `nix shell nixpkgs#openocd --command python3 scwin_v2.py`). **Prompt**: the same `Shell` class as `score_shell_v1.py`, so every command works (add, make, code, plan, run, save, open, set ...); the line is coloured as it is typed (server-side `tokenize`, so no `prompt_toolkit` is needed in the window), Tab completes (`completions`), Up/Down is a history kept in the browser; one command at a time (a second one during a run is refused with a reason). **Cores**: during `run`, a snapshot about four times a second of each block's slices from the card's `T` status and its sequencer `A` status (steps, clocks, busy/done/error, queue counts); a fused group is drawn as one wide box with its class and task number. **Jobs**: the job list with the right answers computed on the host, and MATCH / DIFFERENT after a run (read from the shell's own check lines). **Plan and last run**: batches per block with code and shared words as bars, submit order, the board line, clocks per block, "N of M jobs correct", and a note on run number of the same job set in this window (first run vs repeat, from #1417). **Settings**: the shell's settings. Safety: the server answers only requests with a localhost Host header, and a POST needs the header `X-Score: 1` and no foreign Origin (a page on another site cannot run shell commands through it).
**Changed in existing code (logged here).** `scblocks.run(..., monitor=None)` and `scblocks.main(argv, monitor=None)`: an optional watcher called with `plan`, `poll` and `finish` events inside the existing poll loop; a watcher error is swallowed; with `monitor=None` nothing changes (`test_order_crit_v1`, `test_lengthest_v1`, `test_auto_width`, `test_score_shell_v1` still pass).
**Measured (this computer).** `test_scwin_v2.py` (25 checks, all ok) runs the real server against a FAKE board in the same process (it answers V, O, J, A, T, r; it does NOT run jobs, far RAM reads are zeros, so a run there reports 0 of N correct, expected): page served; foreign Host, missing header, foreign Origin refused; tokens and Tab; add / make / plan / set from the window; plan panel parsed; busy refusal; the live cores panel through the real hook (a fused pair, a single job, an idle slice drawn as 3 boxes, class and task read, queue and sequencer read); clocks parsed; run counting (1, then 2 for a repeat, then 1 again after the jobs change). A headless Chrome screenshot of the page was viewed: layout and colours as intended. Found while testing: `scblocks.main` redirects `sys.stdout` for the whole process during a run, so anything the server or a test prints in that time lands in the shell's output (the server prints nothing during runs; the test prints to `sys.__stdout__`).
**Not known / not done.** (1) NOT run on the real card: the fake's T and A replies are built from the same decoders the real run uses (`host_sc.dec_T`, `scbatch.dec_A`), but whether the real K7 bridge answers T while a batch runs, and how much the extra T exchanges slow the poll loop, is untested; the T command exists in `fpga/host_bridge_v11.v` (read, not run). (2) The cores panel shows class and task number, not the job kind or job number: the mapping from the card's task number to the job list was not worked out. (3) A panel for which fusion unit serves which fused job and the per-core usage (`U`) are not shown. (4) The window does not hold the link between runs (each `run` starts OpenOCD as the shell does). (5) The picture is a 4 Hz snapshot, so a short job can finish between two snapshots. (6) The Tang serial window is not merged into this one. (7) Not tried by a person in a real browser session beyond the headless screenshot.

## #1426 (19 Nov) -- Card: `made_demo2_v1.score` run twice: 12 of 12 jobs correct both times (big constants, shifts by arg, all endings, widths 0 to 3); correction to #1425

**Run (Alan, 19 Nov, about 22:06; `nix shell nixpkgs#openocd --command python3 score_shell_v1.py -f made_demo2_v1.score`, i.e. the shell, not the browser window).** Board version 12, 8 slices per block, one block. 6 made kinds (each checked on the interpreter), 12 jobs in 2 batches (batch 0: 8 jobs, code 123/128, shared 200/256; batch 1: 4 jobs, code 117/128, shared 59/256), 819 words loaded in 0.6 s, 80 script steps. **Run 1: 44,406 clocks = 0.89 ms, 12 of 12 correct, all MATCH. Run 2: 44,396 clocks = 0.89 ms, 12 of 12 correct, all MATCH.**
**Reading (measured).** (1) Covered on the card now: constants too big for a 12-bit immediate (t2_big: four constants incl. 0xDEADBEEF, 0xFFFF00FF), shifts by `arg` (arg 4 and arg 37, whose low 5 bits are 5, both right), the min / and / or / xor endings, a one-word job, 17-word jobs, and the same 8-word job at lw 0, 1, 2 and 3 with identical answers. (2) Still not covered on the card: `add`, `sub`, `and`, `or` with `arg` as operand (only xor-with-arg and shifts-by-arg ran), and more than 17 words per made job. (3) The two runs differ by 10 clocks. This does NOT show a cold/warm gap (#1417 saw about 12,800 clocks between cold and warm on another file), but whether run 1 was the first run after a bitstream load is not known, and with 12 jobs of mixed widths the batch is not the file of #1417, so no cold/warm conclusion is drawn. (4) The clocks are not a width or speed result (mixed widths in one batch; 0.89 ms for the whole set).
**Correction to #1425 (new entry; #1425 left as written).** #1425 says `test_scwin_v2.py` has 25 checks; the count is 28.
**Not yet done:** the same file through the browser window (`scwin_v2.py`) on the card: the window's live cores panel has still not seen the real card.

## #1427 (19 Nov) -- Limits before the larger build: the host now asks the card for its shared RAM size instead of assuming 256; a warning when a job is too long (host side; the card side not run)

**Asked (Alan, 19 Nov, 22:12):** the limitations, "the operands available, and the job length (words)", need addressing before the larger builds.
**Found (read from the code, not measured on a larger build).** (1) Job length: a job's window in the block's shared RAM is 8 header words + its input words + its output words, and ALL the jobs of a batch share that RAM (`dsh`, 256 words on every build so far; 16 jobs max per batch). So one job can have at most 247 input words with a one-word answer, and about 124 with an answer as long as the input. (2) The host did NOT ask the card: `scblocks.run` used `dsh or 256` although the card reports the size (command `Z`, `host_sc.dec_Z`; present in `fpga/host_bridge_v11.v`), so a larger build's extra RAM would have been ignored and long jobs refused by the planner. The code store size (`imem`) was already read from the card. (3) The far RAM size is not reported: `scblocks` assumes 65,536 words for the JTAG card (the block-RAM build) and needs `--far-words` for another one (e.g. DDR3). (4) The build files set `.DSH(256)` in `k7/make_mb_v1.py`; a larger shared RAM is a build change (the window address field allows up to 2**14 words per `SHL=14`; not checked beyond reading the parameter). (5) Operands of `make` (`jobmaker_v1.py`): one input word at a time, steps with a constant or the job's single `arg`; no second array, no neighbour word, no compare / select / clamp, no rotate or arithmetic shift, no multiply (RV32I has none), only one `arg` per job (header word 7 is spare).
**Changed (logged; existing files edited in place).** `scblocks.run`: with no `--dsh` it reads the shared RAM size from the board (`Z`) and says so (`shared RAM: N words per block (read from the board)`); if the read fails it says so and assumes 256. `--dsh` default is now none (asked from the board; `--plan` uses 256 as before, or the given value). `score_shell_v1.py`: settings `dsh` and `far-words` (passed to `plan` and `run`), and `add` warns when a job cannot fit the shared RAM at the current `dsh` (with the numbers and the 247 limit). `SHELL.md` regenerated.
**Measured (this computer, no card).** `test_score_shell_v1.py` (47 checks, all ok; 4 new: the warning, `plan` refusing a 300-word job at 256, `set dsh 1024` accepting it as shared 309/1024, no warning for a job that fits); `test_scwin_v2.py` (29 checks, all ok; 1 new: the fake board answers `Z` with 512 and the run plans with 512). The other tests that touch `scblocks` (`test_order_crit_v1`, `test_lengthest_v1`, `test_auto_width`, `test_profile_plan_v1`, `test_jobmaker_v1`) still pass.
**Not known / not done.** (1) The `Z` read has not been run on the real card (the bridge source answers it; the shared-RAM size the card reports is the one the host will now use). (2) Nothing for long jobs beyond the warning: splitting a long job into pieces and combining the answers is NOT built (design in the conversation). (3) The operand extensions are NOT built. (4) A larger shared RAM is not tested in hardware or simulation.

## #1428 (19 Nov) -- More operands for `make` (`jobmaker_v2.py`) and long data in pieces (`addbig`, `bigjobs_v1.py`); not run on the card

**Asked (Alan, 19 Nov, 22:12 and the answers to my two questions at about 22:20):** long data: split and combine on the host; operands: compare and clamp, neighbours and running totals, two arrays (pairs), multiply by a constant; all four.
**Built: `jobmaker_v2.py` (new; `jobmaker_v1.py` kept).** New steps: `min N`, `max N` (unsigned clamp), `gt lt eq ne` (1 or 0, unsigned), `rol ror sra` (by a number or arg), `mul N` (a number: shifts and adds, the cheaper of N and its negative, at most 12 shifts and adds; or `arg`: a 32-step loop, slow), `diff` (this word minus the previous one at that point of the recipe), `prefix` (running total); and a start `pair OP` (OP: add sub xor and or min max gt lt eq ne mul): the data is two equal halves a and b and the recipe starts from `a[i] OP b[i]` (an odd last word is ignored). Extra state lives in a7 downward (previous word, running total, the second array's pointer, the multiply loop's bit test), constants in a2 upward; the shell refuses a recipe that needs more registers than there are. The shell's `make` uses v2; kinds made earlier with v1 are found by the same recipe (the check now compares the recipe words, not the header comment of the file). **Built: `bigjobs_v1.py` and the shell command `addbig KIND DATA [arg=] [chunk=] [lw=] [crit] [combine=]`:** the data is cut into pieces (default piece size (dsh - 32) / 4 = 56 words at 256), each piece is an ordinary job with a `big` entry (group, part, rule) kept in the job file (other tools ignore it); the pieces' answers combine by `concat` (per-word kinds: vscale, vgain, vxor, made maps), `sum` / `min` / `max` / `xor` / `or` / `and` (totals: vsum, vstress, vcount, vmax, vmin, made kinds with `then`), or `prefix` (vprefix: each piece plus the sum of all earlier pieces' inputs); pair kinds are cut in both halves at the same places. Refused with a reason: crc32, recipes with diff or prefix, unknown kinds without `combine=`, a piece still too big for the shared RAM. After `run` the shell combines the pieces' results from the result file and prints `long job N ... MATCH / DIFFERENT` against the answer for the whole data (the kind's own Python answer on all the data); `list` shows one line per long job; `remove` of a piece removes the whole group; `dup` of a piece is refused. `SHELL.md`, `README.md`, `PROGRAMS.md` updated.
**Measured (this computer, no card).** `test_jobmaker_v2.py` (29 checks, all ok): 600 recipes that v1 accepts give byte-identical code in v2; 19 answers worked out by hand (several of my own first values were wrong - rol 8 of 0x12345678 is 878082066, not 878082192 - and `mul 0xFFFFFFFF` was refused until the negative-constant shortcut was added); refusals with reasons; 117 of 120 random recipes with the new steps (the rest refused for too many constants) agree with Python on the reference interpreter (sizes 0 to 17, random data and arg). `test_bigjobs_v1.py` (38 checks, all ok): for 18 kinds (all the library and built-in kinds, 9 made kinds including pair kinds and every ending) the pieces run as small jobs on the interpreter and combined equal the WHOLE data run as one job in a big shared RAM on the interpreter; refusals; list / remove / dup; `check_big` with right, wrong and missing pieces; save / open keep the grouping; `plan` works with pieces. The earlier tests (shell, window, jobmaker v1, order / crit, estimate, auto width, profile plan) still pass; the window test now expects `ad` to offer add and addbig.
**Not known / not done.** (1) Nothing here has run on the card: `make` steps, pair loops and `addbig` pieces are checked on the interpreter and against Python only; the card result of the new steps (rotates, multiply loops, compares, the extra registers a2..a7) is untested. (2) Code size: each new step costs code words (`mul arg` and `pair mul` about 11, a rotate by arg 6, a constant `mul` two per set bit); the 128-word code store limit still applies per batch. (3) `mul arg` / `pair mul` are 32-step loops and slow on a 1-bit core (not measured). (4) The default piece size (56) is my choice, not a measured optimum; many small pieces mean more batches (a 1000-word `vsum` is 18 jobs). (5) A hand-registered kind needs `combine=` and `addbig` cannot know whether its recipe is pair-shaped. (6) A piece does not carry the state of the previous piece, so `diff` / `prefix` recipes cannot be split (only vprefix, which is handled by the carry). (7) The job-file `big` entry is an extra key in the JSON; scbatch / scblocks ignore it (checked by loading), but a hand-edited file that cuts the group apart would give wrong combined answers (the combine check would then report DIFFERENT). (8) The window's jobs panel lists each piece as a job (the combined verdict is in the log line). (9) Still open from #1427: a larger shared RAM in hardware (build change), and the far RAM size is not reported by the card.

## #1429 (19 Nov) -- Third card test prepared (`made_demo3_v1.score`, not run): the new `make` steps, pairs and long data in pieces

**Prepared (not run).** `made_demo3_v1.score`: 11 made kinds (clamp `max 10 min 100`; `gt 9`; rotate + xor arg + rotate; `sra 4`; `mul 10`; `mul arg`; `pair mul then sum` (a dot product); `pair max`; `diff`; `prefix`; `gt arg then sum`), 12 ordinary jobs (incl. the all-ones word, 0x80000000, a negative `diff` result 4294967287, and a 20-word dot product = 935), then three long jobs through `addbig` (`vsum` 1000 words = 18 pieces, `vprefix` 150 words, a made `gt arg then sum` over 400 words), `show`, `plan`, and `run` twice. Checked here without the card: it plans to 8 batches in 1 block (code 79 to 123 of 128 words, shared 224 to 256 of 256), all expected answers are computed (several re-derived by hand in #1428). The first run asks the board for its shared RAM size (`Z`) for the first time on the card (#1427).
**What a pass looks like:** every job MATCH, "N of N jobs correct", and three lines `long job 0 ... MATCH`, `long job 1 ... MATCH`, `long job 2 ... MATCH`, on both runs. If a long-job line says DIFFERENT while every piece says MATCH, the combining is wrong (host side); if pieces say DIFFERENT, the card result of that step is wrong.
**Not run.** No card result yet. The 8 batches mean the clocks are not comparable with #1423 / #1426.

## #1430 (19 Nov) -- Card: `made_demo3_v1.score` run twice: 41 of 41 jobs correct both times, 3 of 3 long jobs MATCH; the card reports 256 shared words (first `Z` read on the card); correction to #1429

**Run (Alan, 19 Nov, about 22:56; typed and run in the shell: the pasted session shows `score>` lines, 11 `make`, 12 `add`, 3 `addbig`, `show`, `plan`, `run`, `run`; not through the browser window as far as the paste shows).** Board version 12, 8 slices per block, one block. **`shared RAM: 256 words per block (read from the board)`**: the host's new `Z` read (#1427) worked on the real card and returned 256 (the same as the old assumption, so this does not yet show a larger value being used). The plan: 41 jobs in **10 batches** (code 53 to 123 of 128 words, shared 65 to 256 of 256), 4,281 words loaded in 1.1 s (run 1) and 1.2 s (run 2), 286 script steps. **Both runs: 628,287 clocks = 12.57 ms, 41 of 41 jobs correct, all MATCH; `long job 0` (vsum, 1000 words, 18 pieces) combined [500500] MATCH, `long job 1` (vprefix, 150 words, 3 pieces) MATCH, `long job 2` (made `gt arg then sum`, 400 words, 8 pieces) combined [150] MATCH.**
**Reading (measured).** (1) On the card now: clamp (`max 10 min 100`), `gt 9`, rotate left and right with an xor of arg between, `sra 4` (0x80000000 gives 0xF8000000, the sign kept), `mul 10` (including 0xFFFFFFFF wrapping to 4294967286 and 100000), `mul arg` (the loop), `pair mul then sum` (a dot product: 32 and 935), `pair max`, `diff` (including the wrap 3 - 12 = 4294967287), `prefix`, `gt arg then sum`; and long data in pieces for a per-word kind-free total (vsum), a carried running sum (vprefix) and a made total (a made kind with an arg). The host-side combining of pieces gave the right whole answers on the card's results. (2) Not covered on the card: `lt`, `eq`, `ne`, `min` or `max` as the only step, `rol` / `ror` by arg, the cheap-negative path of `mul` (`mul 0xFFFFFFFF` as a recipe step was not run, only as data), the other `pair` operators, `pair` with an ending other than sum, more than one extra-state register in one recipe, `then min / max / and / or / xor` after the new steps. (3) The clocks are identical in the two runs (628,287 both): no cold / warm gap showed here, but whether run 1 was the first run after a bitstream load is not known, so no conclusion about cold and warm; and with 10 batches of mixed kinds the number is not a speed measurement of any one step. (4) The cost of the batches is visible: 41 jobs took 10 batches because only a few jobs share each 256-word shared RAM; the 18 `vsum` pieces of a 1000-word job are spread over batches 1 to 7.
**Correction to #1429 (new entry; #1429 left as written).** #1429 says the demo plans to 8 batches; the real plan has 10 (I cut the printed plan off after 8 batches when I checked it, and the code range is 53 to 123 words, not 79 to 123).
**Not done.** The same file through `scwin_v2.py` on the card (the live cores panel has still not seen the real card).

## #1431 (19 Nov) -- Card: the browser window (`scwin_v2.py`) ran `made_demo3_v1.score` on the real card; the cores panel read the board and agrees with the shell run; observations from one screenshot

**Source.** One screenshot from Alan (19 Nov, about 22:59), taken after the run had finished. I did not see the window while the batches were running, and the screenshot's prompt pane does not show the verdict lines (41 of 41 / MATCH), so **the correct count of this window run is not established by this entry**; the shell run in #1430 is the one that measured it.
**Seen in the screenshot (measured, by eye).** The window connected (`Kintex card via JTAG`). The cores panel read `finished`, board version 12, 8 slices per block (as in #1430), `block 0 41 jobs`, `sequencer: done, 286 steps, 628287 clocks`, `waiting: 0 critical, 0 bulk; running 0`. **So the board status `T` and the sequencer `A` were answered by the real card through the window, and the numbers (41 jobs, 286 steps, 628,287 clocks) are the same as both shell runs in #1430 (third time that clock count).** The plan panel showed batch 0 (3 jobs: d3_clamp, vprefix) and batch 1 (4 jobs: d3_dot, vprefix, vsum) with code and shared bars (batch 1: code 120/128, shared 243/256), the jobs panel listed the long-job pieces (jobs 36 to 40, `d3_cnt`, arg 250, 56 words each and 8 at the end), the settings panel showed the defaults (`dsh=none`, `far-words=none`, port `jtag`).
**Not seen / not known.** (1) Whether the eight core boxes lit up during the run: in the screenshot all eight are empty because the run was over. (2) Whether the real bridge slowed the run while the window polled `T` and `A` (the clock count is unchanged, but wall time was not compared). (3) What `186.3 s since the plan` is: the time since the plan was made, not shown to be the run time; I have not checked whether it stops at the finish.
**Possible faults (not confirmed, not changed).** (a) The prompt pane ends at `registered d3_sra ...` while the panels say finished. The page code scrolls the pane to the bottom only when it is already within 60 pixels of the bottom (`scwin_v2.py` line 265), so this is what happens if the pane was scrolled up; I cannot tell from the screenshot whether it was, or whether a line is lost. (b) In that last visible line `d3_sra` shows as `d3 sra`: the underscore may be cut off at the edge of the pane; unconfirmed. (c) At this window width the jobs list and the plan panel are clipped at the top and bottom; they scroll, so nothing is lost, but it is cramped.
**Not done.** No code was changed. A run watched live (screen recording or a screenshot taken mid-run) is still the missing evidence for the live cores panel.

## #1434 (19 Nov) -- Window: full-screen screenshot after the run: 41 of 41 and 3 of 3 long jobs MATCH through the window; the core boxes showed nothing during or after the run (Alan's report); likely cause: the run is shorter than the polling

**Seen (full-screen screenshot from Alan, 19 Nov, about 23:05, after the run).** The prompt pane ends with `41 of 41 jobs match` and `long job 0 / 1 / 2 ... MATCH` (vsum 1000 words in 18 pieces -> 500500; vprefix 150 words in 3 pieces; d3_cnt 400 words in 8 pieces -> 150). **So the window ran the whole of `made_demo3_v1.score` on the real card correctly (measured); this settles the point #1431 left open.** The cores panel showed `finished`, `sequencer: done, 286 steps, 628287 clocks` (same as #1430 and #1431) and the eight core boxes empty. The jobs panel showed the verdict `MATCH` per piece.
**`since the plan` keeps counting after the finish (measured from two screenshots of the same finished run: 186.3 s in #1431, 414.4 s here).** The label is true (time since the plan was made) but is not the run time; it does not stop at the finish.
**Alan's report (not tested by me).** The core boxes do not appear until the run has finished, and even then show no activity; he suspects the polling is too slow for them to react.
**Reading (reasoned from the code and the logged numbers, not measured).** The whole run on the card is 628,287 clocks = 12.57 ms in total for 10 batches, about 1.3 ms per batch on average. The window gets a core picture only from a `poll` event (`scblocks.run`: send `O` and `A` to the block, then call the monitor; the window then also reads `T`, at most 4 times a second, `scwin_v2.py` line 126 on). One round trip over JTAG is likely at or above the length of a whole run, so the first poll that reaches the window is probably already `done`; the panel then shows an idle block. That would explain both what Alan sees (nothing during, empty boxes at the end). **I have not measured the JTAG round-trip time or the number of polls in this run, so this is the likely cause, not a proven one.** Not a bug in the drawing: the fake-board test (#1425) shows the boxes drawn from `A`.
**Consequence.** A live picture over JTAG can only be seen for runs lasting a good fraction of a second or longer; the current demos are too short. The larger build may be (more jobs, more batches), but its length is unknown. No code was changed.
**Open options (not started, Alan to choose):** (1) count the polls and time one round trip in the window run (a measurement, no behaviour change); (2) a longer test run (many repeats of a plan) to see the panel work at all; (3) after the run, draw a timeline per batch from the plan and the final clock count (labelled as reasoned, not seen live); (4) change the `since the plan` label to stop at the finish.

## #1435 (19 Nov) -- Window: a measured timing line after every run (how many polls, when the first reached the card, the run time, one status read)

**Why.** #1434 reasoned that the whole run (12.57 ms on the card) is shorter than the polling, so the live core boxes never see a busy block. Alan asked for the measurement first (option 1). **Reading of the code that supports the reasoning (not a card measurement):** `scblocks.run` sends the start command and then sleeps 20 ms (`time.sleep(0.02)`) before the first poll; 20 ms is longer than the 12.57 ms the card reports for the whole of `made_demo3_v1.score`, so the first poll would find the run already done even with an instant round trip.
**Change (window only; the shell prints nothing new).** `scblocks.run` now also calls the monitor with a `start` event right after the start command, and passes the board in the `finish` event (edited in place: two lines, no other behaviour change). `scwin_v2.py` records the time of every poll (before its 4 per second throttle) and, at the finish, adds one dim line to the prompt pane: `window timing: N polls; first poll answered X ms after the start command and found it done|busy|...; polls that found it busy: K; the card says the run took Y ms; one status read takes Z ms`. The status read is one extra `T` read timed at the finish (a round trip to the card). The line sits before the `block 0: ... clocks` lines. `test_scwin_v2.py` has one more check for the line (30 checks; it uses the fake board, so only the shape of the line is tested, not its numbers).
**Tests.** `test_scwin_v2.py` and `test_score_shell_v1.py` pass. `tools/quick_check_v2.py --changed` (1,156 tests): **1,153 passed, 1 skipped, 2 failed**, both in `tests/vm/test_man_tang_nano_20k_v2.py` (`test_the_committed_man_and_constraints_are_exactly_what_the_generator_produces`, `test_native_ff_variants_regenerates_identically`). The first failure message (rerun here) is a path string only: the committed `docs/man/tang-nano-20k.man.json` says `yowasp_yosys/share/gowin/cells_sim.v` while this machine's generator finds `/usr/share/yosys/gowin/cells_sim.v`. That is a tool-install difference in this sandbox, not anything these edits touch; I did not rerun the second failure alone or check it on a clean checkout, so "not caused by this change" is my reading, not proven. Nothing in that test was edited.
**For Alan.** Close the old window, start it again (`nix shell nixpkgs#openocd --command python3 scwin_v2.py`), load `made_demo3_v1.score` and run it. Paste the `window timing:` line (or a screenshot). It will say whether any poll found the run busy. If the first poll says `done` and 0 busy polls, #1434's reading is confirmed (the run ends before the first poll), and the live boxes can only be seen on a longer run.
**Not done.** The line has not been run on the real card; its numbers are unseen until Alan runs it. The 20 ms sleep and the 0.05 s gap between polls were not changed.

It is 2 clocks lower (0.0003 %). The job set, the 286 steps and the correct results are the same. ## #1437 (19 Nov) -- Card: the `window timing:` line (from #1435) on a real run: the first poll came 24 ms after the start and found the run already done; one status read takes 4.8 ms; confirms #1434's reading

**Measured (Alan pasted the line, 19 Nov, 23:34, same run as #1436):** `window timing: 1 polls; first poll answered 24 ms after the start command and found it done; polls that found it busy: 0; the card says the run took 12.57 ms; one status read takes 4.8 ms`.
**What it shows.** One poll was made in the whole run and it found the run finished: **no poll saw the block busy, so the core boxes never had anything to draw while it ran; the empty boxes are not a drawing fault.** The run on the card is 12.57 ms, the first poll answered at 24 ms, and one status (`T`) read takes 4.8 ms. #1434's reading (the run is shorter than the polling) is confirmed for this run.
**Reasoned from these numbers (not measured separately).** The 24 ms is the 20 ms sleep in `scblocks.run` plus about one 4 to 5 ms round trip. If the sleep were removed, the first poll would land at about 5 ms, and the next one after the 50 ms pause between polls plus the `T` read, i.e. long after the 12.57 ms run: so at most one or two pictures of a 12.6 ms run, and only if a poll got lucky with the timing. Removing the sleep alone would not give a usable live view; a run would need to last many times longer than a few round trips (on the order of 100 ms or more) to show several pictures.
**Note.** The same run reports 628,285 clocks (#1436) and the card says 12.57 ms, the same as 628,287 would give at the 2-decimal rounding.
**Not changed.** No code change. Open options as in #1434: (2) a longer test run so the panel has something to show; (3) a per-batch timeline drawn after the run from the plan and the final clock count (labelled as reasoned); (4) stop the `since the plan` counter at the finish.

## #1438 (19 Nov) -- Window: the elapsed counter stops at the finish (option 4 of #1434)

Alan chose option 4 (19 Nov, 23:36): the cores panel should show that a run is going and the card's state; on the short test runs the live view is of little use, on longer runs it is wanted. **Change (`scwin_v2.py`):** the live record gets an end time when the run finishes (or when the command ends), and the panel's elapsed time is end minus plan time instead of now minus plan time. While running it reads `running · N s since the plan`; after the finish it reads `finished · N s from the plan to the finish` and no longer grows (it was 186.3 s then 414.4 s on the same finished run, #1434). The figure includes the load of the images (about 1 s), so it is not the card's run time; the card's own run time stays on the `block 0: ... clocks` line and the `window timing:` line. **Test:** `test_scwin_v2.py` has one more check (two reads 0.35 s apart after a run give the same elapsed time); it and `test_score_shell_v1.py` pass. Not run on the card; the page text change is seen only in the browser. The wider changed-files run was not repeated for this small edit (the earlier 2 Tang Nano failures in #1435 are unrelated to it by my reading).

## #1439 (20 Nov) -- A long test run for the window: `bigrun_v1.py`, `window_run_medium_v1.score` (100 jobs) and `window_run_big_v1.score` (500 jobs); the sizes are the host model's estimate, not measured

**What Alan asked (20 Nov, 08:22):** "prep the larger run for the frontend, then that sort of finalises that side". I read it as a bigger test run for the window that lasts long enough for the live cores panel to show something (the point of #1434 to #1438), before the larger card build. If he meant something else (e.g. the build size, or a different set of jobs) he will say.
**What was made.** `bigrun_v1.py` (host only) writes a score file and sizes it; `test_bigrun_v1.py` (7 checks) passes; the two files are generator output (the test compares them with the generator).
- Every job is the made kind `wb_heavy7` (`make wb_heavy7` with seven `mul arg` steps then a sum; the code store is 128 words and the code of eight steps needs 132, so seven is the most that fits). The reference interpreter counts about 100 instructions per word for one `mul arg` step, so about 700 per word for this kind (read from the interpreter, 1040 and 4040 instructions for 10 and 40 words of a one-step kind); a plain sum is about 7.
- 40 words per job, so five jobs (49 words each) fill a batch (245 of 256 shared words). Each job has its own data and its own arg (odd, 3 to 101); the widths repeat 0, 0, 1, 2, 0 so the panel shows single slices and fused groups; answers come from the made kind's Python reference (checked against the interpreter for 10 jobs in the test; the `make` check itself did 14 runs).
- **medium: 100 jobs, 20 batches, far RAM 10,360 words. big: 500 jobs, 100 batches, far RAM 51,800 words** (it ends at 0x2000 + 51,800, inside the 65,536 words the host assumes for the JTAG build; the card does not report its far RAM size, #1427).
**Sizes (ESTIMATE, not measured).** From the host's length model (lengthest_v1, profile `example_2x8_nu3_v2`): medium about 0.8 s of card time, big about 4.1 s; loading at the 19 Nov rate (4,281 words in 1.1 s) about 3 s and about 13 s. The model has no cost for starting a batch and has never been fitted to `mul arg` (it uses the crc32 value of clocks per instruction for every kind). A check against the one measured run of this kind of set: for `made_demo3_v1.score` the same model gives 586,176 clocks (11.72 ms) against 628,285 to 628,287 measured on the card, 6.7 % low; that set has no heavy loops, so it says little about this one. The run will measure the clocks per instruction of `mul arg` (listed as unmeasured in #1430).
**The window with 500 jobs (measured here, not on the card).** Loading the big file into the window took 3.6 s; the page state is about 190 KB the first time and about 110 KB per poll (the jobs list), computed in 8 ms. Not seen in a browser.
**Housekeeping.** The sandbox's untracked made kinds from my runs (`d3_*`, `wb_acc`, the first `wb_heavy` with four steps) are not committed; `wb_heavy` (4 steps) was made once in this sandbox and then deleted, and the committed kind is `wb_heavy7`. Made kinds are never changed, so the name carries the step count.
**For Alan (card).** Close any old window, then: `nix shell nixpkgs#openocd --command python3 scwin_v2.py -f window_run_medium_v1.score` and run it first (about 3 s loading, then the run); then the big one. The `run` line is not in the files: type `run` at the prompt (the files end with `plan`). Take a screenshot while the run goes and paste the `window timing:` line. Not run on the card.
**Not done.** Nothing run on the card; the sizes are estimates; no change to the window itself in this entry.

## #1440 (20 Nov) -- Card: `window_run_medium_v1.score` run in the window: 100 of 100 jobs correct, 42,813,283 clocks (0.856 s), the live core boxes showed real activity for the first time; the host's size estimate was 4 % low; after the finish the boxes now show as dimmed (display change)

**Run (Alan, 20 Nov, about 08:49; the card was power-cycled overnight, so the 2x8 three-unit build `sc_k7_fu2s8u8n3.bit` was loaded first from `/home/alan/openFPGALoader/build/demo-projects/sc_k7_sizes/`; the first load attempts failed with `Open file FAIL` because the loader was started in a folder without the file; the file was found with `find` and loaded by its full path; the run was typed in the window after `-f window_run_medium_v1.score`).** One screenshot after the run. Not stated: whether this was the first run after the load (cold, #1417); the `window timing:` line and the `block 0:` line are above the part of the log in the screenshot.
**Seen (measured).** `100 of 100 jobs match` at the end of the log (the last 16 jobs visible, all MATCH); the jobs panel shows MATCH on the visible rows. The cores panel: `finished`, `2.4 s from the plan to the finish`, board version 12, 8 slices per block, `sequencer: done, 680 steps, 42813283 clocks`, `waiting: 0 critical, 0 bulk; running 4`.
- **Clocks: 42,813,283 = 0.856 s at 50 MHz. The host's estimate in #1439 was 41,171,146 (0.82 s): the card was 4.0 % higher.** That is the first measurement of the made kind `wb_heavy7` (seven `mul arg` steps) and says the model, which uses the crc32 value of clocks per instruction for every kind, was close for this set (one run, possibly cold; one set, not a general result).
- **Loading was faster than estimated (reasoned from the window's own timer).** Plan to finish is 2.4 s in all and the run itself is 0.856 s, so loading the 10,360 words (and starting) took at most about 1.5 s, at least about 6,900 words a second; #1439 assumed 3,900 a second (the 19 Nov figure includes fixed waits of about half a second). So the estimate of 13 s for the big file is probably too high (about 7 to 8 s, reasoned).
- **Live core boxes on the real card, for the first time (measured; seen only after the finish).** The cores panel shows 8 slice boxes: slice 0 `class 4 task 4`, slices 1 to 3 idle, slices 4 and 5 one fused box `fused x2 class 2 task 2`, slice 6 `class 0 task 0`, slice 7 `class 1 task 1`, and `running 4`. That is a picture taken by a poll in the last batch (a run is over when the panel says `finished`, yet it showed four running): the panel kept the last picture it had. So polls did land during this 0.86 s run and read a busy block, and a fused group was drawn as one wide box. How many pictures were taken is not known (the timing line was not in the screenshot).
**Display change (`scwin_v2.py`, page text only).** Because the boxes after the finish are the last picture, not the state at the finish, the page now dims them after the finish and the header says `the boxes are the last picture taken during the run`. `test_scwin_v2.py` has one more check (page text only; not seen in a browser); the window and shell tests pass. Not repeated: the wider changed-files run.
**Not done.** The big file (500 jobs, estimated about 4.3 s of card time with this 4 % applied, reasoned) has not been run; cold or warm for this run is unknown; the number of polls is unknown until the `window timing:` line is pasted.

## #1441 (20 Nov) -- Card: the `window timing:` line of the medium run: 17 polls, 16 found the block busy; the run was the first after the load (cold) (follow-up to #1440)

**Alan (20 Nov, 08:52):** it was the first run after loading the card, and the line: `window timing: 17 polls; first poll answered 22 ms after the start command and found it busy; polls that found it busy: 16; the card says the run took 856.27 ms; one status read takes 4.0 ms`.
**What it shows (measured).** The first poll came 22 ms after the start and found the block busy; 16 of the 17 polls saw it busy (the last one saw it done). One status read takes 4.0 ms (4.8 ms in #1437). So in a run of 856 ms the host polled about every 50 ms, the pause in `scblocks.run`. **#1434's reading is confirmed from the other side: with a run lasting a good fraction of a second, polls land inside it.**
**How many core pictures (reasoned, not counted by the code).** The window reads the status `T` (the core boxes) at most every 0.25 s, so about 4 pictures in 0.86 s (at roughly 0.02, 0.27, 0.52 and 0.77 s), about 4 per second. A faster picture rate would need the 0.25 s throttle lowered (each read costs 4 ms of the polling loop); on the big file, about 4 pictures a second is what to expect. The poll count and the line were not logged anywhere but the log pane.
**Cold.** This run (42,813,283 clocks, #1440) was the first after a fresh load, so by #1417 it may carry the cold extra; whether it did is not known until a second run of the same file is compared (the earlier cold gap was a few clocks, 6 of 48,256, on a much shorter run). **Asked of Alan:** type `run` a second time in the same window and paste the clocks line and the new `window timing:` line.
**No code change.**

## #1442 (20 Nov) -- Window: `-f FILE` served the page only after the whole file had run, and waited 0.2 s between commands; the page now opens at once (found when the 500-job file left a blank page)

**What happened (Alan, 20 Nov, about 08:55 to 09:00).** Starting the window with `window_run_big_v1.score` failed first with `Address already in use`: the medium run's window (pid 60078) and its OpenOCD (pid 60320) were still alive; `pkill -f scwin_v2.py` and a plain `kill` did not stop them (reason not found: `kill -9` was given; I do not know yet why a normal stop signal was ignored, not investigated). Then the new window took "an age" to open and the browser waited on localhost.
**Cause (read in the code, `scwin_v2.py` `main`).** The listening socket was created, but `serve_forever()` was only called after the commands of the `-f` file had all been run, and between two commands the loop slept 0.2 s while the previous one was busy. The browser therefore connected but got no answer until the file was done: 500 `add` lines at 0.2 s or more each is a minute or two. (Loading the same file in the sandbox, without that sleep, took 3.6 s, #1439.)
**Change.** The server now runs in its own thread from the start, so the page opens at once and fills as the commands run; the wait between commands is 0.01 s instead of 0.2 s; Ctrl-C stops the window and shuts the server down (`srv.shutdown()`). **Test:** `test_scwin_v2.py` has one more check: `main -f` with a file of 300 commands answers a request while the file is still running (the first answer came with 1 to 141 of 300 lines done over three runs here) and all 300 then run. The window and shell tests pass. Not seen on Alan's machine.
**Not investigated.** Why the old window and OpenOCD ignored the normal stop signal; the stale-process case is not handled by the window (no friendly message for `Address already in use`, offered earlier, not done).

## #1443 (20 Nov) -- Card: `window_run_big_v1.score` (500 jobs) in the window, twice: 500 of 500 correct, 214,066,400 clocks (4.281 s), the same both runs; the host's size estimate was 4.0 % low again; 80 polls per run

**Run (Alan, 20 Nov, about 09:05; the window restarted after the fix of #1442; the card as loaded for the medium run, which ran before it; typed `run` twice).** Alan: "runs through fine", and one screenshot after a run plus the two `window timing:` lines.

- **Correct: `500 of 500 jobs match`** (screenshot; the last 16 jobs shown, all MATCH). Both runs are reported as through fine; the screenshot shows only one of them.

- **The host's estimate (#1439) was 205,855,732 clocks; the card was 4.0 % higher (ratio 1.03988, the same ratio as the medium run, 1.03988).** So for this kind the model is low by a constant 4.0 % (two sizes, one kind); I have not changed the model or the generator's printed estimate (a factor of 1.04 for `wb_heavy7` could be added as a per-kind CPI; not done).
- **Polling:** 81 polls (80 found the block busy) in the first run, 80 (79 busy) in the second; the first poll 24 ms and 23 ms after the start; one status read 1.8 ms and 2.3 ms (it was 4.8 and 4.0 ms before; it varies). About one poll per 53 ms; with the picture limit of one per 0.25 s that is about 17 core pictures a run (reasoned, not counted).
- **Time (screenshot): `9.5 s from the plan to the finish`** for the run shown, of which 4.28 s is the card's run: loading the 51,800 words and starting took about 5.2 s, about 10,000 words a second (#1439's estimate was 13 s of loading for this file; the medium run was at least 6,900 words a second).
- **The dimmed boxes (#1440's change) work in the browser:** after the finish the panel reads `finished`, `the boxes are the last picture taken during the run`, and the boxes are shown dimmed (slice 0 `class 4 task 4`, slices 6 and 7 `class 0 task 0` and `class 1 task 1`, `running 3`). The window opened at once with the file this time (#1442's fix works on Alan's machine, by his "runs through fine").
**Cold and warm.** No difference showed between the two big runs (4281.33 ms both), but neither was the first after a load (the medium run had run before); so this says nothing about the cold effect of #1417 on a run this long. The first medium run (after the load) was 42,813,283 clocks; five times the second big run is within 15 clocks of it, so if the first medium run was cold, the cold extra was at most about 15 clocks in 42.8 million (reasoned from the scaling, not a measurement).
**Not seen.** No screenshot taken while the run was going: the core boxes changing live during the 4.3 s have not been seen by me; Alan's report is only that the run went through. A second medium run was not asked for again.
