## #1444 (20 Nov) -- Window on the card: Alan watched the core boxes during the big run: they changed and flashed green with the current job, and the first four slices were used less than the last four; the host model gives the same picture

**Alan's report (20 Nov, 09:48; observation by eye, not recorded):** during the big run the boxes changed, flashed green with the running job as they did so, and the first four slices were used less than the second four. **So the live cores panel works on the real card on a run of seconds (#1434 to #1443 closed on this point).**
**Why the lower four are less busy (modelled, not measured).** I ran one batch of this file (jobs of widths 2, 1, 0, 0, 0 in the planner's submit order) through a copy of the host's dispatcher model (`lengthest_v1.simulate`: the dispatcher rules read from the RTL in #1405, the speed-ups of the profile `example_2x8_nu3_v2`), recording where and when each job runs. Result: the width-2 job takes slices 0 to 3 from 0 to 27 % of the batch; the width-1 job slices 4 and 5 until 42 %; one width-0 job slice 6 until 100 % and one slice 7 until 78 %; the fifth job, which finds no free slice at the start, runs on slice 0 from 27 to 87 %. **Busy share per slice: 87, 27, 27, 27, 42, 42, 100, 78 %: slices 0 to 3 average 42 %, slices 4 to 7 average 66 %** (all eight about 54 %). This agrees with what Alan saw, and with the last picture in the screenshots after the big run (slices 0, 6 and 7 busy, the rest idle, as in the tail of the model's batch). It explains the pattern but is a model of the card, not a reading from it: the per-slice times were not measured.
**What it says.** (1) The width pattern 0, 0, 1, 2, 0 was chosen to show fusion in the panel, not for speed; the wide jobs finish early and leave slices idle. (2) Only five jobs fit a batch because the 40-word jobs use 49 of the 256 shared words each; at width 0 that keeps at most 5 of the 8 slices busy, so the shared RAM size limits how many slices a batch can use. Fusing jobs uses slices that would otherwise sit idle. Both points are reasoned from the packing and the model; for the larger build, the shared RAM size (`.DSH(256)` in `k7/make_mb_v1.py`, #1427) is a setting that decides how many jobs a batch can hold.
**No code change.** The modelling script was a scratch copy and is not committed.

## #1445 (20 Nov) -- Planning the larger build (several blocks of 16 slices): an area estimate from the three builds we have, and what is still open

**Alan (20 Nov, 09:51):** the larger build should hopefully put several blocks of 16 slices on the card; the earlier 2x16 attempt, though not finished, gave a clue how full the card would be. **This entry is arithmetic on figures already in the ledger; nothing was built or run.**
**Inputs (measured, nextpnr LUT counts, ledger).** 2x4: 23,426 LUT (#1349); 2x8 without units: 30,682 (#1366); 1x16 (v15): 25,042 (the sweep, #1339, gave 25,205 for another variant); 2x8 with 1 / 2 / 3 eight-lane fusion units per block: 34,891 / 39,076 / 42,615 LUT (#1384, #1387, #1392; the third build is the one on the card now). The card's size: 298,600 LUT6, 597,200 FF, 955 RAMB36 (the figure used in the ledger for this card). Per slice: 3 RAMB18 (51 RAMB18 for 16 slices, #1339); far RAM 64 RAMB36 once.
**Fit (my arithmetic, three points, three unknowns: it fits them exactly and so cannot check itself).** LUT = G + B x blocks + s x slices with G = 4,890 (global), B = 5,640 per block (dispatcher, copy engine, sequencer, RAM side), s = 907 per slice. So one 16-slice block without fusion units is about **20,150 LUT**, and 2x16 about **45,200 LUT (15 % of the card)** (the 50,000 guessed in #1349 was a little higher). Fusion units, measured on 8-slice blocks with 8-lane units: about 2,100, 2,090 and 1,770 LUT per block for units 1, 2 and 3, about **6,000 per block for three**. Units for a 16-slice block (16 lanes? how many?) have not been measured; I assume nothing about them here.
**Blocks of 16 against LUT count (estimate, no units / three units like today's build):** 2 blocks 45k (15 %) / 57k (19 %); 4: 85k (29 %) / 109k (37 %); 6: 126k (42 %) / 162k (54 %); 8: 166k (56 %) / 214k (72 %); 10: 206k (69 %) / 266k (89 %); 12: 247k (83 %) / 318k (over 100 %). The card as built now (2x8, three units): 42,615 LUT = 14.3 %, 17,494 FF = 2.9 %. (nextpnr prints 7 % for the same 42,615: it counts a different set of sites; I use plain counts against the datasheet LUT6 figure and have not checked why they differ.) Block RAM is not the limit: about 51 RAMB18 per 16-slice block plus 64 RAMB36 once; 8 blocks use about 520 of the 1,910 RAMB18 equivalents (27 %).
**What the area figure does not say (the real limit).** Routing and time. The only 2x16 attempt (#1352 to #1361, on the 7.7 GB machine) did not finish: placement peaked at 7.2 GB plus swap, the router took about 48 minutes an iteration and the overuse fell 33,961 / 16,474 / 11,476 / 9,504 in four iterations, not in time for the 6 hour limit; since then the machine has 24 GB, and the 2x8 builds routed in 22 to 36 minutes. A 2x16 on 24 GB has not been tried; how routing time grows beyond that is unknown. The clock is not the problem so far: 1x16 routed at 86 MHz, 2x8 with three units at 82 MHz, against 50 MHz.
**Two build settings that decide how much of the blocks is used (reasoned from #1444).** (1) Shared data RAM per block (`.DSH(256)`, `k7/make_mb_v1.py`): with 40-word jobs only five fit a batch, so at width 0 about five slices of a block are busy; a 16-slice block with 256 shared words would be at most about a third busy on small jobs (fusing uses the rest). A shared RAM of about 1,024 words would hold 16 jobs of that size (16 x 49 = 784). It is block RAM, a small cost, and the host reads the size from the card (`Z`, #1427), so the host needs no change for it. (2) Far RAM: 64K words of block RAM shared by the blocks; the loading rate seen was about 10,000 words a second, so a long run is mostly loading; a bigger far RAM (the DDR3 variant exists, working with a 4-slice pool) is a separate decision. Neither has been decided.
**Open and asked of Alan.** (a) The pre-route utilisation lines of the failed 2x16 build, if its log is still there (`grep -n -A14 "Device utilisation" build_*mb2s16*.log`): the ledger has no size figure for it, only router progress. (b) How many blocks to aim for, how many fusion units per block and of what width, the shared RAM size, and whether far RAM stays block RAM. (c) The proposed order (as in his own plan of 17 Nov: grow one step at a time and read timing and resources at each step): 2x16 with the same unit scheme first on the 24 GB machine, recording time per router iteration, then more blocks.

## #1446 (20 Nov) -- 2x16 build prepared (32 slices): `Makefile.fu2_s16_u16_n3`, checked in simulation; a size prediction logged BEFORE the build

**Alan (20 Nov, 09:59):** build small first: the 2x16 gives 32 real slices to size and test, before anything larger. **Nothing was built on the card side; this is the build files and the simulation checks.**
**Choices I made (Alan had not answered the questions of #1445; he can change any of them, but each change is a different build):** the same scheme as the 2x8 build on the card, scaled to 16-slice blocks: **2 blocks x 16 slices, three 16-lane fusion units per block** (`ULW = 4`, `NU = 3`, `FZ = 1`), the same top (`sc_top_k7_v10`) and pool (`sc_sys_v17`), shared RAM per block `DSH = 256` and far RAM 64K words of block RAM unchanged (a larger shared RAM is the next step, to be changed only after this build's size and routing are known). Written by `python3 k7/make_mb_v3.py 2 16 4 3`: `k7/Makefile.fu2_s16_u16_n3` and `k7/sc_k7_fu2s16u16n3.v` (project `sc_k7_fu2s16u16n3`).
**Checked here (simulation, iverilog; no synthesis, nothing on the card).**
- `python3 test_sys17.py 16 4 1 3` (pool of 16 slices with three 16-lane units): **RESULT PASS**: every task's result equals the instruction-set simulator's, alone at widths 0 to 4, in a mix, two wide jobs at once, and many mixed tasks. Clocks per task alone, width 0 to 4: crc32 194,525 / 101,501 / 54,989 / 31,733 / 23,259 (8.4 times at width 4, 16 slices); shsum 12,717 to 1,397 (9.1 times); fib 9,303 to 1,051 (8.9 times); sum100 40,407 to 4,859. These are simulated clocks of one block, not card measurements.
- The whole board (`fpga/tb_k7fu2.v`: two blocks, `sc_top_k7_v10`, host bridge, far RAM, sequencer) **elaborates** with NB = 2, S = 16, ULW = 4, NU = 3. As for the 2x8 (#1371 era) no job was run through the whole-board testbench.
**Size prediction, logged now so the build can test it (reasoned, #1445):** the fit gives about 45,200 LUT for 2x16 without units; three units add about 6,000 per block on 8-lane units (measured on 8-slice blocks, #1384, #1387, #1392) and a 16-lane unit is bigger (not measured). My expectation is **57,000 to 75,000 LUT (19 to 25 % of the 298,600), 17,000 to 30,000 FF, about 100 RAMB18 plus 64 RAMB36**; a result well outside that range means the fit is wrong. The clock is expected above 50 MHz (1x16 86 MHz, 2x8 with three units 82 MHz); the routing time is the unknown (the old 2x16 took about 48 minutes an iteration on the 7.7 GB machine).
**Commands for Alan (inside `nix develop github:openxc7/toolchain-nix`; first `echo $KINTEX7_CHIPDB; ls -l $KINTEX7_CHIPDB` so nothing regenerates the chip database; one build at a time):** `git pull` in `Imago-Unicell-score`; copy `k7/Makefile.fu2_s16_u16_n3`, `k7/sc_k7_fu2s16u16n3.v`, `k7/stage_mb_v3.sh`, `k7/seeds_v1.sh` into `demo-projects/sc_k7_sizes`; there `S_CORE=$HOME/Imago-Unicell-score/s-core sh stage_mb_v3.sh`; then `MAKEFILE=Makefile.fu2_s16_u16_n3 SEEDS="1 3 2" MAXHOURS=12 bash seeds_v1.sh`; in a second terminal `tail -f progress_sc_k7_fu2s16u16n3.txt` and `free -h` during placement. Afterwards paste: the end of `build_sc_k7_fu2s16u16n3_seed<N>.log` (Max frequency, utilisation lines), the progress file, and how long placement and each router iteration took.
**Not done.** No synthesis or place and route; the card is not touched; the 16-lane unit's size, the router time and whether it routes at all are unknown; the 2x16 of 17 to 18 Nov never finished.

## #1447 (20 Nov) -- Window with 2 blocks of 16 slices (looked at on a FAKE board, headless Chrome here): the panel handles it; narrow boxes were clipped, labels shortened

**Why.** While the 2x16 build runs (started by Alan 10:46, #1446) the window had only ever been looked at with 4 or 8 slices in one block. **Checked here, not on the card and not on Alan's screen:** a scratch fake board (not committed) that answers `V`, `Z`, `O`, `J`, `A`, `T` for 2 blocks of 16 slices, always busy, with fused groups of width 4, 2 and 1; the window page was rendered with the sandbox's headless Chrome (1900 x 920) and the screenshot looked at.
**Seen.** The cores panel draws one row per block, 16 boxes each, the width-4 group as one wide box, the idle slices numbered; the settings, jobs and plan panels are unchanged. **A fault:** the single-slice and width-2 boxes are about 55 pixels wide, so their text (`class 0 task 0`) was clipped to fragments (`s 0 tas`, `d x2 class 2 tas`).
**Change (`scwin_v2.py`, page text only).** On a block of more than 8 slices, boxes narrower than 4 slices show a short label (`t43`, `x2 t42`); wide groups keep the full label (`fused x4 class 4 task 41`); the tooltip of every running box now carries `class N task M`. Blocks of 8 slices or fewer look as before. Looked at again after the change in the same fake setup: all labels readable. `test_scwin_v2.py` has one more check (page text only). The window and shell tests pass.
**Not known.** What the real card's `T` read gives for 16 slices (the host decodes it from the board version, `S`; it has run on 4 and 8 slices only); the real look at Alan's screen size; whether a status read of 32 slices costs more than the 1.8 to 4.8 ms seen with 8.

## #1448 (20 Nov) -- 2x16 build, seed 1, first two hours: synthesis 14 min, placement 26 min, router started at 41 min and had printed no iteration by 74 min; memory 9.1 GB used with no swap

**Measured (Alan's `seeds_v1.sh` progress output, `Makefile.fu2_s16_u16_n3`, started 10:46:09, 50 MHz target, pasted at 12:01, and one `free -h`).** `synthesis/setup` for 14 minutes (10:47 to 11:00); `placing` from the 15th minute to the 40th (about 26 minutes, 11:01 to 11:26); `router start` from the 41st minute (11:27) and still `router start` at the last line, 74 minutes in (12:00): **33 minutes in the router with no iteration line in the progress file** (the progress line shows the newest router line; it was empty). `free -h` at about 12:01: 23 GiB total, **9.1 GiB used**, 3.2 GiB free, 11 GiB cache, 14 GiB available, **swap 0 B used**.
**Against the 2x8 (#1476 era, same flow): synthesis about 5 min, placement about 11 to 14 min, and the router ran about 28 iterations in 26 minutes (about a minute each).** So the 2x16 took about three times as long to synthesise and about twice as long to place; its router has not yet finished its first iteration after 33 minutes, against about a minute per iteration for the 2x8. The old 2x16 (#1361 to #1418) took about 48 minutes an iteration from the second iteration on; its first iteration time was not recorded.
**Correction to #1445 (a reading, not a result).** #1445 tied the slow old router to the 7.7 GB machine. Here the machine has plenty of memory and no swap is used (and the old run's `vmstat` showed no swap traffic during routing either, #1418), so memory is unlikely to be what slows the router; the cause is not known (design size, congestion from the fusion units, or something else). #1445 is left as written.
**What follows from the numbers (reasoned).** If the router needs about 48 minutes an iteration again, `MAXHOURS=12` (ends about 22:46) allows about 14 iterations and the stuck rule only starts after 15, so the build would probably end by the time limit; in the old build the overuse fell about 17 % per iteration at iteration 5 (9,504). Nothing is concluded yet: the first iteration lines are what to look at.
**Asked of Alan:** `tail -3 build_sc_k7_fu2s16u16n3_seed1.log` (the router line) and `top -b -n1 | head -12` (does nextpnr use one core fully, how much memory); and, for later, `nextpnr-xilinx --help | grep -i router` inside the nix shell (whether another router is offered; I could not establish that for this fork).
**No code change.**

## #1449 (20 Nov) -- 2x16 build: the log says `router2` is already in use, nextpnr runs on one core, no iteration after 38 minutes; a 1x16 variant with three 16-lane units prepared to separate the causes (not built)

**Measured (Alan, 20 Nov, 12:04; `tail -3` of `build_sc_k7_fu2s16u16n3_seed1.log` and `top`).** The log ends `Info: Running router2...`, `Info: Setting up routing resources...`, `Info: Running main router loop...`: **the flow already uses nextpnr's `router2`**, so the question of #1448 (is another router offered) has its answer: this is the newer router, no alternative to switch to (whether it can use several cores in this fork is not known). `top`: `nextpnr` at 100 % of one core, 5.5 GB resident, 75 minutes of CPU, the rest of the machine about 45 % idle (four logical processors in use by other things only lightly), swap unused.
**Reading (reasoned).** The router began at 11:27; at 12:04 (37 minutes later) the log has still not printed an iteration, so one iteration takes at least 37 minutes here, where the 2x8 took about one: the same as the old 2x16 (about 48 minutes an iteration). With 12 hours that is about 14 iterations (see #1448). Not yet a result: the first iteration line is not out.
**What the sizes say (reasoned, from ledger figures).** The 2x8 with three units (42,615 LUT) routed in 36 minutes; the 1x16 (25,042 LUT) in 28 minutes; the old 2x16 without units (about 45,000 LUT by the fit of #1445) never finished. So the slow router does not follow from LUT count or from the units alone; two blocks of 16 slices is what has been slow twice. This build has 3 units per block, so it cannot show whether the units matter.
**Prepared, not built: `k7/Makefile.fu1_s16_u16_n3` and `sc_k7_fu1s16u16n3.v` (`python3 k7/make_mb_v3.py 1 16 4 3`): ONE block of 16 slices with three 16-lane units, the same pool and units as the running build.** Its whole board elaborates in iverilog (NB = 1, S = 16, ULW = 4, NU = 3); the pool itself is the one that passed in #1446. Purpose: if it routes in the usual time (under an hour), the 16-lane units are not the cause and the second block of 16 is; if it is also slow, the units are. **Memory would allow running it alongside the running build (14.7 GB available, 5.5 GB used by nextpnr; a synthesis and placement of this size peaked at 7.2 GB in the old 2x16 attempt); this was not asked of Alan yet and has not been run. Two builds use two cores; the earlier rule of one build at a time was for the 7.7 GB machine.**
**No change to the running build.**

## #1450 (20 Nov) -- Alan's alternative: if 16-slice blocks will not route, use the proven 8-slice blocks: `Makefile.fu4_s8_u8_n3` (4 blocks x 8 slices = 32 slices) prepared, elaborates, not built

**Alan (20 Nov, 12:07):** "the alternate is to switch to the proven 8 slice blocks". Taken as: reach 32 slices with four of the 8-slice block that is already built and run on the card (2x8, three 8-lane units), instead of two 16-slice blocks.
**Prepared, not built.** `python3 k7/make_mb_v3.py 4 8 3 3` wrote `k7/Makefile.fu4_s8_u8_n3` and `k7/sc_k7_fu4s8u8n3.v` (project `sc_k7_fu4s8u8n3`: NB = 4, S = 8, three 8-lane units, DSH 256, far RAM 64K words, same top and pool as the 2x8 on the card). The whole board elaborates in iverilog with NB = 4, S = 8, ULW = 3, NU = 3. **Never simulated or built with more than two blocks:** the multi-block top and the far-RAM arbiter are written for any NB (`far_arb` is a fixed-priority arbiter, lowest block first; the bridge's block select is 4 bits, so up to 16), the host planner takes `--blocks N`, but a card or whole-board job run exists only for 1 and 2 blocks (#1352). With four blocks the fixed priority could starve the highest block when the others hit the far RAM all the time; the argument in `far_arb.v` (a block issues one request about every 15 clocks and a request takes 1 to 3 clocks) is a reading, not a test with four.
**Size estimate (my fit of #1445, from measured builds; arithmetic):** 4x8 with three units about **80,300 LUT (27 % of 298,600)**; without units about 56,500; 3x8 with units about 61,500; 6x8 about 118,000. Three units per 8-slice block cost about 6,000 LUT per block (measured, #1384, #1387, #1392).
**What it would settle.** The 2x16 (running, #1448) and the 1x16 variant (#1449) ask whether 16-slice blocks or their 16-lane units are what slows the router. The 4x8 asks the other thing: does the router cope with many blocks if each block is the proven size? Together: if 4x8 routes in the usual time (the 2x8 took 22 to 36 minutes), the slowness is tied to the 16-slice block; if it is slow too, it is tied to the total size or the shared parts (arbiter, bridge). **Trade-off:** the widest job on 8-slice blocks is 8 lanes (width setting 3), not 16; Alan's 17 Nov direction said a 16 cap is fine for now, so this is a step back from that, not a break of it.
**Machine.** 24 GB with 14.7 GB available while the 2x16 runs (5.5 GB resident, one core); a second build alongside is possible; three builds at once would not be (about 6 to 9 GB each at the peak). Nothing started on Alan's machine from here.

## #1451 (20 Nov) -- Router progress of the 2x16 and the 4x8 builds, running side by side on Alan's PC (interim, both still in the router at 13:37); a correction to what I said at 13:29

*Measured (Alan's pasted progress lines and `top`; nothing run from here).* **2x16** (`fu2_s16_u16_n3`, seed 1, started 10:46): router start about 11:27 (my estimate from a 12:04 check, not a logged line), iteration 1 at 12:16 (overused 138,359; about 49 minutes), iteration 2 **not printed by 13:28** (over 72 minutes). **4x8** (`fu4_s8_u8_n3`, seed 1, started 12:13): synthesis about 14 minutes, placing 12:28 to 13:23 (55 minutes), `router start` 13:23, **iteration 1 at 13:24 (overused 153,597; about 1 minute)**, no iteration 2 by 13:28. Reference, 1x16 (#1387 era): iteration 1 overused 44,085, iteration 2 14,083, routed at iteration 32 in 28 minutes. `top` at 13:35: 2x16 nextpnr 5.5 GB resident, 4x8 nextpnr 5.7 GB resident (matched to the builds by CPU time, 182 and 80 minutes; not confirmed with `ps`); 8.9 GB available, swap unused. Both builds together fit in memory. The 2x16 showed 189 % CPU at that moment; at 12:04 it was one core.
**Correction (mine, 13:29).** I said the 4x8's first iteration took up to 58 minutes and concluded the slowness followed total size. That was wrong: the 55 minutes were placing; the 4x8's first router iteration took about a minute. On present data the slow router goes with the 16-slice block (iteration 1 about 49 minutes against about 1), as Alan had suggested, **but the 4x8's later iterations are not yet seen**, so this is not settled either way. The overused count at iteration 1 does not predict speed (4x8 higher than 2x16).
**Open.** Whether the 4x8 routes and how fast its iterations run; whether the 2x16 ever prints iteration 2 (stops at `MAXHOURS` 12, about 22:46). Not a result yet: no bitstream from either.

## #1453 (20 Nov) -- 4x8 seed 1 stopped by the stuck rule after a near miss; 2x16 at iteration 3 after 8+ hours; correction to the #1451 reading of speed

*Measured (Alan's pasted progress lines; nothing run from here).* **4x8** (`fu4_s8_u8_n3`): seed 1 ran 12:13 to 15:39; `seeds_v1.sh` logged "STUCK (overused 6415 at iter 7 -> 8093 at iter 15, fell -26 % < 15 %): stopping it" and started seed 3 at 15:39. Iteration 1 was overused 153,597 (13:24), so the count fell to 6,415 by iteration 7 and then **rose** to 8,093 by iteration 15. Iteration time: about 135 minutes for iterations 1 to 15, so **about 9 to 10 minutes each on average (my arithmetic from two timestamps)**, against about 1 minute for the 2x8 (#1384 era). Seed 3 at 19:25 (226 minutes): router, iteration 1, overused 160,692; when it reached iteration 1 is not known. **2x16** (seed 1, started 10:46): iteration 1 overused 138,359 (12:16), iteration 2 69,708, iteration 3 49,713 at 19:06; at 19:25 (519 minutes) iteration 3 had run 19 minutes; `MAXHOURS` 12 ends it at 22:46. Reference 1x16: 44,085, 14,083, routed at iteration 32 in 28 minutes.
**Corrections.** #1451 read the 4x8's first iteration (about 1 minute) as a sign it would route quickly; the later iterations are about 10 minutes each, so the 4x8 is slow too, only about six times faster per iteration than the 2x16's (about 60 minutes, from three iterations in 8 hours; the 2x16 per-iteration time is an average, not a log). Neither build is at the 2x8's speed, so total size matters, not just the 16-slice block; the 16-slice block is slower still. **Reading, not proven:** the 4x8 reached a count of about 6,400 and then wandered, and the rule that stopped it (a fall below 15 % over eight iterations) can stop a run that would have gone on to route; the 1x16 needed 32 iterations. Whether seed 1 would have routed with the rule off is **unknown**.

## #1454 (20 Nov) -- 4x8 seed 1 rerun with the stuck rule off: same placement and iteration 1 as the first run; three builds were running (old seed 3 not stopped), memory and CPU full, swap used; seed 3 stopped at 21:07

*Measured (Alan's pasted `top`, `ps`, progress file).* **Rerun** (`MINITER=100000`, `MAXHOURS=8`, started 19:35, limit 03:35): placing 19:37 to 20:47 (about 70 minutes; 55 in the first run), `router start` 20:47, **iteration 1 overused 153,597 at 21:00, identical to the first seed 1 run** (one repeat: the same seed gave the same placement and first pass), 13 minutes from router start to iteration 1 (about 1 minute in the first run). **Cause of the slowdown found:** at 21:04 `top` showed three `nextpnr-himbaechel` processes (2x16 pid 71996 at 10 h 07; the old 4x8 seed 3, pid 84505, 5 h 27, which my 19:30 instruction to stop had not removed; the new seed 1, pid 94032, 1 h 31): CPU 97.9 % busy with no idle, load average 6.9, memory 18.9 GB used, 5.0 GB available, **swap 2 GB in use** (it was 0 B before). `kill 84505` at 21:07: processes left are the other two; 10.7 GB available, 41.9 % CPU idle (swap used unchanged at 2.0 GB, which Linux does not release by itself). The `seed 1` log showed no growth between two listings two minutes apart (20,033 bytes at 21:00:41), consistent with a slow iteration or a buffered file; not separated.
**Corrections.** (1) The first 4x8 run of seed 1 (#1453, about 10 minutes per iteration) ran with two builds, not three, so its figure is not affected by this; the rerun's slow start is. (2) The program is **`nextpnr-himbaechel`**, not `nextpnr-xilinx` as I had been calling it; my earlier remarks about its router (router2, one core, iteration behaviour) are not verified for this tool; the 2x16 was seen at 189 % CPU, so at times it uses more than one core. (3) Three builds do not fit on this PC (stated in #1450; the stop instruction slipped).
**Open.** Whether seed 1 routes with the rule off; the 2x16's iteration 3 onwards (limit 22:46).

## #1455 (21 Nov) -- Results: 4x8 (rule off) used its 8 h budget without routing, 2x16 still running after 17 h at iteration 5; two smaller experiments prepared (3x8 with units, 4x8 without units)

*Measured (Alan's pasted progress files, `pgrep`, log lines, 21 Nov 04:07).* **4x8** (`fu4_s8_u8_n3`, seed 1 rerun, `MINITER=100000`): "time budget 8 h used up: stopping it" at 03:35; last line iteration 23, **overused 15,594** (best seen 6,415 at iteration 7 in the first run; 8,093 at iteration 15; the count rose during the run). About 395 minutes for iterations 1 to 23 after the router started, so about 18 minutes per iteration on average (my arithmetic). **Not routed.** With the rule off the run did not converge in 23 iterations; this is one run, not proof that the 4x8 cannot route. **2x16** (`fu2_s16_u16_n3`, seed 1): the `seeds_v1.sh` progress file ends at 19:34 on 20 Nov (528 minutes, no finish line) while pid 71996 (`nextpnr-himbaechel`) is still alive at 04:07 (about 17 hours); the log was last written 01:25. Iterations in the log: 2: 69,708; 3: 49,713; **4: 43,735; 5: 44,089** (iteration 1 was 138,359). About 3 hours per iteration (arithmetic from five iterations over about 17 hours, router start estimated 11:27); the count has stopped falling. **My reading, not confirmed:** the script on that build stopped at 19:34, one minute before the 4x8 rerun started at 19:35; likely cause is the Ctrl-C I told Alan to press in "the 4x8's terminal" reaching the 2x16's terminal. The router process is started in its own session, so it survived; from then on its 12-hour limit and stuck check no longer applied.
**Summary of the sizes so far (LUT counts from my fit unless marked measured):** 1x16 25,042 measured, routed 28 min; 2x8 with units 42,615 measured, routed 22 to 36 min; 2x16 with units (predicted 57k to 75k) at iteration 5 after 17 h, 44,089 overused; 4x8 with units about 80,300, 23 iterations, 15,594 overused. Nothing at or above the 4x8/2x16 size has routed.
**Correction to #1453/#1454:** #1454 said switching the stuck rule off for seed 1 was worth trying; it did not help here. **Prepared, not built:** `k7/Makefile.fu3_s8_u8_n3` + `sc_k7_fu3s8u8n3.v` (3 blocks of 8 slices, three 8-lane units, about 61,500 LUT by the fit) and `k7/Makefile.fu4_s8_u1_n0` + `sc_k7_fu4s8u1n0.v` (4 blocks of 8, no fusion units, about 56,500 LUT by the fit); both elaborate in iverilog (NB 3 and 4, S 8). The second separates the fusion units from the number of blocks. The generator writes `.FZ(0), .ULW(2), .NU(0)` for no units (the same logic as before units existed).

## #1456 (21 Nov) -- Alan's reading: the routing problem is tied to something the blocks have in common; what they share in the RTL, and a count that lines up with the results (reading, not proven)

**Alan (21 Nov 04:10):** "it looks like there is a routing problem tied to a commonality between the blocks themselves and this is the point we need to look at."
*Read from the files (`fpga/sc_top_k7_v10.v`, `host_bridge_v11.v`, `far_arb.v`), counted with a script from the block port list; nothing built.* The blocks share **one host bridge, one far-RAM arbiter, one far RAM, and broadcast command buses** (every command bus goes to every block and is gated by `sel[gi]`). Each block returns to the hub a wide set of status and result wires (`o_busy`, `o_blocked`, `o_tail`, `o_search` at 64 bits each, `st_sl` 32 per slice, `st_bs` 40 per slice, queue, trap, fusion, scan, far-request and result words): **about 1,635 bits per block at 8 slices and 2,219 at 16 slices** (my count, includes the request and shared ack lines). The bridge reads them byte by byte (`rbyte = st_sl[...]` and similar, indexed by the host's byte number), so they are used and cannot be optimised away by the tool; whether every bit survives to routing is not checked. **Total wires into the hub:** 1x16 2,219 (routed); 2x8 3,270 (routed); 2x16 4,438 (stalled); 3x8 4,905 (not built); 4x8 6,540 (not routed). So the builds that routed have at most about 3,300; those that did not have 4,400 or more. **Five points, one count: a correlation that fits, not proof.** Cases that could still explain it otherwise: total size (LUT 43k routed, 57k+ failed), which moves with the same builds. Units are not part of this count (they are inside a block) and the 1x16 with units is not built. **A possible change (not made):** return status through a local byte port: the bridge sends a status address (broadcast, a few bits), each block selects its own byte, and only 8 bits per block come back (against 1,635 or 2,219); the same bytes, one extra clock of latency to handle in the bridge. A test of the reading: the same 4x8 with the narrowed hub; same logic size, so if it routes quickly the hub is the cause, if it does not the size is. **Not done:** no RTL changed; no simulation, no build. Needs Alan's go-ahead because it changes `sc_block_v3`, `host_bridge_v11`, the top and the multi-block tests.

## #1457 (21 Nov) -- The status read moved inside the blocks (`sc_top_k7_v11`, `host_bridge_v12`, `blk_rd_v1`): same replies byte for byte in simulation at 2 and at 4 blocks; 4x4 and 8x4 build files prepared; predictions logged before any build

**Alan (21 Nov 04:13 to 04:19):** asked for the narrowed status path ("create that") and whether the blocks could join through local controllers in a tree; then: start with a small run, "a 4 x 4", then an "8 x 4" ("we know the lower parts work, its the upper parts we need to test"), and run both designs (old and new) at the same time and compare them.
**Built (new files only; no proven file edited).** `fpga/blk_rd_v1.v`: the byte-reading logic of `host_bridge_v11` for the classes Q C T U E N A, **copied by script** (`gen_v12.py`, from the v11 text, not retyped), with the snapshots (cycle count, queue counts, busy and leader counts, hold, the 40-bit slice count) taken inside the block on the same clock edges. `fpga/host_bridge_v12.v`: v11 with those inputs removed; it now takes ONE byte (`rd_byte`) and drives `rd_cmd`, `rd_idx`, `rd_snap`, `rd_lat` (combinational, from the registers v11 used). `fpga/sc_top_k7_v11.v`: one `blk_rd_v1` per block, an 8-bit byte mux in place of the wide muxes (about 1,300 to 2,200 bits per block before), three LED bits per block; blocks, far-RAM arbiter, far RAM and the command broadcast as in v10. Still wide to the bridge, per block: the shared-RAM read word and its handshake (`dh_*`, 34 bits), the far-RAM host access (`fh_*`, 34 bits), `f_busy`, and the far-RAM request lines (57), about 130 bits against 1,635 (8 slices). `fpga/tb_k7fu3.v` (testbench for v11), `k7/make_mb_v4.py` and `k7/stage_mb_v4.sh` (generator and staging for the new top; files named `hn...`).
**Tested (simulation, iverilog, `test_hubnarrow_v1.py`; the same job plan run through the old top and the new one, every read command (V C T U E Q N A I Z L) sent to every block before the run, 150,000 clocks into it and after it).** **2 blocks x 4 slices (7 jobs, units on): all 90 status replies equal byte for byte, old against new, and all jobs give the Python answer on the new top.** **4 blocks x 2 slices: all 180 status replies equal, all 7 jobs right on both tops (run with job widths 0 to 1, which fit 2-slice blocks).** This is the **first whole-board run with more than 2 blocks** (#1352 had 1 and 2): the arbiter, the block select and the shared far RAM worked with 4 blocks in simulation. **A first 4-block attempt failed on BOTH tops** because the test gave blocks 2 and 3 jobs of width 2 (4 lanes) in 2-slice blocks, so those blocks sat busy at step 11 with no error (measured: blocks 0 and 1 done in about 12,000 and 28,000 clocks, blocks 2 and 3 still busy after 1.7 to 2.2 million); the rerun with fitting widths passed. My first reading of that failure as a possible far-RAM starvation was wrong. Not tested: any card; any NB above 4; timing or routing (simulation only).
**Predictions, logged before any build (my hub-width count from #1456; LUT from my fit, #1445).** 4 blocks x 4 slices, no units: about **42,000 LUT** (the 2x8 that routed was 42,615 measured), hub wires into the bridge in the OLD top about 5,400 (4 x (1,051 + 73 x 4 + request lines), my count). Old top at 4x4: **hub reading says it struggles (more wires than the 2x16 that stalled), size reading says it routes like the 2x8.** New top at 4x4: **hub reading says it routes like the 2x8 (22 to 36 minutes), size reading says the same as the old top.** The two builds have the same logic and different hub width, so the pair separates the two readings. 8 blocks x 4 slices (about 79,000 LUT, old hub about 10,700 wires) comes second.
**Prepared, not built:** `k7/Makefile.fu4_s4_u1_n0` + `sc_k7_fu4s4u1n0.v` (old top, 4x4, no units); `k7/Makefile.hn4_s4_u1_n0` + `sc_k7_hn4s4u1n0.v` (new top, same); the same pair for 8x4 (`fu8_s4_u1_n0`, `hn8_s4_u1_n0`). Stage with `k7/stage_mb_v4.sh`.
**The tree question.** Answer for now: yes, in the same spirit. The status path is now the first level of such a tree (each block answers locally, one byte goes up); the same pattern fits the far-RAM requests (a small arbiter for each group of blocks, registered, then one at the top) and the command broadcast (registered stages). The far-RAM tree changes the arbitration order and adds clocks, so the proven far-copy clock counts (#1352) would change; it is **not built**, and is the next step if the narrowed 4x4 still fails.

## #1458 (21 Nov) -- 4x4 (no units), old top against new narrowed top, seed 1, both ended by the stuck rule, neither routed: the narrowed top got 7 times closer and took a third of the time

*Measured (Alan's pasted progress lines and log summaries, 21 Nov 09:22).* Builds started about 06:00 on Alan's PC, one after the other in two terminals; 2x16 and the earlier 4x8 builds were no longer running. **New top** (`Makefile.hn4_s4_u1_n0`, sc_top_k7_v11 + host_bridge_v12 + blk_rd_v1): overused at iterations 1, 2, 3 = 67,339 / 19,425 / 7,359; 20 iterations; **lowest 993**; "STUCK (overused 1161 at iter 12 -> 1046 at iter 20, fell 9 % < 15 %)" at 43 minutes (06:41). **Old top** (`Makefile.fu4_s4_u1_n0`): 69,095 / 25,108 / 12,455; 15 iterations; **lowest 7,073**; "STUCK (overused 7250 at iter 7 -> 9247 at iter 15, fell -27 %)" at 142 minutes (08:22). Same logic, same seed, same size (my fit: about 42,000 LUT, not measured, no place-and-route report read); only the hub differs (my count: about 5,400 wires into the bridge in the old top). Times include synthesis and placing. Both: one seed; stopped at different iteration counts (20 against 15), so the two cannot be compared at the same iteration from what I have.
**Reading (not proven).** The narrowed hub helps a lot (a seven-fold lower best count, a third of the time), which is what the hub reading of #1456 predicted for this pair and not what the size reading predicted (it predicted equal). It is **not enough on its own**: the narrowed 4x4 hovered at about 1,000 overused (1,161, 1,027, 1,079, 1,046 for iterations 12 to 20) and did not route. Whether it would route without the stuck rule, or at a lower speed target, is unknown. The stuck rule has now ended three near misses (4x8 seed 1 at 6,415 to 8,093; this build at about 1,000; the old 4x4 at 7,000 to 9,000).
**Not done.** No place-and-route report read for either (utilisation, maximum frequency); the 8x4 pair not built; no card.

## #1459 (21 Nov) -- command side narrowed: ID-addressed command bus (`sc_top_k7_v12`, `host_bridge_v13`, `blk_cmd_v1`, `blk_rd_v2`); replies equal byte for byte to the old top at 2 and 4 blocks, after one simulator artefact was found and fixed

*Idea (Alan, 21 Nov):* the FPGA's own command path is not part of the routed wiring; here the same idea in the fabric: keep the broadcast but make it carry a block ID, and only the selected block acts on it. *Built (clones; v10/v11/v12 bridge, blk_rd_v1 left as proven):* `host_bridge_v13` no longer drives about 450 decoded wires to every block; it pulses `c_v` (one clock, where v12 set its pulse registers) with the raw command byte `c_cmd` and eight argument bytes `c_a` (73 wires), and `blk_cmd_v1` in each block decodes them when its select line is set, giving the same values in the same clock (a pulse, or the new value in that cycle and held after; the far-RAM host request is held until accepted, as before). Conditions that decide whether a command is carried out (`dh_busy`, `f_busy`, `f_init`) stay in the bridge. `sc_top_k7_v12` is `sc_top_k7_v11` with the 40 decoded signals replaced by a `blk_cmd_v1` per block; generators `gen_v13.py`, `gen_top_v12.py` (the one-word change to `blk_rd_v2` was made by hand afterwards). Build files `make_mb_v5.py` / `stage_mb_v5.sh` write `Makefile.hc<NB>_s<S>_u<W>_n<NU>` (`hc4_s4_u1_n0`, `hc8_s4_u1_n0` made; **not built on Alan's PC**).
*Measured (simulation, iverilog; `test_hubnarrow_v2.py` (clone of v1 with `NEWTOP=12`; v1 untouched) with `NEWTOP=12`):* first run **FAIL**: 4 of 90 replies differed, byte 0 only, in E queries on block 1 (job 0 and 3, mid-run and after; old 00 / new ee and the reverse); bytes 1 to 4, all Q replies, all counters equal. Cause (found with a probe printing the job ID and the byte in the cycle the bridge reads it): the block already had the new job ID in that cycle (decoder timing correct), but `blk_rd_v1` computes its byte with `assign rbyte = rbyte_f(cmd, idx)` and the function reads the status signals directly, not as arguments; in iverilog such a call is re-evaluated only when its arguments change, so a status signal that settles a clock after `cmd`/`idx` (the job ID, now one clock later than in v11) gave the previous value. **A simulation artefact, not a hardware difference** (synthesis treats the function as plain logic); my first guess ("byte 0 reads the previous query") was half right, and my earlier guess that it was an uninitialised table was wrong. Fix: `blk_rd_v2` passes every signal the function reads as an extra unused argument. After it: **PASS at 2 blocks x 4 slices (90 status replies equal, 129 replies, jobs right on both builds, cycle counters non-zero) and at 4 blocks x 2 slices (180 replies equal, 221 replies, LWS=1,1,1,0,1,1,0)**. Wire counts of the shared command side are my arithmetic (about 450 to 73), no synthesis report read.
**Not done / limits.** No place-and-route of `hc4` / `hc8`; no card; a latent point in `blk_rd_v1` (v11 top) remains: it passed #1457 because the signals moved with `cmd`/`idx` there, so the v11 equality results stand but are not protected against it. The far-RAM request lines (57 bits per block) and the 32-bit ack are still shared. **Sequencer (read, not run):** `batch_seq_v2` runs a script of 4-word steps from far RAM (END, JUMP, LCODE, LDATA, SAVE, REGION, WINDOW, LIBRARY, SUBMIT, WAIT) and pulses the scheduler commands, so a job can run on the card with no host between steps; it is wired only in older tops (k7_v1, v6, pool15, bridges v8 and v9), has no block-select opcode and no access to the other host commands. Plan: a v3 with a `BLOCK n` opcode producing the raw `c_v/c_cmd/c_a` word and a bridge switch (host / sequencer), giving Alan's two modes (job dumped on the card; direct host sequencing); not started.

## #1460 (21 Nov) -- router results after #1458: 4x4 seeds 3 and 2, 8x4 seed 1 (stuck rule on): none routed; the 8x4 reached 277 overused, the 4x4 best 140

*Measured (Alan's pasted progress lines).* New top (`sc_top_k7_v11`, status inside the blocks), no units, 50 MHz, stuck rule on (15 iterations before judging, 15 % over 8). **4x4 seed 3:** iterations 4 to 23: 3,752 / 2,378 / 1,975 / ... 1,148 (iter 9) / 925 (11) / 785 (15) / 719 (16) / 653 (20) / 705 (23): STUCK (785 at 15 to 705 at 23, 10 %), 73 minutes. **4x4 seed 2:** 67,249 / 18,741 / 6,125 / 2,888 / 1,715 (iter 5) / 734 (8) / 310 (15) / 184 (22) / 154 (28) / 140 (41): STUCK (149 at 33 to 140 at 41, 6 %), placing 29 minutes, iterations about 1 minute. **8x4 (`hn8`) seed 1:** placing 75 minutes; 405 (iter 14) / 284 (21) / 277 (28): STUCK (301 at 20 to 277 at 28, 7 %). Earlier: 4x4 seed 1 lowest 993 (#1458); 4x4 seed 1 with the rule off rose to 1,903 at iteration 39 (not logged before). Placement-estimate maximum frequency: hn4 113.40 MHz, fu4 91.36 MHz against the 50 MHz target (read from the logs): not timing-limited. One seed each; seeds differ widely (993, 653, 140 at 4x4).
**Reading (not proven).** The 8x4 (277) got closer than the 4x4 seeds 1 and 3, which fits the narrowed design scaling better than the old one (the old 4x8 stalled at 6,000 to 8,000), but one seed each does not separate seed luck from size. Alan's note: even if these routed, that would be near the limit of this design; the measurements here can only say where that limit sits, not remove it. **Open:** two reruns with the rule off were started by Alan: 8x4 seed 1 (MINITER=100000, MAXHOURS=3, confirmed in the process environment) and 4x4 seed 2 (command suggested; not confirmed); results not yet seen.

Neither routed. I do not have the values between the two points (only the lines Alan pasted).

Same seed gave the same path up to the point Alan's earlier lines show (4x4 seed 2: 140 at iteration 41 in both runs, from the progress logs; not separately compared line for line).

## #1464 (21 Nov) -- the command-bus build (`hc2_s8_u8_n3`) on the card: jobs correct, clocks equal to the old 2x8 within run-to-run jitter

*Measured on Alan's Kintex card (pasted output, 16:31 to 16:38).* First attempt failed because Alan ran the system `/usr/bin/openFPGALoader` ("Unknown device with IDCODE: 0x23751093"); inside the openXC7 shell (`/nix/store/...-openfpgaloader-1.1.1`) the load worked ("done 1"). Commands from `LADDER.md` and `cold_test_v1.sh`. **One job, block 0:** `ladder_1_one_job.json`, 1 of 1 correct (10), 9,109 clocks. **Two blocks** (`ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 2 --unit-lw 3 --lw 2`, fresh load before each): new build 4 of 4 jobs correct, block 0 28,444 / block 1 28,283 clocks; old `fu2_s8_u8_n3` 4 of 4 correct, 28,445 / 28,283. **Repeated, fresh load each, three more runs per build (block 0, block 1):** new 28,446 28,285 / 28,445 28,285 / 28,443 28,285; old 28,445 28,289 / 28,449 28,287 / 28,446 28,285. ## #1466 (21 Nov) -- CORRECTION: the unit-less builds (`NU(0)`) do not run jobs; the routing results of #1458, #1460, #1461 and #1465 were on a build that is not functional; fixed generator `make_mb_v6.py` (`hd...`)

*Measured on the card (Alan, 18:24 to 18:25).* `sc_k7_hc4s4u1n0.bit` (4 blocks x 4 slices, no units, ID-addressed command bus) loaded ("done 1"); the host found 4 slices per block, wrote the programs into far RAM; all four blocks stopped with **error 6 (a WAIT timed out: a job did not finish)**, with `--lw 1` and again with `--lw 0` (my first guess, that `--lw 1` needed a unit, was wrong).
*Measured in simulation (iverilog, `test_hubnarrow_v2.py` with `NB=2 S=4 FZ=0 LWS=0,...`):* with `NU=0` (what `make_mb_v4.py` and `make_mb_v5.py` wrote for every unit-less build) **no job completes on either top** (old `sc_top_k7_v10` + bridge v11 and new `sc_top_k7_v12`): status 0xEEEEEEEE (unset), done count 0, 90 status replies equal because both fail the same way. With `FZ=0 NU=1`: **PASS** (jobs right on the old build, all jobs give the Python answer on the new build, cycle counters and done counts non-zero, 90 replies equal).
*Cause (read from `sc_sys_v17.v`, consistent with both results):* the file is made for at least one unit (arrays `[NU-1:0]`, `[0:NU-1]`; its own header says units need FZ = 1), so `NU=0` gives malformed ranges; the generator's "ULW 0 = no unit" wrote `.FZ(0), .ULW(2), .NU(0)`. I did not trace which signal is unset inside the module. *Fix:* `make_mb_v6.py` (clone of v5; v5 left as proven) writes `.FZ(0), .ULW(2), .NU(1)` for ULW 0 and names the files `hd<NB>_s<S>_u1_n1` (`hd4_s4_u1_n1`, `hd8_s4_u1_n1` made, not built); `stage_mb_v6.sh`.
**What this changes (read, not measured).** (1) Every unit-less result so far is on a design that cannot run jobs: #1458 (hn4 vs fu4), #1460, #1461, the hn8 runs, and the "ROUTED" #1465 (`hc4`). Synthesis may have removed logic whose results were never used, so those builds are probably smaller or easier to route than a working 4x4; I have not read their LUT counts (requested) and cannot say how much. The comparison of the designs against each other has the same defect on both sides, but "the 4x4 routes" is **not established**. (2) Builds WITH units are not affected: `hc2_s8_u8_n3` (FZ 1, NU 3) ran correctly on the card (#1464); the earlier 2x8 and 16-slice builds also have units. (3) The equivalence tests of #1457 and #1459 used units and stand; the unit-less configuration was never simulated before today. I should have run a unit-less simulation before building and routing for hours; that is the lesson for the next step: no build goes to the PC until its configuration has passed a job test in simulation.
**Next.** Rebuild `hd4_s4_u1_n1` (new top) and the same with the wide-command top for a fair pair, after a simulation of the exact configuration passes (done for NB=2, S=4; NB=4 not yet).

## #1467 (21 Nov) -- the exact 4x4 unit-less configuration (`FZ=0 NU=1`) passes in simulation; LUT count and the end of the 4x4 seed 2 rerun for the non-functional builds

*Measured.* **Simulation** (`test_hubnarrow_v2.py`, `NB=4 S=4 NU=1 FZ=0 LWS=0,0,0,0,0,0,0 NEWTOP=12`, old `sc_top_k7_v10` + bridge v11 against new `sc_top_k7_v12`): both builds ran, 221 replies each, **all 180 status replies equal byte for byte**, jobs right on the old build, **all jobs give the Python answer on the new build**, C replies show work done on all 4 blocks (cycle counters about 4.67 to 5.00 million, done counts 1, 2, 2, 2). This is the configuration of `hd4_s4_u1_n1` (made by `make_mb_v6.py`, #1466); it took about 35 minutes. **LUT count** of the non-working `hc4` (NU 0), from Alan's grep of `build_sc_k7_hc4s4u1n0_seed1.log`: SLICE_LUTX 43,788 (7 %), SLICE_FFX 20,198, CARRY4 2,029. For scale only: the 2x8 with units (`hc2_s8_u8_n3`) is 44,819 (#1463); my earlier fit plus about 1,100 per block for the command side would put a unit-less 4x4 near 46,400 (arithmetic from rough fits, not a measurement), so `hc4` is about 6 % lower; I cannot tell how much logic was dropped.
**4x4 seed 2 rerun (`hn4`, the NU 0 build, stuck rule off, 6 h limit), Alan's lines up to 19:11:** lowest overused **6 at iteration 939 (116 minutes)**; from about minute 126 it rose (22 at iteration 1013, 99 at 1089, 998 at 1118, 2,565 at 1128, 3,550 at iteration 1131, 236 minutes) and was advancing by about one iteration every several minutes. It never reached 0 and is a non-functional build, so no conclusion about routing a working 4x4 follows from it except that this seed oscillated near zero and then diverged.
**Reading (not proven).** The simulated working 4x4 config is equal old and new, so the plan is to build `hd4_s4_u1_n1` and, for a fair comparison, an equivalent of the wide-command top with `NU 1`; **not done:** neither built, nothing on the card for the unit-less configuration.

## #1468 (21 Nov) -- the working 4x4 (`hd4_s4_u1_n1`: new top, no fusion, `FZ=0 NU=1`) ROUTED on seed 1 and runs correctly on the card: 4 of 4 jobs right on 4 blocks

*Measured (Alan's pasted output, 20:33 to 20:43).* **Routing:** seed 1, 50 MHz, stuck rule on, first seed tried: placing to minute 25, "iter 1 overused 70526", 3,998 at iteration 3, **0 at iteration 43**, "DONE, bitstream sc_k7_hd4s4u1n1.bit written" at 30 minutes of the run (20:41), "Max frequency for clock 'u.SYS_CLK': 76.31 MHz (PASS at 50.00 MHz)". **Card** (`openFPGALoader -c ft232` from the openXC7 shell, "done 1"; `scblocks.py jtag ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 4 --lw 0`): board version 12, 4 slices per block, 4 blocks; all 4 blocks finished in 0.11 s; per block clocks (10 steps each) 92,758 / 92,761 / 86,156 / 66,075; **4 of 4 jobs correct**, `ladder_show` MATCH on all four (5050, 134650, 242, 9649). The configuration had passed the simulation of #1467 (180 status replies equal, jobs right) before the build was made.
**Reading (one seed; no fusion).** With the status and the command decode both inside the blocks, a 4x4 that actually runs jobs routes on its first seed in half an hour and works on the card, where the earlier designs in this family did not route at 4 blocks in four (wide command, unit-less but non-functional) or three (old top) tries. The earlier "ROUTED" `hc4` (#1465) was not a working design (#1466); this one is. **Not done:** LUT count of `hd4` not yet read; no `hd8`; no fusion at 4 blocks (`hd4_s4_u4_n2` made, its simulation still running at the time of writing); no same-seed `hn`-type comparison with `NU 1` (the wide-command top was never rebuilt with the fix, so the claim that the command narrowing, and not the `NU` repair, made the difference is **not isolated**: the earlier `hn4` runs had the broken `NU 0`).
