## #1470 (21 Nov) -- the 8x4 (`hd8_s4_u1_n1`, new top, no fusion) did not route on seed 1 but reached 56 overused; its configuration runs jobs in simulation at 8 blocks

*Measured (Alan's pasted progress lines, build started 20:46).* Seed 1, 50 MHz, stuck rule on, 3 h limit: placing 78 minutes, router start to minute 83, then overused **143,017** (iteration 1, printed at minute 83 to 91) / 36,836 / 10,688 / 1,436 (iteration 6) / 137 (iteration 22) / 63 (iteration 37) / **56 (iteration 45)**; "STUCK (overused 63 at iter 37 -> 56 at iter 45, fell 11 % < 15 %): stopping it" at 22:21; no route. Iteration 1 is about 9 minutes; after that about one iteration per minute. **Simulation** (new top only, `NB=8 S=4 NU=1 FZ=0`, job widths all 1, 7 jobs planned one per block on blocks 0 to 6): no link errors, **all jobs give the Python answer**, done counts 1 on blocks 0 to 6 and 0 on block 7 (no job planned there); about 70 minutes. A first attempt of this simulation (with the old top as well) was killed by a restart of my workspace overnight and gave nothing; the old top was not run at 8 blocks, so the equality of replies old against new is **not** shown at 8 blocks.
**Reading (one seed; not proven).** At 8 blocks x 4 slices the design with status and command decode inside the blocks comes to 56 overused where the old design stalled at about 6,000 to 8,000 for 4 blocks x 8 slices and 7,000 at 4x4 (#1458, #1451: different shapes, same total slices for 4x8); it did not route, which is the first time the target size is this close. Seeds vary widely (the 4x4 `hn` seeds gave 993, 653, 140), so seed 1 alone does not tell whether 8x4 routes. **Not done:** LUT count of `hd8` not read; no rule-off rerun, no other seed; no card run; not built with fusion.

## #1471 (22 Nov) -- the 8x4 (`hd8_s4_u1_n1`) ROUTED on seed 1 with the stuck rule off: 0 overused at iteration 122, 64.07 MHz; the bitstream was then deleted by a second run in the same folder and is being regenerated

*Measured (Alan's pasted progress lines, run started 21 Nov evening, finished 22 Nov 05:24).* Seed 1, 50 MHz, `MINITER=100000` (stuck rule off), up to 4 h: at minute 88 / 89 / 90 / 91 of the run, iteration 41 / 71 / 105 / 122 = overused **58 / 35 / 17 / 0**; "DONE, bitstream sc_k7_hd8s4u1n1.bit written" (05:24), "Max frequency for clock 'u.SYS_CLK': 64.07 MHz (PASS at 50.00 MHz)"; total 92 minutes. The same seed with the stuck rule on had been stopped at 56 overused in iteration 45 (#1470): here the rule ended a run that was about 80 iterations from finishing. This is the 8 blocks x 4 slices build without fusion (`FZ=0 NU=1`), the configuration simulated at 8 blocks in #1470 (new top only).
**Second run in the same folder (Alan, apologised for it; my advice had left the risk open).** A second terminal ran `seeds_v1.sh` on the same project with `SEEDS="2 3"`; its seed 3 started about 05:31, after seed 1 finished, and **deleted the finished `.bit`** (the script removes `.fasm`, `.frames` and `.bit` before each seed, as its header says); that seed 3 sat at iteration 2 (46,035 overused) until its 3-hour limit (08:31), probably a memory squeeze while two builds were running, which I did not check. At 12:45 only `sc_k7_hd8s4u1n1.json` and the top `.v` were left; the seed 1 log with the result and the timing line survived. The rerun of seed 1 with the rule off was started to regenerate the bitstream (same seed, same placement; the same route is expected but not yet confirmed), with a copy to `keep_hd8_seed1_ok.bit` as soon as it is written.
**Reading (one seed; not proven).** The design with status and command decode inside the blocks places and routes at 8 blocks x 4 slices (32 slices) and meets 50 MHz with margin (64.07 MHz); the stuck rule is too eager for this design (it ended a run that was one dip from zero, here and for `hn4` seed 2 at 6 overused). Two practical rules: one build per project name at a time, and copy the bitstream to a safe name when it is written. **Not done:** the rerun's result; LUT count of `hd8`; the card test (`--blocks 8 --lw 0`); no 8-block old-against-new equality; no fusion at 8 blocks.

## #1472 (22 Nov) -- measured size of the 8x4 (`hd8_s4_u1_n1`): 89,781 LUT (15 %); the stepped plan to larger builds

*Measured (nextpnr "Device utilisation" in `build_sc_k7_hd8s4u1n1_seed1_noroute.log`, the first, unrouted run of the same netlist; Alan's grep):* SLICE_LUTX **89,781** / 597,200 (15 %), SLICE_FFX **43,775** (7 %), CARRY4 **4,123** (5 %). My estimate before reading it (fit 4,890 + 5,640 per block + 907 per slice, plus about 1,100 per block for the command side) was 88,000 LUT and 45,000 FF, within 2 % and 3 %; the same method gave 46,400 for `hd4`, measured 45,726 (#1469). Final timing figures so far (placement/route "Max frequency", PASS at 50 MHz): `hc2_s8_u8_n3` 68.95, `hd4_s4_u1_n1` 76.31, `hd8_s4_u1_n1` 64.07 MHz.
**Plan (Alan, 22 Nov): scale up in steps, test each, until timing or routing fails.** Predictions from the same fit (arithmetic, not measurements): 8x6 about 104,000 LUT (17 %), 8x8 about 118,000 (20 %), 8x12 about 147,000 (25 %), 8x16 about 176,000 (29 %), all without fusion units; units add about 6,000 per block for three 8-lane units (same rough fit). Cautions: the final timing figure fell from 76 MHz at 4 blocks to 64 MHz at 8 (two points, no extrapolation) so timing may limit before routing; more slices per block adds local wiring that the narrowing did not touch (1x16 routed in 28 minutes, #1357-era measurement, not re-read here); 6 slices per block was never simulated (2, 4 and 8 were); every step needs its exact configuration to run jobs in simulation before it is built (#1466). `hd8_s8_u1_n1` (8 blocks x 8 slices) build file made, not simulated, not built.
**Not done.** Everything above beyond 8x4; the card test of 8x4 (waiting for the regenerated bitstream).

## #1473 (22-23 Nov) -- 8x4 passes on the card (4 blocks used); 16x2 fails in placement on three seeds; 12x2 places but the router stalls near 2,300 overused; 8x8 not finished in 4 h

**Measured, 8x4 card test (`hd8_s4_u1_n1`, regenerated bitstream, kept as `keep_hd8_seed1_ok.bit`, `scblocks.py jtag ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 8 --lw 0`):** 4 of 4 jobs match (5050, 134650, 242, 9649); clocks 92,758 / 92,761 / 86,156 / 66,074. **Scope:** the job file has only 4 jobs, so blocks 0-3 ran work; blocks 4-7 were present (board reports version 12, 4 slices per block) but had no job. The regenerated build reproduced the first one exactly (iteration 122, 64.07 MHz, 98 min; same seed, same netlist).
**Measured, 16x2 (`hd16_s2_u1_n1`, 16 blocks x 2 slices, FZ 0 NU 1; not simulated: the 16-block simulation was killed twice by workspace restarts after about 3 h each, Alan chose to build anyway, which is a departure from the #1466 rule):** synthesis 141,579 LUT (23 %), 67,972 FF (11 %), 6,577 CARRY4, 113 RAMB18, 64 RAMB36; my fit predicted about 142,000 (read: agrees). **Placement fails** on seeds 1, 3 and 2 with "Unable to find legal placement for cell ... of type SLICE_FFX after 10001 attempts" (a different cell each seed; 2,810 control sets). `--placer-heap-cell-placement-timeout 100000` was on the command line (log line read) but the log still printed "max placement attempts per cell = 10000": the option had no effect in this nextpnr build. Cause not identified.
**Comparison with the 8x4 (log reads):** FDRE 40,476 -> 64,057, FDRE_1 (falling edge) 2,920 -> 3,248, control sets 1,889 -> 2,810, LUT-FF pairs 26,849 -> 41,852. **Correction:** my first guess, that falling-edge registers in `sc_sys_v17` scale with the block count and break placement, is not supported (they grew 11 %, the 8x4 placed with 2,920); my figure of "about 203 per block" was wrong.
**Measured, 12x2 (`hd12_s2_u1_n1`, not simulated, same departure from #1466):** placed (about 80 min), then routing: overused 167,970 (iter 1), 50,922 (2), 18,183 (3), 5,670 (5), 2,898 (8), 2,537 (10), 2,192 (15), 2,251 (16), 2,261 (17), 2,307 (18); the 4 h budget (counted from the start of the build, so about 2 h 15 min of routing) ended the run, rule off. **Correction:** I first called it "still falling"; the timestamps show a plateau near 2,200-2,300 that rose over the last three iterations. For comparison the 8x4 reached 56 overused and then routed at iteration 122. I also gave 52 min for iteration 2; the log shows 40 (20:15 to 20:55), and I said the 12x2 placed in minutes and the 8x8 much slower; the 12x2 took about 80 min and the 8x8 about 2 h 40.
**Measured, 8x8 (`hd8_s8_u1_n1`, not simulated):** placement took about 2 h 40, routing started 21:09 and was at iteration 1 (209,371 overused) when the 4 h budget stopped it. No conclusion about whether it would route.
**Reasoned, not shown:** that shared wiring (far-RAM request lines, 57 bits per block, and the 32-bit acknowledge broadcast) is the next limit; narrowing it the way the command bus was narrowed (#1457) is the candidate next step. Not built.
**Other.** `seeds_v2.sh` (adds `PNR_EXTRA`), `Makefile.hd12_s2_u1_n1`/`sc_k7_hd12s2u1n1.v`, `Makefile.hd16_s2_u1_n1`/`sc_k7_hd16s2u1n1.v` and `vivado/run_vivado_v1.tcl` (untested: no Vivado was available) are in `s-core/k7/`. Vivado (read from AMD's licensing page): free Basic tier covers all 7-series, no Incremental Compile/DFX/Abstract Shell; the 50K logic-cell cap applies to the built-in simulator/ILA, not to implementation; install is large (about 98 GB download); `/` had 5.1 GB free, so it goes on the 1.3 TB drive. A Tom's Hardware piece on an AI-designed tile CPU (Architect Labs "Frostbite", pre-silicon, claims by the company) was discussed as context only; nothing in it was verified.
**Not done:** 8-job/16-job card test of the 8x4 with all blocks working; any simulation of 12, 16 or 8x8 builds; any far-RAM narrowing; a baseline comparison with a soft core (LiteX on the Tang or the K7 card); the Vivado runs.

## #1474 (24 Nov) -- Vivado 2026.2 (free Basic) installed; the 8x4, 12x2 and 16x2 all place, route and meet 50 MHz in Vivado in about 15 min each; far-RAM DRC REQP-1962 stopped the 12x2 and 16x2 until lowered to a warning (exploration only)

**Setup (read from Alan's pastes).** Installer `FPGAs_AdaptiveSoCs_Unified_SDI_2026.2` run on Linux Mint 22 (warning "OS 'linuxmint' is not a recognized/supported host OS", harmless); installed to `/media/alan/InternalHDD/Xilinx/2026.2/Vivado` (layout `<install>/<version>/Vivado/settings64.sh`, not the older `Vivado/<version>`); `vivado -version` v2026.2; part `xc7k480tffg1156-1` present; log line "A valid Vivado Design Suite BASIC license has been detected ... expire on 22-nov-2027". Vivado first failed with `libncurses.so.5` missing; `apt` had no `libncurses5`/`libtinfo5`; the fix used was symlinks from `libncurses.so.6`/`libtinfo.so.6` (a common workaround, not verified beyond it starting).
**Scripts (clones; v1 and v2 left as written).** `vivado/run_vivado_v1.tcl` stopped at `read_xdc` ("No open design"; in-memory project, my mistake); `run_vivado_v2.tcl` (non-project flow, `clock50_v1.xdc` gives the 50 MHz clock) ran the 8x4 through; `run_vivado_v3.tcl` = v2 plus `set_property SEVERITY {Warning} [get_drc_checks REQP-1962]`, **exploration only, writes no bitstream**.
**Measured, Vivado, all at 50 MHz target (Alan's greps of `timing_summary.txt`, `route_status.txt`, `utilization_routed.txt`):**
| build | LUT (of 298,600) | FF (of 597,200) | BRAM tiles | setup WNS | hold WHS | routed nets, errors |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8x4 `hd8s4u1n1` (v2) | 49,046 (16.43 %, synth) | 40,193 | 116.5 | +1.071 | +0.047 | 87,285, 0 |
| 12x2 `hd12s2u1n1` (v3) | 56,331 (18.87 %) | 47,850 | 106.5 | +0.657 | +0.051 | 103,546, 0 |
| 16x2 `hd16s2u1n1` (v3) | 72,393 (24.24 %) | 62,897 | 120.5 | +1.079 | +0.039 | 134,759, 0 |
8x4 routing took 4 min (elapsed), 16x2 routing 5.5 min; a whole run about 15 min, against 98 min for the open flow's 8x4 and no result at all for 12x2 / 16x2.
**Measured, open tool for the same builds:** 12x2 `hd12_s2_u1_n1`: SLICE_LUTX 106,849 / 597,200 (17 %), SLICE_FFX 51,316; my fit predicted about 107,000 (a prediction checked after calibration on other sizes). 16x2: 141,579 (23 %) against a fit of about 142,000. The open tool's capacity (597,200 LUT sites) is twice Vivado's (298,600 LUTs), so compare percentages: 12x2 17 % vs 18.9 %, 16x2 23.7 % vs 24.2 %. Raw counts differ by about a factor 1.9 and I did not trace why (reasoned: Vivado maps the per-slice register files to block RAM, "Synth 8-7052" lines name `rf_a_reg`/`rf_b_reg`).
**Not the same netlist.** Vivado runs its own synthesis, so these results mix a stronger router with a smaller netlist; they show the design fits and routes under another toolchain, not that the open router was the only limit.
**Far-RAM DRC (read from the log).** For 12x2 and 16x2, `place_design` stopped with six `DRC REQP-1962` errors: cascaded RAMB36E1 pairs `u/ctl/mem_reg_0_x` / `mem_reg_1_x` for x = 2, 7, 12, 17, 22, 27, ADDR15 pin not tied the same. The 8x4 raised none, with the same `farram_bram_v1` (64K words x 32 bit). **Cause not found.** The check was lowered to a warning in v3 only to see whether the design routes; **a bitstream from that is not trusted** (the check may guard a real addressing fault). Candidate fix, not written: split the far RAM into two 32K banks (new clone, job simulation first).
**Not done:** any Vivado bitstream; any simulation of the 12- or 16-block configurations (the 16-block simulation was killed twice by workspace restarts); the card test of the 8x4 with all 8 blocks (`ladder_6_eight_jobs.json`, expected 1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480, computed in Python, not on the card); builds `hd16_s4_u1_n1` and `hd16_s8_u1_n1` (made and pushed, fit puts them near 29 % and 38 % of the card, an extrapolation) have not been run; the Vivado timing figure is slack against a 20 ns target, not a maximum frequency (the open tool printed 64.07 MHz for the 8x4, Vivado's +1.071 ns implies about 52.8 MHz, the two do not measure the same thing).

## #1475 (24 Nov) -- card: the open-tool 8x4 runs all 8 blocks correctly; the first Vivado-built bitstream (8x4) loads and talks but gives wrong job results; Vivado 16x4 and 16x8 route; two-bank far RAM written

**Measured on the card, open-tool 8x4 (`keep_hd8_seed1_ok.bit`), `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json` (8 jobs, one per block), `--blocks 8 --lw 0`:** 8 of 8 jobs match the Python answers (1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480; the answers were worked out in Python before the run). Clocks per block 85,151 / 66,599 / 71,096 / 57,879 / 57,039 / 49,159 / 40,439 / 42,983 (sum about 470,000; blocks run at the same time so the run took as long as the longest, 85,151; one block doing them in turn would need about 5.5 times as long, reasoned from clocks per job, not measured). First time all 8 blocks of this build each ran a job (the earlier test, #1473, used 4). Loading from `/usr/bin/openFPGALoader` fails ("Unknown device with IDCODE: 0x23751093") and `nix develop github:openxc7/toolchain-nix` started rebuilding; the working loader is `/nix/store/77r1cv86zjls4xix4kijr1sv9vn9a5yq-openfpgaloader-1.1.1/bin/openFPGALoader -c ft232 FILE.bit` (found with a case-insensitive search; the store has 17,557 entries and was intact).
**Vivado, more sizes (Alan's greps; v3 script: DRC REQP-1962 lowered, no bitstream; the scripts read every `rtl/*.sv`, see below, so treat these numbers as provisional):** 16x4 `hd16s4u1n1`: 95,475 LUT (31.97 %), 79,038 FF, 168.5 BRAM tiles, setup slack +1.187 ns, hold +0.012, 172,547 nets routed, 0 errors. 16x8 `hd16s8u1n1`: 139,800 LUT (46.82 %), 111,254 FF, 264.5 BRAM tiles, setup +0.388, hold +0.017, 246,107 nets, 0 errors (routing 9 min 47 s, peak memory 5.5 GB). My fit had said about 29 % and 38 % (open-tool units, extrapolation); Vivado measures higher at the top (slices cost more than the 907 LUT the fit assumes, or the two tools count differently; the open tool never built these sizes). Setup slack fell from about +1.1 to +0.388 at 16x8: timing may limit first (reasoned). The count of REQP-1962 lines in the v3 logs (2 for both 12x2 and 16x4) does not show whether the check would trip on the 16x4: lowered violations are not printed under that ID.
**Vivado-built 8x4 on the card (`run_vivado_v4.tcl`, check ON, `hd8s4u1n1.bit`, 18.7 MB, `write_bitstream completed successfully`):** loads (Done, done 1); board version 12, 4 slices per block, 1,224 words loaded into far RAM; all 8 blocks run their scripts to the end with no error and the end tags match; **0 of 8 jobs correct**: every job's status word reads 8 (expected 1) and every result 8; blocks finish in about 13,000 clocks each (the open build: 40,000-85,000). `far_ram_check_v1.py jtag 0x2000 512` (four patterns, 512 words each): **0 wrong words, no bit differs, on both the Vivado bitstream and the open-tool control**; it covers only addresses 0x2000-0x21FF (lower half of the 64K-word RAM; the failed DRC concerned the upper half, 0x8000 and up, not tested).
**Duplicate modules (read from the Vivado log):** 21 `Synth 8-9873` lines: `sc_lane` 8, `sc_rdsel4/8/16` 2 each, `sc_wacc4/8/16` 2 each (fusion-unit files, not used by an `FZ=0` build, reasoned), `pk_ram` 1 (defined in `sc_sys_v12` and `sc_sys_v17`; I diffed the two definitions: identical). So the duplicates are probably not the cause; not proven. `run_vivado_v5.tcl` (reads only the Makefile's `ADDITIONAL_SOURCES`; untested) is pushed.
**Not found:** why the Vivado netlist's jobs report status 8. Candidates, none checked: how Vivado maps the block RAMs (it put the per-slice register files in block RAM, "Synth 8-7052"), a difference between the yosys and Vivado reading of some construct, an asynchronous crossing. Next steps listed for Alan: single job on one block with the Vivado bitstream (`ladder_1_one_job.json`, expected 10), a catalogue of Vivado warning types, the v5 rebuild.
**Also made:** `farram_bram_v2.v` (far RAM as two 32K-word banks, no cascade), equal to v1 on 5,623 random reads in a standalone test (0 differences); `sc_top_k7_v13.v`, `tb_k7fu5.v`, `test_hubnarrow_v3.py`, `make_mb_v7.py` / `stage_mb_v7.sh` (builds `hf...`), `Makefile.hf16_s2_u1_n1`; the whole-top simulation with it failed once on 2 blocks x 2 slices, but the OLD top failed the same jobs in that run (setup, not the new RAM), and two reruns were killed by workspace restarts: **not simulated**. Build files `hd2_s16_u1_n1`, `hd4_s16_u1_n1`, `hd16_s4_u1_n1`, `hd16_s8_u1_n1`, `run_vivado_v4.tcl`, `run_vivado_v5.tcl`, `far_ram_check_v1.py`, `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json`.
**Corrections in this entry:** my statement that the 16x4 might have been "clean" of the DRC was wrong (the log count could not show it); my first reading of "status 8" as a sequencer error was wrong (the sequencer reported no error; the job status words were 8).
**Not done:** the cause of the Vivado 8x4's wrong answers; any Vivado bitstream tested beyond the 8x4; simulation of 12-, 16-block, `hf` or slim-link configurations; the slimmer far-RAM link (designed, not built: 8 beats of 8 bits would cut the per-block wires from 55 to about 9, an arithmetic estimate); the two-arbiter / second-port idea; the 2x16 and 4x16 builds (files made, not run).

## #1476 (25 Nov) -- Vivado-built 8x4 still fails the job test after the duplicate-module fix (v5); the same single job passes on the open-tool bitstream; cause not found

**Measured on the card (single job `ladder_1_one_job.json`, vsum of [1, 2, 3, 4], expected [10], `--blocks 8 --lw 0`):** open-tool bitstream `keep_hd8_seed1_ok.bit`: `[10]`, 1 of 1 correct, 9,047 clocks. Vivado v4 bitstream: card `[8]`, status 8, n_out 8, 6,796 clocks. Vivado v5 bitstream: card `[8]`, status 8, n_out 8, 6,795 clocks (loaded and confirmed by Alan). The v5 eight-job run: 0 of 8 (every job status 8, n_out 1, card `[8]`, about 12,800-13,500 clocks per block). So the card, cable, job file and test setup are fine; the Vivado netlist does not give the right answers, and v5 behaves like v4.
**Measured, v5 build (`run_vivado_v5.tcl`, reads only the Makefile's ADDITIONAL_SOURCES):** 0 "Synth 8-9873" duplicate-module warnings (v4: 21); bitgen 0 critical warnings; timing WNS +1.071 ns, WHS +0.047 ns, 0 failing endpoints (134,237 endpoints), all constraints met. Warning types in the v5 log: Synth 8-7129 x85, 8-6014 x37, 8-6901 x5, 8-3936 x5, 8-4767 x2 (v4 also had 8-8895 x60 and 8-9873 x21; those came from reading every file in `rtl/`, including `sc_top_k7_v1.sv`, which the build does not list).
**Read from the log lines:** 8-4767: two RAMs built from registers (`t_state_reg`, sc_sys_v17.sv:879, and an unnamed one at :935). 8-3936: width trims on `s_shift_reg`/`s_sel_reg`/`s_tdi_reg` (3 to 2 bits, jtag_link_v2), `w3_reg` (32 to 16) and `w0_reg` (32 to 4) in batch_seq_v2; not checked whether these are only unused upper bits.
**Correction:** I said the eight-job `n_out` of 1 under v5 showed the netlist had changed; that compared it with the v4 SINGLE-job run (n_out 8) and was not like for like. The single-job v4 and v5 results are the same, so the duplicate definitions did not matter, as first suspected before I called them the likely cause. Earlier in the same session I also told Alan the duplicate overwrite was "my current suspect"; it is ruled out as the cause (not as a warning).
**Lead, a guess:** `HDR = 8` in `jobio.py` (header size; the first header word `in_off` is also 8). Status 8, output 8 (and n_out 8 in the single-job runs) equal that number, whatever the job does (sum or max). That is consistent with the host reading header or unchanged words back, i.e. the job not running, or its results not reaching the read-out. The Vivado builds also run about 2,250 clocks (25 %) less than the open-tool one. Neither point is confirmed.
**Candidates not checked:** `sc_sys_v17.v` declares `imem_sh` with `ram_style = "distributed"` (line 251) and `shd` with `ram_style = "block"` (line 265); Vivado honours these and the open flow may map them differently; read-during-write behaviour of inferred RAMs; start-up values (`initial` blocks). The first run to do next: a Vivado post-synthesis functional simulation, or reading back the block's shared RAM window after a job.
**Consequence:** every Vivado timing result so far (8x4, 12x2, 16x2, 16x4, 16x8) shows that the design routes and meets 50 MHz, not that it works. The 12x2, 16x2, 16x4 and 16x8 were also built from every file in `rtl/` (v2/v3/v4); they are not rerun with v5.
**Not done:** the cause; the slimmer far-RAM link (parked until the Vivado result is understood); the 2x16 and 4x16 builds; the warning catalogue of the 8x4 beyond the lines above; whole-top simulation of 12/16-block configurations.

## #1477 (25 Nov) -- the Vivado-built 8x4's shared RAM stores data wrongly through the host path (887 of 1,024 words wrong; open-tool build 0); far RAM identical except the 3 result words; negedge-free clone (sc_sys_v18, build `hg`) made, simulated at pool level, not yet built or tried on the card

**Measured on the card, single job (`ladder_1_one_job.json`, vsum [1, 2, 3, 4], expected [10]), `--blocks 8 --lw 0`:** `far_dump_v1.py` (read-only, 0x2000 + 128 words) after the job: the open-tool and Vivado v5 dumps are identical except three words, 0x2063/0x2064/0x2065 (status, n_out, result): open 1, 1, 10; Vivado 8, 8, 8. The code image and job data in the far RAM are identical, and the far RAM itself reads back correctly on both bitstreams (#1475).
**Measured, new `shared_ram_check_v1.py` (host commands M write / L read on block 0's shared RAM):** `pattern` mode, 256 words, four patterns: open-tool bitstream 0 wrong words (RESULT: shared RAM host path OK); Vivado v5 bitstream 255 / 250 / 254 / 128 wrong (887 total), with data bits 0-31 all affected (low byte most). The `dump` taken after the job shows, in the Vivado build, mostly 8s where the open-tool build shows the job window (word 1 = 4, word 2 = 12, inputs 1 2 3 4 at words 8-11, answer 10 at word 12). So the job's failure is the shared RAM (block RAM `shd`) not holding or returning data in the Vivado netlist; it is not (as I first thought) only the copy-out. The same single-job run also repeated the earlier clock count (Vivado 6,795-6,796 clocks; open tool 9,046-9,049).
**Read, not measured:** examples such as pattern C word 3 written 0xfffffffc read 0xfffffff3 (two bit pairs swapped) and pattern B reading 0x1000 on even words and 1 on odd words look like address and bit lanes mixed, but the pattern was not worked out; that the RAM is "not built as the RTL describes" is my guess.
**What I looked at:** `sc_sys_v17.v` lines 251 and 265 mark `imem_sh` distributed and `shd` block; lines 296-299 and 580-590 put the RAM address/data/write-enable inputs through falling-edge registers (`p_adr_n`, `p_we_n`, `p_wd_n`, `rf_aa_n`, `dm_a_n`, `rflush_n`, `dflush_n`, `rnext_n`, `dnext_n`) "as a hold-time measure". The Vivado warning `Synth 8-6901` ("identifier used before its declaration", e.g. `slice_busy` at sc_sys_v17.sv:694) I ruled out as a cause (Vivado would have refused a second declaration). The Vivado log's mapping of `shd` was not yet read (Alan was asked for `grep -n "shd|p_adr_n|imem_sh"` of the v5 log and the RAMB/RAM line counts of `utilization_routed.txt`).
**Made (clones, nothing proven edited):** `sc_sys_v18.v` (v17 with every falling-edge register in front of the block RAMs replaced by a direct wire; in a zero-delay view neutral, timing differs), `tb_sys18.v`, `test_sys18.py`, `fpga/sc_block_v4.v`, `fpga/sc_top_k7_v14.v`, `k7/make_mb_v8.py`, `k7/stage_mb_v8.sh`, `k7/Makefile.hg8_s4_u1_n1`, `k7/sc_k7_hg8s4u1n1.v`. **Simulated:** `python3 test_sys18.py 4 2 0 1` (pool level, S=4, FZ=0, NU=1, all program widths and the mixed batches) RESULT PASS in 2 min. **Not simulated:** the whole top (`sc_top_k7_v14`; elaborates in iverilog except the Xilinx `BSCANE2` primitive, which the old testbenches replace); not built; not on the card. It is an experiment: if the Vivado build with it gives [10], the falling-edge registers were what Vivado mishandled; if not, they are ruled out.
**Also made this session:** `far_dump_v1.py`, `shared_ram_check_v1.py`.
**Not done:** the cause; the grep of the Vivado log for how `shd` was mapped; the 2x16/4x16 Vivado runs; the sub-arbiter grouping idea (Alan, 25 Nov: groups of 4 blocks with a small arbiter each, then one top arbiter; wires at the top for 16 blocks 912 -> about 228, with the 8-bit slim link about 36; not built, not simulated, the claim that wire count causes the open-tool failures is unproven); the slim link.

## #1478 (25 Nov) -- the Vivado-built 8x4 WORKS once the falling-edge registers in front of the block RAMs are removed (build `hg8s4u1n1`, sc_sys_v18): shared RAM clean, single job [10] in 9,046 clocks

**Measured on the card, Vivado `hg8s4u1n1` (v5 script, reads only the Makefile's files; 0 duplicate-module warnings; setup slack +5.188 ns, hold slack +0.029 ns, 0 failing endpoints, 132,903 endpoints; bitstream written 15:33):**
`shared_ram_first_v1.py` straight after the load: reads of words 5, 7, 0, 5, 0 gave B C A B A (healthy; the Vivado `hd8s4u1n1` bitstream gave A A A A A on the same test). `shared_ram_check_v1.py pattern 0 0 256`: 0 of 256 wrong on all four patterns, "RESULT: shared RAM host path OK" (the `hd8s4u1n1` bitstream: 887 wrong words). `ladder_1_one_job.json` (vsum [1, 2, 3, 4]): 1 of 1 correct, 9,046 clocks (open-tool bitstream: 9,046-9,049; `hd8s4u1n1`: 6,795 clocks and [8]).
**Measured before that (same day), the failing `hd8s4u1n1`:** `shared_ram_spaced_v1.py` (one request at a time, 20 ms pauses): 63 of 64 words wrong, all 63 returned word 0's value, so the fault is not dropped back-to-back requests; `shared_ram_first_v1.py`: A A A A A, so it is not "the read data updates only once"; a write of C to word 7 after A to word 0 did not overwrite A, so it is not a stuck address on writes either.
**Conclusion (what the measurements support):** the only difference between `hd8s4u1n1` and `hg8s4u1n1` is `sc_sys_v18.v` (every falling-edge register in front of the block RAMs replaced by a direct wire); the first fails the shared-RAM tests and every job, the second passes the shared-RAM tests and the single job. So the falling-edge address/data/write-enable registers (`p_adr_n`, `p_we_n`, `p_wd_n` and the register-file and data-window ones) are what the Vivado netlist got wrong. The `HDR = 8` lead of #1476 is explained: every read returned word 0 of the job window, which is `in_off` = 8.
**Read / reasoned, NOT shown:** WHY Vivado's netlist misbehaves with them. My guess, from the log, was one extra or one missing clock of read latency that makes the host pick up the data of the idle slot's address 0; `inspect_shd_v1.tcl` (untested, written for this) was not run and is no longer needed to proceed. In the RTL view the two versions are the same (`test_sys18.py 4 2 0 1` passed), so this is a difference between what the RTL says and what the Vivado netlist does, not a logic error in v17. The falling-edge registers were put in "as a hold-time measure" for the open flow: **the open-tool builds must keep sc_sys_v17**; v18 is the Vivado line.
**Consequences:** (1) every earlier Vivado result (8x4 `hd`, 12x2, 16x2, 16x4, 16x8) was for a netlist that fails the job test; their timing figures mean "routes and meets 50 MHz", not "works"; the Vivado runs of 12x2, 16x2, 16x4 and 16x8 should be redone with the `hg` files (`make_mb_v8.py NB S 0 1`). (2) The open-tool 8x4 (v17) is still the only fully tested build: 8 of 8 jobs on 8 blocks (#1475). (3) `hg8s4u1n1` has had ONE job on block 0 tested; the eight-job, all-blocks run is not done yet.
**Not done:** `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json` on `hg8s4u1n1`; Vivado `hg` builds of the larger shapes (and 2x16, 4x16 as requested earlier); the cause of the difference between the RTL and the Vivado netlist; the sub-arbiter grouping (Alan, 25 Nov); the slimmer far-RAM link.

## #1479 (25 Nov) -- Vivado `hg8s4u1n1`: all 8 blocks run correctly on the card (8 of 8 jobs); the Vivado line now works at 8x4

**Measured on the card, Vivado `hg8s4u1n1` (sc_sys_v18; setup +5.188 ns, hold +0.029 ns), `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json`, `--blocks 8 --lw 0`:** 8 of 8 jobs correct, one per block, answers 1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480 (all match); per-block clocks 85,152 / 66,599 / 71,094 / 57,879 / 57,041 / 49,159 / 40,438 / 42,985; all 8 blocks finished in 0.14 s. The same job set failed 0 of 8 on `hd8s4u1n1` (#1476). The clock counts were not compared with the open-tool 8-job run (that one was a different job set, #1475).
**What this settles:** the Vivado netlist is functionally right at 8x4 once the falling-edge RAM-input registers are removed (#1478); the host link, far RAM, arbiter and all eight blocks work in a Vivado-built bitstream. Not settled: why the registers break the Vivado netlist; whether other shapes behave (only 8x4 is tested).
**Next (Alan's plan, 25 Nov):** Vivado `hg` builds of 2x16 and 4x16 (`make_mb_v8.py 2 16 0 1`, `4 16 0 1`; fewer blocks, more slices per block), then larger shapes; the far-RAM DRC REQP-1962 (cause unknown, trips at 12 and 16 blocks) still stops 12/16-block builds with the check ON. Whole-top simulation of these shapes is not done (rule #1466 departed from knowingly, as for the earlier exploration builds).

## #1480 (25 Nov) -- Vivado `hg2s16u1n1` and `hg4s16u1n1` stop at the far-RAM DRC REQP-1962 before placement (3 and 6 errors); two-bank far RAM combined with sc_sys_v18 (build `hh`), not yet built

**Measured (Alan's logs, 25 Nov, `run_vivado_v5.tcl`, check ON):** both builds stop at `place_design` with "DRC finished with 3 Errors" (2x16: cascaded pairs `u/ctl/mem_reg_{0,1}_{4,15,23}`) and "6 Errors" (4x16: `mem_reg_{0,1}_{2,7,12,17,22,27}`), "Placer not run". So REQP-1962 also trips at 2 and 4 blocks: it is not tied to the block count as #1474 suggested (12 and 16 blocks tripped, 8 blocks did not). Which pairs are flagged varies between builds (12x2, 16x2 and 4x16 flag 2, 7, 12, 17, 22, 27; 2x16 flags 4, 15, 23; 16x4 had 2 errors). The cause is still unknown; the warning that lowering the check "can lead to board damage or other functionality issues" is why it stays on.
**Read:** `hg8s4u1n1` (8x4) passes the same check with farram_bram_v1, so the check depends on the build, not only on the far RAM file.
**Made (clones, nothing proven edited):** `fpga/sc_top_k7_v15.v` (v14 with `farram_bram_v2`: the far RAM as two 32K-word banks, no cascaded RAMB36 pairs, same ports and latency; equal to v1 on 5,623 random reads in a standalone test, #1475), `k7/make_mb_v9.py`, `k7/stage_mb_v9.sh`, `Makefile.hh8_s4_u1_n1`, `Makefile.hh2_s16_u1_n1`, `Makefile.hh4_s16_u1_n1` with their `sc_k7_hh...v` wrappers (the files are named `hh`: sc_sys_v18 + sc_block_v4 + sc_top_k7_v15 + farram_bram_v2). **Not simulated as a whole top, not built, not run on the card.** `farram_bram_v2` has never run on the card; the plan is to build `hh8s4u1n1` first, and check it with the single job, the eight-job test and `far_ram_check_v1.py` (including the upper half, 0x8000 and above) before the 2x16 and 4x16.
**Not done:** the cause of REQP-1962; builds of `hh` shapes; the upper half of the far RAM has still not been tested on any card build.

## #1481 (25 Nov) -- Vivado `hh8s4u1n1` (v18 dispatcher + two-bank far RAM) passes on the card: far RAM clean in both banks, 8 of 8 jobs on 8 blocks; the REQP-1962 check no longer applies to it

**Measured, build:** `hh8s4u1n1` through `run_vivado_v5.tcl` (check ON, reads only the Makefile's files), setup slack +6.095 ns, hold slack +0.061 ns, 0 failing endpoints (132,739 endpoints); bitstream written and loaded (SRAM only).
**Measured on the card:** `far_ram_check_v1.py jtag 0x2000 512` and `jtag 0x8000 512`: 0 of 512 wrong on all four patterns in both runs ("far RAM stores and returns every pattern correctly"). The 0x8000 run is the first test of the upper bank (`mem_hi`) on any card build; it covered 512 words, not the whole 64K-word range. `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json --blocks 8 --lw 0`: 8 of 8 jobs correct, answers 1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480; per-block clocks 85,153 / 66,600 / 71,094 / 57,881 / 57,041 / 49,159 / 40,441 / 42,982 (the `hg8s4u1n1` run: 85,152 / 66,599 / 71,094 / 57,879 / 57,041 / 49,159 / 40,438 / 42,985, so the two-bank far RAM costs no measurable time).
**What this settles:** the two-bank far RAM works on the card at 8x4 in both banks (spot-tested), together with the v18 dispatcher. Not settled: whether it clears the REQP-1962 check at 2x16, 4x16 and the larger shapes (the check is about cascaded RAMB36 pairs, which v2 does not have, so I expect so; not shown); the full 64K-word range of the far RAM; why the cascade check trips in some builds and not others.
**Next:** `hh2s16u1n1` and `hh4s16u1n1` builds (files already in the repo, #1480), then the larger shapes.

## #1482 (25 Nov) -- Vivado `hh2s16u1n1` and `hh4s16u1n1` (two-bank far RAM) pass the far-RAM DRC, place, route and meet 50 MHz; not yet loaded on the card

**Measured (Alan's pasted timing summaries, 25 Nov, `run_vivado_v5.tcl`, check ON; the paste gave two summaries in order without file names, matched to the builds by endpoint count: 67,361 endpoints = the smaller design, 2x16, and 128,580 = 4x16 (inference; `hh2s16u1n1`'s own summary re-grepped afterwards matches the first)):** `hh2s16u1n1`: setup slack +3.525 ns, hold slack +0.052 ns, 0 failing endpoints (67,361). `hh4s16u1n1`: setup +1.570 ns, hold +0.045 ns, 0 failing endpoints (128,580). 0 duplicate-module warnings for `hh2s16u1n1` (checked). During the 2x16 route the node overlaps fell 6,876 -> 419 -> 88 -> 26 -> 4 -> 2 -> 0 in the first global iteration (intermediate WNS +3.424 ns); not compared with the earlier builds' overlap counts.
**What this settles:** the far-RAM DRC (REQP-1962) that stopped `hg2s16u1n1` and `hg4s16u1n1` does not stop the two-bank builds at 2x16 and 4x16, and both route and meet timing in Vivado (slack, not Fmax).
**Not done:** loading them on the card; the single job, the job tests and the far-RAM checks at 2 and 4 blocks; the duplicate-module count for `hh4s16u1n1`; the 16-block and 12-block shapes; the Vivado builds at 8x8 and larger.

## #1483 (25 Nov) -- Vivado `hh2s16u1n1` runs correctly on the card: 2 blocks of 16 slices, single job [10] and 8 of 8 jobs; `hh4s16u1n1` timing and duplicate count confirmed as its own

**Measured on the card, `hh2s16u1n1` (loaded SRAM only; board reports 16 slices per block, 128 code words, 256 shared words):** `ladder_1_one_job.json --blocks 2 --lw 0`: 1 of 1 correct, 9,206 clocks (the 8x4 builds took 9,046-9,049 for the same job; not explained, 16 slices per block instead of 4). `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json --blocks 2 --lw 0`: 8 of 8 jobs correct, answers 1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480; 4 jobs per block in 2 batches each (block 0: jobs 1 2 4 7, 131,177 clocks, 32 steps; block 1: jobs 0 3 5 6, 114,716 clocks, 32 steps); both blocks finished in 0.09 s.
**Measured (Alan's grep of the `hh4s16u1n1` files):** setup +1.570 ns, hold +0.045 ns, 128,580 endpoints, 0 failing: confirms that the second timing summary of #1482 belongs to the 4x16 (my inference there was right); `Synth 8-9873` count 0.
**What this settles:** a Vivado-built bitstream with fewer, wider blocks (2 x 16 slices) works on the card, with the two-bank far RAM, the v18 dispatcher and several jobs per block. Not settled: the 4x16 on the card; clocks per job at different slice counts (not a like-for-like comparison here); the far RAM above the tested ranges.
**Next:** load `hh4s16u1n1` and run the same three tests (single job, eight jobs on 4 blocks, far RAM check).

## #1484 (26 Nov) -- Vivado `hh4s16u1n1` (4 blocks x 16 slices) works on the card: single job [10], 8 of 8 jobs, far RAM clean at 0x8000

**Measured on the card, `hh4s16u1n1` (SRAM only; board reports 16 slices per block):** `ladder_1_one_job.json --blocks 4 --lw 0`: 1 of 1 correct, 9,209 clocks (`hh2s16u1n1`: 9,206). `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json --blocks 4 --lw 0`: 8 of 8 jobs correct, 2 per block (block 0: jobs 1 7, 87,035 clocks; block 1: jobs 0 6, 68,570; block 2: jobs 2 5, 72,678; block 3: jobs 3 4, 59,657; all finished in 0.10 s); answers 1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480 all match. `far_ram_check_v1.py jtag 0x8000 512`: 0 of 512 wrong, all four patterns.
**Measured timing of the three working Vivado builds (setup / hold slack at 50 MHz):** `hh8s4u1n1` +6.095 / +0.061 ns (132,739 endpoints); `hh2s16u1n1` +3.525 / +0.052 (67,361); `hh4s16u1n1` +1.570 / +0.045 (128,580). Slack falls as the design grows, but the three shapes differ in both blocks and slices per block, so the effect of each is not separated.
**What this settles:** the Vivado line (v18 dispatcher + two-bank far RAM, `hh` files) works on the card at 8x4, 2x16 and 4x16, each with the single job and the eight-job test. Not settled: shapes larger than these (8x8, 8x16, 16x16, 12 or 16 blocks); the whole 64K-word far RAM; behaviour in the open tools of the wide shapes (not built).
**Next:** read the Vivado LUT counts of the three builds (`utilization_routed.txt`) to fit a size model in Vivado units, then choose the next shape (8x8, 8x16 or more blocks) before building.

## #1485 (26 Nov) -- Vivado size model from three working builds (LUT = 945 + 3,172 x blocks + 682 x total slices); it reproduces the earlier 16x4 and 16x8 within 0.7 %; `hh8s8u1n1` and `hh8s16u1n1` build files made

**Measured (Alan's `utilization_routed.txt` greps, Vivado units, 298,600 LUTs and 597,200 registers on the XC7K480T):** `hh8s4u1n1`: 48,153 LUTs (16.13 %; 44,601 logic + 3,552 memory), 38,847 registers (6.50 %); `hh2s16u1n1`: 29,118 LUTs (9.75 %; 28,154 + 964), 21,904 registers (3.67 %); `hh4s16u1n1`: 57,291 LUTs (19.19 %; 55,459 + 1,832), 42,398 registers (7.10 %).
**Computed (a fit through those three points, solving LUT = a + b x blocks + c x total slices; arithmetic, not a measurement of other shapes):** a = 945, b = 3,172.5 LUTs per block, c = 682.1 LUTs per slice. Check against two builds NOT used in the fit (the Vivado 16x4 and 16x8 of #1475, 32 % and 46.8 %, before the falling-edge fix, same logic size): fit 95,361 vs about 95,552, and 139,017 vs about 139,745 (within 0.7 %). Registers, same method: about 1,408 + 2,824 per block + 464 per slice (not used further; 6-7 % so far).
**Predictions, computed with that fit, not built:** 8x8 (64 slices): 69,981 LUTs, 23.4 %; 8x16 (128 slices): 113,637, 38.1 %; 12x8: 104,499, 35.0 %; 16x8: 139,017, 46.6 %; 16x16 (256 slices): 226,329, 75.8 %; 8x32 (256 slices): 200,949, 67.3 % (whether a block of 32 slices is supported by the RTL has not been checked). The LUT count says the shape fits; it does not say it routes or meets 50 MHz: setup slack fell from +6.095 ns (8x4) to +1.570 ns (4x16) as the builds grew.
**Made:** `Makefile.hh8_s8_u1_n1`, `sc_k7_hh8s8u1n1.v`, `Makefile.hh8_s16_u1_n1`, `sc_k7_hh8s16u1n1.v` (generated by `make_mb_v9.py`); not built, not simulated as whole tops.
**Not done:** Vivado builds of 8x8 and 8x16; block-RAM counts for the wide shapes (the 8x4 uses 105 RAMB18 + 64 RAMB36; the per-slice share was not measured).

## #1486 (26 Nov) -- two draft documents (build/load/check guide; manual gap list and scale-test plan); correction: the card has DDR3; the `hh` builds have no fusion unit

**Made (drafts, written from the ledger; neither has been read through by Alan):** `s-core/K7_BUILDS_AND_LOADING_v1.md` (the two build lines, how to build with Vivado, the loader command, the card checks in order, measured results of the three working builds, limits) and `s-core/USAGE_MANUAL_AND_SCALE_TESTS_PLAN_v1.md` (what S-Core documents exist, the gaps, the proposed chapters of a usage manual, the open items, and eight scale tests T1-T8 for load, retrieve and reload; **none of the tests has been written or run**). Alan asked for both (26 Nov), and said that, if no usage manual exists, it becomes the next target once the slice-per-block builds are done; none exists as one piece (README, LADDER, PROGRAMS, BATCH_FORMAT, SHELL are partial). `docs/build_manual.py` does not list any S-Core page, so `docs/manual.html` was not rebuilt.
**Correction of #1481-#1485 answers to Alan:** I told him (26 Nov, when he asked about expanding the far RAM) that I had not looked whether the card has external memory. `s-core/README.md` line 7 and ledger #1338 say the card's DDR3 was used as the far RAM with the open flow (UberDDR3, GPL-3.0, channel 0, 4-slice build, telemetry 24 of 24, 10 clean reloads): so a larger far RAM could come from the DDR3 as well as from more block-RAM banks. Not tried with the `hh` builds or Vivado.
**Read, new:** the `hh` builds are unit-less (`make_mb_v9.py ... 0 1`: FZ 0, NU 1, the unit declared and never bound), so they run width 1 only; the measured `--lw 0` tests do not test fusion. Fusion on the Vivado line is untried. Also: the falling-edge RAM-input registers that fail in Vivado were introduced for the Tang Nano's block RAM (README versions table, v6: "the only block-RAM shape that works on this board, falling-edge RAM inputs"), which is why the open-tool builds keep them.
**Not done:** the 8x8 and 8x16 results (Vivado running at the time of writing); a fusion build in Vivado; any of T1-T8; reading `PROGRAMS.md` and `SHELL.md` against these builds.

## #1487 (26 Nov) -- Vivado `hh8s8u1n1` (64 slices) and `hh8s16u1n1` (128 slices) work on the card: first-read test healthy, single job [10], 8 of 8 jobs on 8 blocks; LUT fit refitted on five builds

**Measured, builds (Alan's greps, 26 Nov; both matched to their files by his `for` loop; 0 `Synth 8-9873`; bitstreams written 01:29 and 02:00):** `hh8s8u1n1`: setup +3.938 ns, hold +0.027 ns, 171,865 endpoints, 0 failing; 68,451 LUTs (22.92 %), 53,567 registers (8.97 %). `hh8s16u1n1`: setup +1.423 ns, hold +0.025 ns, 251,410 endpoints, 0 failing; 113,615 LUTs (38.05 %), 83,375 registers (13.96 %). The size fit of #1485 had predicted 69,981 and 113,637 LUTs (8x8 2.2 % high, 8x16 0.02 % high).
**Measured on the card, both builds (SRAM only):** `shared_ram_first_v1.py jtag 0` straight after the load: B C A B A (healthy). `ladder_1_one_job.json --blocks 8 --lw 0`: 1 of 1 correct, 9,109 clocks (8 slices per block) and 9,207 clocks (16 slices per block). `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json --blocks 8 --lw 0`: 8 of 8 jobs correct on both, answers 1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480; per-block clocks 8x8: 85,490 / 67,187 / 71,385 / 58,384 / 57,271 / 49,582 / 40,786 / 43,158; 8x16: 86,204 / 67,816 / 71,993 / 58,938 / 57,759 / 50,055 / 41,183 / 43,537. All blocks finished in 0.12 s. The far RAM and the shared RAM beyond block 0 were not tested on these two builds.
**Measured, single-job clocks by slices per block (the same job, all Vivado `hh` builds):** 4: 9,046; 8: 9,109; 16: 9,206 to 9,209 (about 1.8 % from 4 to 16). **Read, not measured as the cause:** the shared code store gives each slice a turn in rotation (`sc_sys_v17.v`: "a slice waits at most S-1 clocks for its turn").
**Computed, refit of the Vivado size model on five builds (least squares over 8x4, 2x16, 4x16, 8x8, 8x16):** LUT = 1,120 + 3,055 x blocks + 686 x total slices; every build within 1.5 % (largest error 8x4 -1.4 %, 8x8 +1.4 %). Predictions (not built): 12x8 103,593 (34.7 %), 12x16 169,408 (56.7 %), 16x8 137,750 (46.1 %), 16x16 225,504 (75.5 %), 8x32 201,065 (67.3 %; whether 32 slices per block is supported by the RTL was not checked).
**What this settles:** the Vivado `hh` line works on the card at five shapes (8x4, 2x16, 4x16, 8x8, 8x16: up to 128 slices, 8 blocks, 38 % of the LUTs) with a single job and the eight-job test. Setup slack falls with size (+6.095, +3.525, +1.570, +3.938, +1.423 ns for 8x4, 2x16, 4x16, 8x8, 8x16). Not settled: 12 and 16 blocks in the `hh` line; fusion; the scale tests (#1486).
**Alan's remark (26 Nov), 16x8 uses more LUTs than 8x16 for the same slices, may link to the failures:** by the fit each block costs about 3,055 LUTs (about 4.5 slices); the open-tool failures (#1473) were the many-block, few-slice builds (16x2, 12x2); a test would be an open-tool build of `hd4s16u1n1` (4 blocks, 64 slices, more LUTs than 12x2 or 16x2). A pattern, not shown.
**Not done:** that open-tool build; the per-module LUT report (`report_utilization -hierarchical`, given to Alan, not run yet); 12- and 16-block `hh` builds; fusion in Vivado.

## #1488 (26 Nov) -- Per-module LUT report of `hh8s4u1n1` (routed checkpoint, depth 4): each block is its own full copy, about 5,120 LUTs, 87 % of it the `sys` core; no sharing between blocks

**Measured (Alan ran `report_utilization -hierarchical -hierarchical_depth 4` on `vivado_out_hh8s4u1n1/routed.dcp`, 26 Nov; I saw only the lines his grep kept: the first six blocks, and only the rows named sc_block, sys, bs, fc).** Columns read as Vivado's usual order (total LUTs, logic LUTs, LUTRAM, SRL, FFs, RAMB36, RAMB18); the header row was not in the paste, so that order is read, not seen.
- Each `blocks[n].b` (`sc_block_v4`) has its own `sys` (`sc_sys_v18`), `bs` (`batch_seq_v2`) and `fc` (`far_copy`) with separate module copies (`sc_sys_v18_68`, `_61`, `_54` ...). Vivado did not merge the blocks into one.
- Block total: 5,121 to 5,123 LUTs in blocks 0, 1, 4, 5; 5,482 in block 2; 5,559 in block 3. Every block: 4,201 registers (4,200 in blocks 2, 3), 13 RAMB18, 432 LUTRAM.
- `sys`: 4,443 to 4,446 LUTs in every block (about 87 % of a block); of that the `sys` top row alone is 2,447 to 2,451 LUTs and 2,691 registers. The remaining ~2,000 LUTs of `sys` sit in lower modules (the slices) that his grep did not print.
- `bs`: 449 to 450 LUTs in blocks 0, 1, 4, 5 but 803 and 802 in blocks 2 and 3. `fc`: 238 LUTs, except 315 in block 3. These two account for the +360 and +438 LUTs of blocks 2 and 3. **Cause not known** (the RTL for the blocks is the same file; Vivado optimised these copies differently).
**Computed, against the size fit of #1487:** 8 x 5,121 = 40,968 LUTs in blocks; the build total is 48,153, so about 7,200 LUTs are outside the blocks (far RAM, arbiter links, host side). Per block, the fit's 3,055 LUTs is roughly the block's fixed part (5,121 minus 4 slices at 686, about 2,380) plus a share of that outside part (about 760 per block). A reading of the numbers, not a separate measurement.
**Not done:** the rows for the slices and for blocks 6 and 7 (grep too narrow); the same report on a Vivado build with more slices per block, to separate the per-slice cost from the per-block cost.

## #1489 (26 Nov) -- Correction to #1488: the per-block cost outside `sc_block` is only about 62 LUTs; the ~5,000 LUTs outside the blocks are not located yet; no slice rows exist at depth 4

**Measured (Alan's second grep, same report):** `blocks[0].cm` (`blk_cmd_v1`): 0 LUTs, 374 registers; `blocks[0].rd` (`blk_rd_v2`): 62 LUTs, 94 registers; blocks[7] the same (1 and 62 LUTs). `blocks[7].b` is 5,485 LUTs (4,200 registers), so blocks 2, 3 and 7 are the larger copies (5,482, 5,559, 5,485); block 6 not seen. The grep for `slice`, `sub_arb`, `arb` matched no row: at depth 4 the slices do not appear as separate rows (named otherwise or inside `sys`; not checked).
**Computed, corrects #1488:** the seven blocks seen sum to 37,012 LUTs; block 6 unseen (5,121 to 5,560), plus 8 x 62 for `rd`, gives about 42,600 to 43,100 in blocks and their per-block command/read parts, so about 5,000 to 5,500 LUTs of the 48,153 sit elsewhere (not 7,200 as written in #1488, which assumed all blocks at 5,121). What that remainder is (far RAM, arbiters, host side, top glue) is **not measured**. #1488's reading that the fit's per-block 3,055 includes a ~760 share of outside logic is an allocation of the fit, not shown by the report; per-block logic outside `sc_block` is only about 62 LUTs. Whether the outside part grows with the block count, and so whether the sub-arbiter grouping would cut LUTs, is therefore **not supported yet**; its case rests on wires and routing (#1478 estimate), not on this report.
**Next to look at:** the top rows of `utilization_hier.txt` (depth 1 and 2, non-block modules).

## #1490 (26 Nov) -- Per-module report of `hh8s4u1n1`, third grep: slices are `uc_core_v2v` at about 495 LUTs each; `jtag_link_v2` is 940 LUTs; about 4,000 to 4,500 LUTs still not located

**Measured (Alan's third grep of `utilization_hier.txt`, output cut at 40 lines by `head`):** whole design 48,153 LUTs, 38,847 registers, 64 RAMB36, 105 RAMB18 (matches the build). `u` (`sc_top_k7_v15`) holds all of it. `jl` (`jtag_link_v2`): 940 LUTs, 1,166 registers, 1 RAMB18. Twelve `slot[n].core` rows (`uc_core_v2v`, 151 registers, no RAM) of 491 to 519 LUTs each; they appear to be the slices (4 per block, so 32 in the build), but the paste does not show which block they belong to (their position in the file and indentation suggest under each block's `sys`; read, not checked).
**Computed:** slice average about 498 LUTs; 4 slices about 2,000 LUTs, which matches the ~2,000 LUTs of `sys` not in its own top row (#1488). The size fit's 686 LUTs per slice is therefore about 190 above the slice core itself; the difference is slice-related logic elsewhere (not located). With `jl` (940), blocks and per-block read parts (about 42,600 to 43,100, #1489), about 4,100 to 4,500 LUTs of the total are still not placed in a named module (the output was cut off).
**Not done:** the rows of the top-level modules other than `jl` (far RAM, arbiters, dispatch, host side); `head -40` cut them off.

## #1491 (26 Nov) -- `hh8s4u1n1` hierarchy, top-level rows: `host_bridge_v13` (`br`) is 4,141 LUTs with 255 registers; with `jl` (940) and `ctl` (84) this places the ~5,000 LUTs left over in #1489

**Measured (Alan's fourth grep, same routed checkpoint):** `jl` (`jtag_link_v2`) 940 LUTs, 1,166 registers, 1 RAMB18; `br` (`host_bridge_v13`) **4,141 LUTs (all logic), 255 registers, no RAM**; `ctl` (`farram_bram_v2`) 84 LUTs, 40 registers, 64 RAMB36. No row for `far_arb` or for the block-selection muxes appeared (the `far_arb` grep of #1488 matched nothing; no `(u)` row seen).
**Computed:** jl + br + ctl = 5,165 LUTs; with the blocks and per-block read parts (about 42,600 to 43,100, #1489) the total is 47,800 to 48,300, which matches the build's 48,153. So the LUTs are now accounted for by named rows; the open point is what `br` holds.
**Read (source `s-core/fpga/host_bridge_v13.v`, 240 lines):** a small state machine: a 16 x 8 argument array, a few 32-bit and 10-bit registers, a command `case`, one byte-select function. It takes the selected block's one status byte (`rd_byte`) and one `dh_q` word as single inputs. That reads as a few hundred LUTs, not 4,141 (my reading of the size, not a measurement). The top (`s-core/fpga/sc_top_k7_v15.v`) contains the block-selection muxes and the far-RAM arbiter wiring.
**Hypothesis, not tested:** Vivado counted the top's block-selection muxes and/or the far-RAM arbiter under `br` (no separate rows for them). If so `br` grows with the block count. **Test (no build needed):** run the same hierarchical report on the routed checkpoints of `hh2s16u1n1` (2 blocks) and `hh8s16u1n1` (8 blocks) and compare `br`; a flat 4,141 would say it is the bridge itself.
**Consequence for the sub-arbiter plan:** unchanged until that test; if `br` scales with blocks, grouping could cut LUTs as well as wires.

## #1492 (26 Nov) -- `host_bridge_v13` LUTs grow with both the block count and the slices per block: 1,431 (2x16), 4,141 (8x4), 5,906 (8x16); the content of `br` is still unknown

**Measured (Alan's reports on the routed checkpoints of `hh2s16u1n1` and `hh8s16u1n1`, 26 Nov, rows `jl`, `br`, `ctl`):** `br` (`host_bridge_v13`): 1,431 LUTs and 208 registers at 2 blocks x 16 slices; 5,906 LUTs and 291 registers at 8 x 16; (4,141 and 255 at 8 x 4, #1491). `jl`: 928 and 950 LUTs (flat, 940 at 8x4). `ctl` (far RAM): 66 and 94 LUTs (84 at 8x4).
**Computed:** per block: 716 LUTs (2x16), 738 (8x16), 518 (8x4); i.e. close to proportional to the block count, and higher with more slices per block (about 18 LUTs per slice per block from the 8x4 to 8x16 pair; two points only). `br` is 5.2 % of the 8x16 build's LUTs (5,906 of 113,615) and 8.6 % of the 8x4 build's.
**What this settles:** #1491's hypothesis that `br` is not just the small state machine holds in the sense that it grows with the number of blocks; the registers also grow (208, 255, 291), although the source of `host_bridge_v13.v` has one per-block register only (`hold_v`, NB bits). So the logic Vivado reports under `br` includes something else that scales per block (read: the top's block-selection and far-RAM request logic is not given its own row; not shown which part it is).
**Not settled:** what exactly those LUTs are. A plain N-to-1 mux of the roughly 100 bits that return from each block (about 55 far-RAM request bits, 32 data bits, one status byte) would, by my rough estimate, cost far fewer than 700 LUTs per block (an estimate, not measured). **Consequence for the sub-arbiter plan:** grouping would cut wires; a saving in these LUTs is not shown by this result, since the cost does not look like a plain mux and its cause is unknown.
**Next to look at:** a count of the LUT cells under `u/br` by name stem in the routed checkpoint (Tcl given to Alan, untested).

## #1493 (26 Nov) -- LUT cells under `u/br` of `hh8s16u1n1` are named after block-side signals (status readback), not the bridge state machine

**Measured (Alan's Tcl, routed checkpoint of `hh8s16u1n1`):** 6,702 LUT cells have names starting `u/br/` (the report says 5,906 LUTs for `br`; the difference is probably two cells sharing one physical LUT, not checked). Name stems by count (digits replaced by N): `tb[N]_i_N` 977; `rs_v[N]_i_N__N` 504; `rs_sl_reg_N_N_N_N_i_N__N` 462; `lb_e[N]_i_N__N` 343; `imem_sh_reg_N_N_N_N_i_N__N` 280; `rg_ce_reg_...` 231; `hp_wd[N]` 224; `fh_wd_r[N]` 224; `lim_b[N]` 163; `lim_l[N]` 154; `pc[N]` 147; `qk_reg_...` 127; `k[N]` 127; `mem_lo_reg_...` 120; `t_code_reg_...` 119; `fa[N]` 119; `rg_dl_reg_...` 112; `left[N]` 112; `state[N]` 111; `lb_v` 98; `slot[N].sl_q` 85; `err[N]` 84; `slot[N].fw_a` 73; `dsave[N]` 69 (list cut at 25 rows).
**Measured (grep of the sources):** `lb_e`, `hp_wd`, `t_code`, `rs_v` occur in `sc_sys_v18.v` (the block's dispatcher); `fh_wd_r` in `fpga/blk_cmd_v1.v`; `lim_b` in `fpga/sc_block_v4.v`; `dsave` in `fpga/host_bridge_v13.v`. `rs_sl_reg`, `imem_sh_reg`, `rg_ce_reg`, `qk_reg`, `mem_lo` matched none of the six files searched (generated names or other files; not searched further). Only `tb` and `dsave` of the listed stems are in the bridge source, and `tb` is the largest group (977).
**Reading, not shown:** most LUTs counted under `br` are named after registers of the blocks, so they look like the readback of block status that feeds the bridge's transmit byte (`tb`), merged by Vivado into `br` (cross-boundary), not the bridge's own state machine. That fits the growth with blocks and with slices per block (#1492). The report's per-module split is therefore an attribution, not a statement of which source module owns the logic; totals are unaffected.
**Possible direction (idea, not built):** a status readout that scans the status serially (shift chain) in place of wide multiplexers would shrink this part, in line with Alan's earlier one-wire streaming idea. Not designed or measured.

## #1494 (26 Nov) -- Grouped far-RAM arbiter (sub-arbiters, groups of 4 blocks): `far_arb_grp_v1`, standalone test passes, yosys size estimate, top clone `sc_top_k7_v16` and build files `hi...`; not simulated as a whole top, not built in Vivado, not on the card

**Why:** Alan, 26 Nov: the 55 request wires per block (valid, write, 21 address, 32 data; counted from `far_arb.v`) go to the top arbiter one set per block; use a shared bus with a select, and the requests are served one at a time anyway. Kintex-7 has no internal tri-state, so the shared bus is built as a register slot per group (a "sub-arbiter") instead of a long mux.
**Built (new files, nothing proven edited):** `s-core/fpga/far_arb_grp_v1.v` (modules `far_sub_v1` and `far_arb_grp_v1`, same ports as `far_arb` plus parameter G): each group of G blocks has a sub-arbiter that takes the lowest-numbered requesting block into ONE register slot, offers it to the top `far_arb` (now NG = ceil(NB/G) requesters), and waits for its acknowledge before taking another; `ack_d` stays one shared 32-bit bus. Priority stays fixed. Read from the design: about one clock more per far-RAM request. `s-core/tb_far_arb_grp.v` (standalone test). `s-core/fpga/sc_top_k7_v16.v` (v15 with the arbiter line swapped and parameter GA = 4), `s-core/k7/make_mb_v10.py` (files named `hi<NB>s<S>u1n1`), `s-core/k7/stage_mb_v10.sh`, and generated `Makefile.hi8_s4_u1_n1` and `sc_k7_hi8s4u1n1.v` for the first build.
**Measured (iverilog, `tb_far_arb_grp.v`: random reads and writes by every block into its own address region, a RAM model with 1 to 3 clock latency and random busy gaps, each read checked against a shadow copy, an acknowledge to a block with nothing pending counted as an error):** PASS with 0 errors for the grouped arbiter at NB/G = 10/4, 4/4, 1/4, 16/4, 7/2, 16/8 (150 operations per block each), and for the flat `far_arb` at 10 and 16 blocks. Run lengths (grouped vs flat): 6,387 vs 6,549 clocks at 10 blocks; 10,271 vs 10,176 at 16 blocks. A deliberately broken copy (slot address taken from block 0 always) FAILED with 621 errors, so the test can fail. Not shown: behaviour under the real far-RAM timing and with the real blocks.
**Measured (yosys `synth_xilinx`, generic mapping, arbiter alone; not Vivado):** NB 8: flat 638 LUTs, 378 MUXF7/F8, 4 registers; grouped (G 4) 519 LUTs, 327 MUXF7/F8, 118 registers. NB 16: flat 786 LUTs, 657 MUXF7/F8, 5 registers; grouped 569 LUTs, 216 MUXF7, 235 registers. So the arbiter alone is a few hundred LUTs either way (small against the ~5,900 LUTs counted under `br`, #1492), and grouping trades registers for LUTs. This is a rough estimate from another tool; the wire and routing effect, which was the point, is not measured by it.
**Checked:** yosys `hierarchy -check` of the whole v16 top with all its files finds every module except the Xilinx primitive BSCANE2 (expected, as for v15).
**Not done:** simulation of the whole top (v14, v15 and v16 were never simulated as a whole top; only the arbiter was here); a Vivado build of `hi8s4u1n1` and bigger (12 and 16 blocks, where the effect should show); any card run; the narrower far-RAM link; the serial status readout idea (#1493). Rule #1466 (simulate before the card) applies: a card run of a `hi` build would be a knowing departure for exploration, Alan's call.
**Build commands for Alan (Vivado, `hi` line):** `S_CORE=$HOME/Imago-Unicell-score/s-core sh stage_mb_v10.sh` and `python3 make_mb_v10.py 8 4 0 1` in `s-core/k7`, then the same Vivado command as for the `hh` builds with `hi8s4u1n1` in place of `hh8s4u1n1`.

## #1495 (26 Nov) -- Vivado `hi8s4u1n1` (grouped arbiter, 8 blocks of 4 slices) vs `hh8s4u1n1`: no saving at 8 blocks (LUTs +175, slack about the same); `br` is 371 LUTs smaller

**Measured (Alan's greps, 26 Nov, 0 `Synth 8-9873`; bitstream not loaded on the card):** `hi8s4u1n1`: setup +5.985 ns, hold +0.029 ns, 132,935 endpoints, 0 failing; 48,328 LUTs (16.18 %), 38,947 registers (6.52 %). `hh8s4u1n1` (#1481): +6.095 / +0.061 ns, 48,153 LUTs, 38,847 registers. Hierarchy rows of `hi8s4u1n1`: `jl` 940 LUTs, `br` 3,770 LUTs (252 registers), `ctl` 79 LUTs; `hh8s4u1n1` had 940, 4,141 (255) and 84.
**Computed:** LUTs +175 (+0.4 %), registers +100, setup slack -0.110 ns, hold slack -0.032 ns, `br` -371 LUTs. One build of each; how much slack and LUT count move from run to run with the same design was not measured, so differences of this size are not shown to be real. Route overlaps and wire counts were not asked for or reported.
**What it says:** at 8 blocks the grouped arbiter gave no gain in total LUTs or timing in this pair. The drop of 371 LUTs under `br` is consistent with part of the far-RAM request logic having been counted there (#1493 reading), but it is larger than the yosys estimate of the arbiter's own saving at 8 blocks (119 LUTs, #1494) and the total did not fall, so where the rest went is not known.
**Not done:** a pair at 16 blocks (`hh16s4u1n1` and `hi16s4u1n1`), where the effect should be larger; the route-overlap and wire figures; whole-top simulation; card run of any `hi` build.

## #1497 (26 Nov) -- 16 blocks of 4 slices (64 slices) in Vivado: old arbiter `hh16s4u1n1` and grouped arbiter `hi16s4u1n1` both build and meet timing; the grouped one routes more easily but has less setup slack (+0.709 vs +1.663 ns); neither on the card yet

**Measured (Alan's greps of the two logs and reports, 26 Nov; 0 `Synth 8-9873` in both; bitstreams exist, not loaded):**
- `hh16s4u1n1` (old arbiter, the first 16-block `hh` build; no REQP-1962 stop): setup +1.663 ns, hold +0.040 ns, 259,336 endpoints, 0 failing; 95,071 LUTs (31.84 %). Route overlaps at the first global iteration: 22,689, 958, 106 (and so on; 11 counts in all, to 0, Alan's earlier paste).
- `hi16s4u1n1` (grouped arbiter, groups of 4): setup +0.709 ns, hold +0.038 ns, 259,764 endpoints, 0 failing; 95,177 LUTs (31.87 %). Overlaps: 18,706, 499, 31 (8 counts in all, to 0, from the grep count).
**Computed:** grouped vs old: opening overlap count -17.6 %, 8 vs 11 routing passes, LUTs +106 (+0.1 %), endpoints +428, setup slack -0.954 ns, hold slack -0.002 ns. One build of each, so the run-to-run spread is not known; the overlap and pass differences may or may not be real. The size fit of #1487 predicted 93,904 LUTs for 16x4; measured 95,071 (+1.2 %), inside the 1.5 % of the fit.
**What this says:** at 16 blocks the grouped arbiter made routing easier by these two counts, did not change LUTs, and left less setup slack. Why the slack is lower is not known (the extra register stage should shorten the far-RAM request paths; the path that sets the slack in `hi16s4u1n1` was not looked at). `hi16s4u1n1` has +0.709 ns, the thinnest setup margin of any build so far (the 8x16 `hh` is +1.423 ns); it is still positive with 0 failing endpoints.
**Not done:** which path limits the slack in each build (`report_timing_summary` path); the card test of `hh16s4u1n1` (the 16-block shape has not run on the card); any card run of `hi` (the whole top v16 was not simulated, #1494); slack spread over repeat builds.

- `hh16s8u1n1` (16 blocks x 8 slices = 128): built 11:03 to 11:35 (about 33 min); setup +0.703 ns, hold +0.018 ns, 337,733 endpoints, 0 failing; 135,658 LUTs (45.43 %), 105,770 registers (17.71 %). Route overlaps, first iteration: 30,024, 980, 105, 21, 4, 2, 0.
- `hh16s16u1n1` (16 blocks x 16 slices = 256): built 11:35 to 12:28 (about 53 min); setup +0.259 ns, hold +0.012 ns, 496,036 endpoints, 0 failing; 226,031 LUTs (75.70 %), 165,359 registers (27.69 %). Overlaps: 60,280, 5,845, 1,264, 357, 168, 79, 22, 14, 1, 6, 1, 0, then 2, 21, 7, 2, 0 (the grep took twelve lines after the phase heading, so the later numbers may belong to a following routing phase; not checked).
**Computed, against the #1487 fit (predicted 137,808 and 225,616):** `hh16s8u1n1` measured 1.6 % below the prediction; `hh16s16u1n1` 0.2 % above. Refit on eight builds (8x4, 2x16, 4x16, 8x8, 8x16, 16x4, 16x8, 16x16): LUT = 1,178 + 3,045 x blocks + 685.5 x total slices, every build within 1.46 %.
**Setup slack across the `hh` builds (ns), by size:** 8x4 +6.095 (32 slices); 2x16 +3.525 (32); 8x8 +3.938 (64); 4x16 +1.570 (64); 16x4 +1.663 (64); 8x16 +1.423 (128); 16x8 +0.703 (128); 16x16 +0.259 (256). So slack falls with total slices and the 256-slice build has +0.259 ns at 50 MHz, still positive with 0 failing endpoints; hold +0.012 ns is also positive. Slack is Vivado's, not Fmax, and these are single builds.
**Not done:** card tests of both (the 256-slice design is the largest so far; the code to check: first-read test, single job, `ladder_6`, `ladder_7` with `--blocks 16`, shared RAM on blocks 8 and 15); whether 16 slices per block fuse (units not built: `hh` is width 1); whether the overlap list above is one phase.

## #1502 (26 Nov) -- `hh16s8u1n1` (16 blocks x 8 slices = 128 slices, 45.4 % of the LUTs) on the card: first-read test healthy, single job, 16 of 16 jobs correct, shared RAM of block 15 OK

**Measured (Alan's card output, 26 Nov; loaded `k7/vivado_out_hh16s8u1n1/hh16s8u1n1.bit`, load DONE; board reports version 12, 8 slices per block, 128 code words, shared RAM 256 words per block):** `shared_ram_first_v1.py jtag 0`: B C A B A (healthy). `ladder_1_one_job.json --blocks 16 --lw 0`: 1 of 1 correct, 9,109 clocks (the same count as `hh8s8u1n1`, #1487). `ladder_7_sixteen_jobs.json --blocks 16 --lw 0`: one job on each of 16 blocks, 2,480 words into far RAM in 3.8 s, all 16 blocks finished in 0.15 s, 16 of 16 jobs correct and matching (1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480, 3014, 303, 8451, 681, 16928, 1115, 29045, 1605); per-block clocks 40,788 to 89,527. `shared_ram_check_v1.py jtag pattern 15 0 256`: A, B, C, D each 0 of 256 wrong, `RESULT: shared RAM host path OK`.
**Computed:** the same jobs took slightly more clocks than with 4 slices per block (for example block 0: 89,527 vs 89,168; block 15: 43,160 vs 42,985; 0.4 % more), consistent with the single-job counts (9,046 at 4 slices, 9,109 at 8, #1487); a pattern in these two data points, no cause shown.
**What this settles:** a 128-slice, 16-block Vivado build runs correctly on the card, with all 16 blocks working at once. Not done on this build: far RAM at 0x8000, blocks 8 and others' shared RAM beyond block 15, fusion. The 256-slice `hh16s16u1n1` is the next card test.

## #1503 (26 Nov) -- `hh16s16u1n1` (16 blocks x 16 slices = 256 slices, 75.7 % of the LUTs, setup slack +0.259 ns) on the card: first-read test healthy, single job, 16 of 16 jobs correct, shared RAM of block 15 OK

**Measured (Alan's card output, 26 Nov; loaded `k7/vivado_out_hh16s16u1n1/hh16s16u1n1.bit`, load DONE; board reports version 12, 16 slices per block, 128 code words, shared RAM 256 words per block):** `shared_ram_first_v1.py jtag 0`: B C A B A (healthy). `ladder_1_one_job.json --blocks 16 --lw 0`: 1 of 1 correct, 9,206 clocks (the 4x16 and 8x16 builds gave 9,209 and 9,207, #1487). `ladder_7_sixteen_jobs.json --blocks 16 --lw 0`: one job on each of 16 blocks, 2,480 words into far RAM in 3.9 s, all 16 blocks finished in 0.18 s, 16 of 16 jobs correct and matching (same answers as #1499, #1502); per-block clocks 41,188 to 90,271. `shared_ram_check_v1.py jtag pattern 15 0 256`: A, B, C, D each 0 of 256 wrong, `RESULT: shared RAM host path OK`.
**Computed:** block 0's job took 90,271 clocks here vs 89,167 at 4 slices and 89,527 at 8 (about 1.2 % more than at 4 slices); same direction as the single-job counts (#1502). No cause shown.
**What this settles:** the largest design built so far, 256 slices on 16 blocks at 75.7 % of the LUTs and +0.259 ns Vivado setup slack at 50 MHz, runs correctly on the card with all 16 blocks working at once. The size model (#1501) predicted its LUT count within 0.2 %. Not tested on these two builds: far RAM at 0x8000, fusion (the `hh` line is width 1), long runs (soak), reload cycles (scale tests T1-T8, #1486), and whether the result holds across temperature or on another card.
**Open from this:** the largest `hh` shape that the current host can address is 16 blocks (4-bit select); the 16 x 16 build uses 75.7 % of the LUTs, so the next size steps are a bigger host select for more blocks, or more slices per block (the dispatcher tracks at most 64; 32 and 64 per block unbuilt; RTL support for 32 not checked), or the far-RAM and scale tests.

## #1504 (26 Nov) -- 8 blocks x 32 slices (`hh8s32u1n1`): dispatcher passes its pool-level simulation at S = 32 (no fusion); build files made; a 32-slice FUSED block needs a new 32-lane unit that does not exist yet

**Alan, 26 Nov:** about 80 % of the LUTs is the usual FPGA target; 256 slices on one card is good; try 8 x 32 so that a fusion block could be one 32-slice block.
**Measured (iverilog, `python3 test_sys18.py 32 2 0 1`, full log kept in `/tmp/sys18_s32.log` on the workspace, 9 minutes of run time):** `RESULT PASS`, no `FAIL` line, exit 0: the dispatcher `sc_sys_v18` with 32 slices, no fusion unit (FZ 0, NU 1), runs every test program (shsum, sum100, fib, crc32, softmul_gcd, shifts_signs) alone and in the mixed and "two wide" groups with every result equal to the instruction-set simulator, no trap. Clocks per task alone: shsum 13,197; sum100 41,975; fib 9,319; crc32 198,781; softmul_gcd 17,299; shifts_signs 2,557 (the same at every width, as expected without a unit). This is a one-block, pool-level test; not the whole top, not Vivado, not the card.
**Read (source):** `sc_sys_v18.v` has default S = 32 and a 64-bit slice-busy vector; the fusion unit files exist for 4, 8 and 16 lanes only (`uc_unitw4/8/16`, `uc_unith4/8/16`), the unit generate chain handles ULW 2, 3 and (by the file comment) 4, and the stage and make scripts copy only those. So an 8 x 32 build WITHOUT fusion needs no new RTL; a 32-slice fused block (ULW 5) needs a 32-lane unit pair and a ULW-5 branch in the dispatcher, a new versioned file and its own simulation. Not read in detail, so how much work that is is not known.
**Computed (fit of #1501, not measured):** `hh8s32u1n1` 201,026 LUTs (67.3 %), against 226,031 (75.7 %) for the 16 x 16 with the same 256 slices; the saving is 8 blocks fewer at about 3,045 LUTs each, i.e. about 25,000 LUTs, which would be the room for fusion units (their LUT cost per 32-lane unit is unknown). The 80 % mark is 238,880 LUTs.
**Risks (reasoned, not measured):** setup slack with 32 slices per block in one dispatcher is unknown (the 16 x 16 had +0.259 ns; an earlier clock limit at 16 slices came from the slice-busy popcount, #1344, since reworked); the single-job clock count rises slowly with slices (9,046 / 9,109 / 9,206 at 4 / 8 / 16 per block).
**Files made (new, nothing proven edited):** `s-core/k7/Makefile.hh8_s32_u1_n1`, `s-core/k7/sc_k7_hh8s32u1n1.v` (`make_mb_v9.py 8 32 0 1`); not built.
**Not done:** the Vivado build of `hh8s32u1n1`; any card run; the 32-lane fusion unit and ULW-5 dispatcher; fusion in the Vivado line at any width.

- S 16, ULW 4 (16-lane unit), clocks per task at widths lw 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 (1, 2, 4, 8, 16 slices fused): shsum 12,717 / 6,637 / 3,597 / 2,205 / 1,397; sum100 40,407 / 21,015 / 11,319 / 6,471 / 4,859; fib 9,303 / 4,823 / 2,583 / 1,391 / 1,051; crc32 194,525 / 101,501 / 54,989 / 31,733 / 23,259; softmul_gcd 16,835 / 8,771 / 4,739 / 2,723 / 2,011; shifts_signs 2,557 / 1,325 / 709 / 397 / 283.
- S 8, ULW 3 (8-lane unit), widths lw 0 to 3: shsum 12,709 / 6,629 / 3,589 / 2,069; sum100 40,399 / 21,007 / 11,311 / 6,463; fib 9,231 / 4,751 / 2,511 / 1,391; crc32 193,365 / 100,341 / 53,829 / 30,573; softmul_gcd 16,715 / 8,651 / 4,619 / 2,603; shifts_signs 2,557 / 1,325 / 709 / 397.
**Computed, speedup over lw 0 at S 16 (lw 1, 2, 3, 4):** shsum 1.92 / 3.54 / 5.77 / 9.1; sum100 1.92 / 3.57 / 6.24 / 8.32; fib 1.93 / 3.6 / 6.69 / 8.85; crc32 1.92 / 3.54 / 6.13 / 8.36; softmul_gcd 1.92 / 3.55 / 6.18 / 8.37; shifts_signs 1.93 / 3.61 / 6.44 / 9.04. So fusing all 16 slices of a block gives about 8.3 to 9.1 times on these six programs, and each doubling of width gives about 1.9 times at first, falling to about 1.4 to 1.5 times from 8 to 16 slices.
**Earlier on the card (ledger #1417 and the entry at line 395 of the ledger, open-tool build `fu2_s8_u8_n3`, three 8-lane units per block):** `ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 2 --unit-lw 3 --lw 2`: block 0 28,444, block 1 28,283 clocks, repeatable within a few clocks across fresh loads. Its LUT count is unknown (the unit-less `hh4s16u1n1` is 57,291 LUTs; the open-tool builds put an 8-lane unit at about 3,900 LUTs each, #1387, not the same tool).
