## #1512 (26 Nov) -- Fusion works on the card in a Vivado build: `hh4s16u16n1` runs every job correctly at every width (lw 0 to 4); speedup 1.9, 3.3 to 3.4, 5.2 to 5.4, 8.1 to 8.3 times

**Measured (Alan's card output, 26 Nov; loaded `k7/vivado_out_hh4s16u16n1/hh4s16u16n1.bit`, load DONE; board reports version 12, 16 slices per block):** `shared_ram_first_v1.py jtag 0`: B C A B A (healthy). `ladder_1_one_job.json --blocks 4 --lw 0`: 1 of 1 correct, 9,216 clocks (the unit-less 4 x 16 gave 9,209). `ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 4 --unit-lw 4 --lw N`, N = 0 to 4, two passes each: **4 of 4 jobs correct in all ten runs.** Clocks per block (blocks 0, 1, 2, 3), pass 1: lw 0: 94,467 / 94,458 / 87,227 / 66,913; lw 1: 50,405 / 50,403 / 46,227 / 35,516; lw 2: 28,364 / 28,367 / 25,738 / 19,823; lw 3: 18,177 / 18,172 / 16,133 / 12,465; lw 4: 11,442 / 11,442 / 10,675 / 8,282. Pass 2 is within 15 clocks of pass 1 in every cell (for example block 0, lw 4: 11,445; block 3, lw 3: 12,458); the first run after the load was not slower here.
**Computed, speedup over lw 0 (lw 1, 2, 3, 4):** blocks 0 and 1: 1.87, 3.33, 5.20, 8.26; block 2: 1.89, 3.39, 5.41, 8.17; block 3: 1.88, 3.38, 5.37, 8.08. The simulation of #1509 (other programs, one block) gave about 1.92, 3.5 to 3.6, 5.8 to 6.7 and 8.3 to 9.1 times; the card ratios are a little lower at lw 2 and lw 3 and about the same at lw 4. Not looked into why; the card jobs include loading and result copying (a single lw 0 job takes 9,216 clocks, #1498), and the jobs and programs differ from the simulation, so the figures are not directly comparable. Fixed costs per job would pull the ratios down; that reading was not tested.
**What this settles:** the fusion unit (16 lanes, `uc_unitw16`, `uc_unith16`) works on the card in a Vivado-built bitstream, four blocks at once, all widths 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 slices fused, with correct answers and repeatable clocks. The danger noted in #1511 (Vivado mishandling the unit as it did the falling-edge registers) did not show.
**Not done:** fusion at more than 4 blocks or at 8 and 32 slices per block (the 8 x 32 build with a 32-lane unit does not exist yet, #1504); the other job kinds (vprefix, vgain, vscale) at wide widths; fusion with several units per block (NU above 1); a fusion build of the 256-slice shapes; the critical path.

## #1513 (26 Nov) -- What a 32-lane fusion unit needs: read of the unit generators and the dispatcher, and a trial run of the generator at 32 lanes (scratch copy, nothing added to the repository)

**Alan, 26 Nov:** yes, look at it; the ultimate shapes, by size, are 8 x 32 or 16 x 16.
**Read (source):** the fusion units are GENERATED, not hand-written: `uc_unit4.v` (hand-written 4 lanes) -> `make_unit_v1.py L` (lane cells; `uc_unitg<W>`) -> `make_unit_v2.py L` (pipelined shift index; `uc_unitp<W>`) -> `make_unit_v3.py L` (word-RAM wrapper; `uc_unitr<W>`) -> `make_unit_v5.py L` (pool hooks; `uc_unith<W>` and `uc_unitw<W>`), W = 2**L lanes. The usage lines name L = 2, 3, 4 only. The gate is `test_wide.py uc_unitg<W> L`: the unit at every width against the RV32I reference simulator and `uc_core_v2` at the same width, same results and same clock counts. The base core `uc_core_v2` is described as "any datapath width W = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32". The dispatcher `sc_sys_v18.v` picks the unit file in a generate chain: ULW 2 -> `uc_unitw4`, ULW 3 -> `uc_unitw8`, anything else -> `uc_unitw16` (so ULW 5 would silently get the 16-lane unit unless a branch is added); it keeps the task width in 3 bits (`launch_lw`) and clamps it to ULW. The host (`scblocks.py`) takes `--unit-lw` as a number and clamps widths to min(unit width, log2 slices); no cap at 4 was seen in the lines read.
**Measured (a scratch copy of `s-core` in `/tmp`, since deleted; nothing added to the repository):** `python3 make_unit_v5.py 5` ran without error and wrote `uc_unitg32/p32/r32/h32/w32`. `iverilog` on `uc_unitg32.v`: 4 elaboration errors, `pc[31:32]`, `imm_sr[31:32]` and `addr[31:32]` "out of order" at lines 148, 149, 204, 205 (the 32-lane case rotates a 32-bit register by 32, which leaves an empty part select), plus a warning at line 71: `wire [4:0] WE` holds 5'd32 truncated to 0. So the generator at L = 5 is close but not correct as it stands. The gate was not run (it stops at the compile errors).
**Reasoned, not shown:** further problems may sit behind these (the gate will show: digit counter, carry tap `cc[32]`, shift funnel at one 32-bit digit, the RAM digit select `kk`). Steps, each a new versioned file: (1) clones of the four generators (v6 to v9 style) that special-case W = 32 (rotate by 32 = no change, 6-bit `WE`); (2) run the gate at L = 5 until it passes (the gate runs every width 1 to 32 and 40 random programs; the 16-lane run took about 6 minutes in the pool test, the stand-alone gate time at 32 is unknown); (3) `sc_sys_v19` = v18 plus a ULW 5 branch for `uc_unitw32`; (4) pool test `test_sys` at S 32 and ULW 5 (the S 32 run without units took 9 minutes); (5) the host needs `--lw 5` allowed (not checked in detail); (6) a Vivado build.
**Computed sizes (estimates, not measured):** a 16-lane unit costs about 2,550 LUTs in Vivado (#1511); a 32-lane unit is a guess of 4,000 to 5,500 each. 8 x 32 with one 32-lane unit per block: 197,205 + 8 x (4,000 to 5,500) = 229,000 to 241,000 LUTs (77 to 81 % of the chip). 16 x 16 with the existing 16-lane unit per block: 226,031 + 16 x 2,550 = 266,800 LUTs (89 %), well over the 80 % mark; its setup slack was +0.259 ns before adding units. By this estimate 8 x 32 is the shape that can carry a full-block fusion unit, 16 x 16 would have to use smaller units or fewer of them.
**Not done:** any of the steps above; the stand-alone gate at L = 5.

**#1514 (26 Nov) -- 32-lane fusion unit: generator clones make_unit_v6 to v9 written; the stand-alone gate passes at L = 5.**
**Measured:** `python3 make_unit_v9.py 5` writes uc_unitg32, p32, r32, h32 and w32 (.v); `uc_unitg32.v` compiles with iverilog -g2012 with no errors. The gate `python3 test_wide.py uc_unitg32 5` printed "276/276 runs: results equal to the reference simulator and to uc_core_v2, clock counts equal to uc_core_v2 at W = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32" and RESULT: PASS (exit 0). CPI at W = 32 on the six programs: sum100 4.00, fib 4.15, bubblesort 4.49, softmul_gcd 4.22, crc32 4.18, shifts_signs 4.56 (W = 16: 5.99 to 6.83).
**What changed (new files only; the proven v1 to v5 generators are untouched):** v6 = v1 for L = 5 only (6-bit WE; rotate by 32 is no rotation, so pc_rot, imm_rot, pc_ins and addr_ins at k = 5 use pc, imm_sr and sum_d[31:0] directly); v7, v8, v9 = clones of v2, v3, v5 that call v6, v7, v8.
**Not shown:** the chain files p32, r32, h32, w32 were generated but only uc_unitg32 is checked by this gate (they are exercised by the pool test); no pool or card run yet; size of the 32-lane unit in LUTs is still an estimate (#1513).
**Next:** sc_sys_v19 (v18 plus a ULW 5 branch for uc_unitw32), pool test S 32 ULW 5, host --lw 5, Vivado 8 x 32 fusion build.

**#1515 (26 Nov) -- pool-level fusion with the 32-lane unit: sc_sys_v19 (v18 plus a ULW 5 branch for uc_unitw32) passes the pool test at S 32, ULW 5; widths 0 to 5 correct, but no speedup from 16 to 32 lanes on these six programs.**
**Measured:** `python3 test_sys19.py 32 5 1 1` (iverilog, about 6 minutes): RESULT PASS with the alone sweep extended to lw 0..5 (test_sys19.py is a clone of test_sys18.py; its sweep stopped at lw 4, so the first run did not exercise 32 lanes; the second run with lw 5 did). Clocks per task, lw0..5: shsum [13197, 7117, 3853, 2781, 2773, 2769]; sum100 [41975, 22583, 12887, 9707, 9707, 9707]; fib [9319, 4839, 2711, 2091, 2091, 2091]; crc32 [198781, 105757, 59245, 46507, 46507, 46507]; softmul_gcd [17299, 9235, 5203, 4011, 4011, 4011]; shifts_signs [2557, 1325, 709, 555, 555, 555]. Mixed and "many" task sets also pass. New files: sc_sys_v19.v, tb_sys19.v, test_sys19.py (v18 files untouched).
**Measured, important:** in the pool, whole-task clocks stop falling at lw 3 (8 lanes) for five of six programs and at lw 3/4/5 only shsum shaves a few clocks. So at this level a 32-lane unit buys nothing on these programs; the stand-alone gate (#1514) showed the unit itself is faster (CPI 4.0 vs 6.0), so the limit is elsewhere in the pool path (reading, not proven: instruction fetch and dispatch per instruction). The card at lw 0..4 on hh4s16u16n1 (#1512) gave speedups still rising (5.2 then 8.3 on block 0), which does not match this plateau; different job mix, not yet explained.
**Not done:** host --lw 5 check, Vivado 8 x 32 fusion build, any card run.

**#1516 (26 Nov) -- bottleneck found: a fusion unit gets one instruction per S clocks (one fetch slot per round of the code store), so width stops helping once execution takes less than S clocks per instruction.**
**Measured (probe tb_sys19p.v + probe_sys19.py, S 32, ULW 5, one task alone, counters on the unit while bound):** whole-task clocks are identical at lw 3, 4, 5 (sum100 9,707; fib 2,091; crc32 46,507). Over that fixed time the unit's clock-enable count falls with width (sum100 3,036 / 1,824 / 1,218 at lw 3 / 4 / 5) while the "fetching" count rises (4,244 / 6,668 / 7,880): the wider the unit, the more of the task it spends waiting for the next instruction. Instructions fetched with clock-enable (ce and fetching together): sum100 304, fib 66, crc32 1,454, the same at every width. Clocks per fetched instruction: 9,707 / 304 = 31.9; 2,091 / 66 = 31.7; 46,507 / 1,454 = 32.0, i.e. S = 32.
**Read from the RTL (sc_sys_v19.v lines 254 to 266 and 550, not separately tested):** one shared code store; a free-running slot counter gives each slice one clock in S to load its fetch register, and a slice's clock enable `ce_g` needs its slot phase (`cph`). The fusion unit borrows the leader slice's fetch register and clock enable, so it executes at most one instruction per S clocks whatever its width.
**Consequences:** (a) this is also the cause of "single-job clocks grow with slices per block" (#1498 to #1510: 9,046 / 9,109 / 9,206 / 9,464 for S 4 / 8 / 16 / 32 are the same effect, not tested separately); (b) a 32-lane unit at S 32 gains nothing on these programs; the speedup ceiling for a unit in a block of S slices is about CPI_1 / S where the unit would need fewer than S clocks per instruction, so at S 16 a 16-lane unit (CPI about 6) is already below S and is also fetch-limited, which fits the card figures flattening towards lw 4; (c) the unit's own execution is correct and fast (#1514, CPI 4.0 at 32 lanes), the pool path is the limit.
**Not done / options, none built:** give a bound unit a fetch slot every clock (it owns the code store while fused, other slices wait); or a small fetch buffer of several words per slot; or a second code-store read port for the unit. Each needs a new dispatcher version and a simulation check that slice results and shared-RAM timing are unchanged.

**#1517 (26 Nov) -- fix 1 for the fetch limit built and passing in simulation: sc_sys_v20 lets a bound fusion unit take the code-store read on every clock whose slot owner does not want it; unit speed now scales with width.**
**Change (new files only: sc_sys_v20.v, tb_sys20.v, test_sys20.py; v19 untouched):** the code-store address is `steal_v ? target of the bound unit's leader slice : target of the slot owner`. The unit's leader steals a clock only when the slot owner does not want a fetch (`want_all[slot_cnt]` low), so no other slice loses its slot; the first bound unit (lowest index) that wants a fetch gets the clock. The leader's fetch register loads on its own slot or on a stolen clock. The ordinary slot path is unchanged.
**Measured (iverilog pool test, one task alone, clocks):** S 32, ULW 5, NU 1: RESULT PASS, widths 0..5 correct. sum100 lw0..5: 41,975 / 20,615 / 10,919 / 6,071 / 3,647 / 2,435 (v19 had 41,975 / 22,583 / 12,887 / 9,707 / 9,707 / 9,707); fib 9,319 / 4,751 / 2,511 / 1,391 / 831 / 551 (v19 ... 2,091 floor); crc32 198,781 / 99,359 / 52,847 / 29,591 / 17,963 / 12,149 (v19 floor 46,507); shifts_signs 2,557 ... 169; non-fusion (lw0) clocks unchanged. Speedup lw5 over lw0: sum100 17.2x, fib 16.9x, crc32 16.4x (v19 best 4.3x, 4.5x, 4.3x). Also RESULT PASS: S 16 ULW 4 NU 2, S 8 ULW 3 NU 2, S 4 ULW 2 NU 1 (mixed and "many" task sets included). Per-width clocks are the same for a given width at all four shapes (e.g. sum100 lw3 6,071), so the unit no longer depends on S.
**Not shown:** other slices' timing with a unit running next to busy slices is only covered by the mixed sets in the test (their clocks moved by a few clocks, e.g. crc3/0 198,783 vs 198,781 alone); no timing/area figure (the added logic is a small search over NU units and an address mux, estimate only); no Vivado build and no card run of v20; the host and the 8 x 32 build still need `--lw 5`.
**Next (Alan's order):** expand the storage area after this.

**#1518 (26 Nov) -- code store moved to block RAM: sc_sys_v21 (v20 plus a registered-read code store); passes the pool test at S 32/8/4; costs about 2 clocks per fetch.**
**Why:** the code store was `ram_style = "distributed"` (LUT RAM, asynchronous read) with IMEM_WORDS 128 in every build; the cost of LUT RAM grows with words, so a big store would eat LUTs (estimate, not measured: roughly one LUT per 2 words per 32 bits, so a few hundred LUTs per block at 1,024 words and more than a thousand at 4,096). Block RAM costs almost no LUTs but reads one clock late.
**Change (new files only: sc_sys_v21.v, tb_sys21.v, test_sys21.py; v20 untouched):** `imem_sh` is `ram_style = "block"`; `sh_q` is registered; each slice keeps `ld_q` (my request went to the store last clock) and `ld_a` (its address) and loads `fw`/`fw_a` one clock after the request; a code write (imem_we) cancels a request in flight. The slot and steal logic of v20 is unchanged.
**Measured (iverilog, testbench store 4,096 words, one task alone, clocks):** S 32, ULW 5, NU 1, RESULT PASS, no FAIL lines, mixed and "many" sets included. sum100 lw0..5: 41,977 / 20,815 / 11,119 / 6,271 / 3,847 / 2,635 (v20: 41,975 / 20,615 / 10,919 / 6,071 / 3,647 / 2,435); fib lw5 571 (v20 551); shifts_signs lw5 171 (169); plain (lw0) jobs change by 2 clocks or less. Cost: about 2 clocks extra per branch-or-fetch wait, which is 8 % at lw5 on sum100 and nothing on plain jobs. Also RESULT PASS: S 8 ULW 3 NU 2, S 4 ULW 2 NU 1.
**Read, not changed here:** the host reports IMEM_WORDS to the PC in two bytes (so up to 65,535 words) and the plan tool takes `--imem N`; code regions use IAW = clog2(IMEM_WORDS) bits; make_mb_v9 fixes `.IMEM_WORDS(128)` in the wrapper, so a bigger store needs a new generator version. The BRAM count per block and whether the block-RAM read meets 50 MHz are not measured.
**Not done:** a top and block that use sc_sys_v21 (the build line still instantiates v18 through sc_block_v4 and sc_top_k7_v15); generator with an IMEM_WORDS argument; Vivado builds; card tests; whether the shared far-RAM and data addressing must widen (next step, with the RAM side).

**#1519 (26 Nov) -- new build line `hj`: sc_block_v5 + sc_top_k7_v17 (dispatcher sc_sys_v21: unit steals free code-store clocks, code store in block RAM) and generator make_mb_v11.py with a code-store size argument; three build files made, nothing built yet.**
**Files (new, proven ones untouched):** `fpga/sc_block_v5.v` (sc_block_v4 with sc_sys_v21), `fpga/sc_top_k7_v17.v` (sc_top_k7_v15 with sc_block_v5), `k7/make_mb_v11.py NB S ULW NU IMEM` (writes `Makefile.hj<NB>_s<S>_u<W>_n<NU>_i<IMEM>` and `sc_k7_hj<NB>s<S>u<W>n<NU>i<IMEM>.v`; names the project `hj...`; source list gets sc_sys_v21, sc_block_v5, sc_top_k7_v17), `k7/stage_mb_v11.sh` (stage_mb_v10 plus sc_sys_v21, sc_block_v5, sc_top_k7_v17, uc_unitw32 and uc_unith32). Made with it: `hj4s16u16n1i128` (same shape as hh4s16u16n1, so the v21 change alone can be compared), `hj4s16u16n1i1024` (adds the bigger code store), `hj8s32u32n1i1024` (8 x 32, a 32-lane unit per block).
**Measured:** iverilog elaboration of the whole top (hj8s32u32n1i1024 with every listed source) gets through except BSCANE2, which is the Xilinx primitive and expected to be missing; nothing else is unresolved. A yosys read of the same list stops on uc_unitw4 being referenced by the generate chain, the same situation as for earlier builds that list only one unit width (reading: inactive generate branches; not re-checked against hh builds in this entry).
**Not done / not shown:** the whole top is unsimulated (as for v14 to v16); the host program still defaults to --imem 128 and must be given the new size (or asks the board; not checked); sizes, timing and card results of any hj build. How a bigger code store is loaded and used by programs (the plan/compile tools take --imem) is untested at sizes above 128.
**Run on the build machine (Alan):** `cd ~/Imago-Unicell-score/s-core/k7; git pull; sh stage_mb_v11.sh; sh run_builds_v1.sh hj4s16u16n1i128 hj4s16u16n1i1024 hj8s32u32n1i1024` (the builds run one after another; the first two take about half an hour each; the 8 x 32 one longer).
**Requested, not started (Alan):** a front-end function where the user enters a design size and gets generated Verilog (NB, S, ULW, NU, IMEM wrapper plus file list), which they run through their own toolchain to make a bitstream that the front end then loads and uses. The generator `make_mb_v11.py` already writes the wrapper and the file list for one tool flow; the missing pieces would be a size check against a board's limits (using the size model of #1501/#1511), a neutral output (Verilog plus a file list, no Makefile for our tools), and the front-end loading of a foreign bitstream with the right --blocks/--lw/--imem values.

**#1520 (26 Nov) -- design note (Alan's request, extends #1519): a store of pre-generated bitstreams, the user's or ours, that the front end can pick, load and run. Not built.**
**Idea:** the front end keeps a folder of bitstreams, each with a small manifest (build name and line, NB, S, ULW, NU, IMEM_WORDS, DSH, DMW, far words, setup and hold slack, LUT count, the git commit it came from, a checksum of the .bit). The user picks one (or the front end picks the smallest that fits their jobs from the size model of #1501 and #1511), loads it with the existing loader (SRAM only, never flash), and the front end sets --blocks, --lw, --unit-lw and --imem from the manifest.
**Checks that already exist and would be used:** the host answers a 'V' query with S, IMEM_WORDS and DMW (host_bridge_v13), so the front end can compare the loaded design with the manifest and refuse a mismatch; the first-read test after load (healthy `B C A B A`); the standing rule that a bitstream whose timing failed is not loaded (a manifest with negative slack is flagged and refused).
**Open:** the 'V' reply does not carry NB, ULW or NU (read from the 'V' text in the bridge header, not checked in the RTL), so those would rely on the manifest; where the store lives (folder on the PC or a repo); what happens for a user's own build from our generated Verilog (#1519), whose manifest the user would have to write or the generator would write beside the Verilog.

**#1521 (26 Nov) -- decision (Alan), answers the open points of #1520: the bitstream store is local to the user's machine; the front end only needs to be told the folder, and the generator's output goes there.**
**Decided:** no hosted store. A user who keeps their files in git or off-site storage does that in the normal way outside the front end. The front end has one setting, the folder where generated Verilog and bitstreams live; it lists bitstreams there by their manifests. Not built.
**Still open (not decided):** that the generator writes the manifest beside the Verilog (my suggestion in #1520, not yet agreed), and the extra fields in the board's 'V' reply.

**#1522 (26 Nov) -- build manifest and board profiles: `make_manifest_v1.py`, `targets_v1.json`, `k7/make_mb_v12.py` (v11 plus a manifest written beside the Verilog); says what fits on which board.**
**What it writes:** `sc_k7_<build>.manifest.json` with blocks, slices per block, unit lanes and units per block, code words, data sizes, far RAM words, the host arguments (--blocks, --lw, --unit-lw, --imem), predicted LUTs (low and high, with the basis), and a verdict per board in targets_v1.json: recommended (at or under the 80 % target), possible, tight, no, or unknown, with the reason. Kintex XC7K480T: 298,600 LUTs (from Vivado's 226,031 = 75.7 %), 80 % = 238,880. Tang Nano 20K: vendor figures (20,736 LUT4, 15,552 FF, 828 Kbit block SRAM in 46 blocks, 27 MHz; Sipeed wiki and datasheet), verdict always unknown for an S-Core pool because none has been built for it and LUT4 is not comparable with the LUT6 model.
**Measured (checked by running it):** hj4s16u16n1i1024 predicts 66,982 LUTs against the 67,501 Vivado reported for hh4s16u16n1 (0.8 % under; the code-store change is not in the model); hj16s16u1n1i128 predicts 224,002 against 226,031 measured for hh16s16u1n1 (0.9 % under). hj8s32u32n1i1024 predicts 230,794 to 242,794 (77.3 to 81.3 %), verdict possible, because the 32-lane unit size is a guess. Widths 4 and 8 give "unknown" (unit sizes not measured), 16 lanes uses the measured 2,550.
**Not done:** the front end does not read manifests yet; the size of the 2, 4, 8 and 32-lane units and of the block-RAM code store are not measured, so those verdicts stay unknown or ranged until the hj builds report; no Tang S-Core build exists, so the Tang verdict cannot be filled in; the manifest has no slack, bitstream checksum or commit yet (they exist only after a build; a later step adds them from builds_summary).
**Next:** when the hj builds finish, fill in unit and code-store sizes from their reports.

**#1523 (26 Nov) -- direction (Alan): once the designs are complete, the manifests and the front end become the base of a complete user package: a full manual including programming, and a system a user other than us can use. Planning note, nothing built.**
**Shape proposed (mine, not yet agreed in detail):** (1) the manual's build tables are generated from the manifests and the measured build reports, so numbers cannot drift from the builds (the manual already has a generator, docs/build_manual.py, and a plan file, s-core/USAGE_MANUAL_AND_SCALE_TESTS_PLAN_v1.md, T1 to T10); (2) a programming chapter: job format, limits per build (code words, shared RAM, far RAM, jobs per batch, lanes), the fusion width rule and what each width gives (#1516, #1517 figures), examples that run; (3) a setup chapter for the user's own build: generator, board profile, toolchain hand-off, bitstream folder, loading (SRAM only); (4) the front end: board and folder setting, manifest list, loaded-design check with the host 'V' reply, refusal of failed-timing builds.
**Depends on:** the hj builds (unit and code-store sizes), the scale tests, and the open points of #1520/#1521 (V reply fields, manifest content after a build).

**#1524 (26 Nov) -- correction and rule (Alan), to #1523: the manual generator docs/build_manual.py belongs to the UniCell system and is not to be reused or edited for S-Core; an S-Core manual needs its own generator written for S-Core_go.**
**Correction:** #1523 named docs/build_manual.py as the existing generator for the S-Core manual's tables; that was wrong in scope. Nothing was changed in it. **Rule:** the S-Core manual gets a new, separate generator (own file name, own output), reading the S-Core manifests and build reports; docs/build_manual.py and docs/manual.html stay as they are. Not built.

**#1525 (26 Nov) -- to-do list: s-core/S_CORE_TODO.md created (Alan asked for a list kept and crossed off, as he may forget parts within a week).**
**What it is:** one living file with five groups (builds on the card, design changes, front end and user builds, scale tests and manual, standing rules), each item ticked or open and tied to the ledger entry that shows it. It is edited in place, unlike the ledger. Pointer added to current/START.md so a new session finds it. Contents are the items of #1514 to #1524 plus the older parked ones (#1491 hi card test, T1 to T10, far RAM expansion).

**#1526 (27 Nov) -- the three hj builds finished and all met timing: hj4s16u16n1i128 67,840 LUTs +0.358/+0.048 ns; hj4s16u16n1i1024 69,337 LUTs +0.574/+0.043; hj8s32u32n1i1024 (8 x 32, 32-lane unit per block) 243,417 LUTs (81.52 %) +0.595/+0.034. Not yet on the card.**
**Measured (Alan's run_builds_v1.sh summary, 26 Nov 19:13 to 22:02; vivado exit 0 for all, Synth 8-9873 count 0, ERROR lines 0, no failing endpoints):** build times 28 min, 22 min, 1 h 59 min. Slice LUTs / registers: i128 67,840 / 44,088 (22.72 %); i1024 69,337 / 45,112 (23.22 %); 8 x 32 243,417 / 150,650 (81.52 %, 25.23 %). Setup / hold slack (Vivado WNS / WHS): +0.358 / +0.048; +0.574 / +0.043; +0.595 / +0.034. Bit files all 18,735,121 bytes (the part's fixed size).
**Computed from those numbers (differences, single builds):** v21 dispatcher alone (hj4s16u16n1i128 against hh4s16u16n1 at 67,501): +339 LUTs (+0.5 %); setup slack fell from +2.987 to +0.358 ns (-2.6 ns). Code store 128 to 1,024 words (i1024 against i128): +1,497 LUTs and +1,024 registers over four blocks, i.e. about 374 LUTs and 256 registers per block, far fewer than distributed RAM would cost (my estimate in #1518 was several hundred LUTs per block at 1,024 words, so it is the same order, not a saving; whether the store was placed in block RAM is NOT shown by this summary, since it prints no RAM-block count); the extra registers fit the wider address registers of every slice (reading, not checked). The slack went up from +0.358 to +0.574 with the bigger store, so the spread between single builds is at least 0.2 ns and the 2.6 ns fall is well beyond that.
**Computed, unit size:** 8 x 32 hj against hh8s32u1n1 (197,205): +46,212 LUTs over 8 blocks = 5,777 per block; taking off the v21 plus code-store cost seen at 4 x 16 (1,836 over four blocks = 459 per block) leaves about 5,300 per 32-lane unit; the code-store and address cost is probably larger at 32 slices, so the unit is about 5,000 to 5,300 LUTs, about twice the 16-lane unit (2,550). My #1513 guess was 4,000 to 5,500. The model of #1501 (base 198,794 for 8 x 32) plus 8 x 5,300 gives 241,194, 0.9 % under the measured figure.
**Meaning:** the 8 x 32 fusion design fits (81.5 %), slightly over the 80 % working target, and met timing with +0.595 ns, better than the plain 8 x 32 (+0.156, single builds). Setup slack on the 4 x 16 shape is much smaller than before the dispatcher change; timing is met, but the margin for larger or faster designs is smaller there; cause not looked into (reading: the new code-store address mux and the steal search; untested).
**Recorded:** `builds_measured_v1.json` (measured results per build); `make_manifest_v1.py` now uses a measured figure when one exists and takes the 32-lane unit as 5,000 to 5,300; manifests written for the three hj builds (hj8s32u32n1i1024 verdict on the Kintex card: tight, 81.5 % against the 80 % target); K7_BUILDS_AND_LOADING_v1.md table rows added. **Not done:** card tests of any hj bitstream; the critical path of the new dispatcher; RAM-block counts.

**#1527 (27 Nov) -- card test of `hj4s16u16n1i128` (v21 dispatcher, 4 x 16, 16-lane unit per block, 128 code words): everything correct; fusion speedups 1.9 / 3.5 / 5.7 to 5.9 / 8.3 to 9.3 times, a little above the earlier build; the test jobs are too small to show the fetch fix.**
**Measured (Alan's card output, 27 Nov 00:57; loaded `k7/vivado_out_hj4s16u16n1i128/hj4s16u16n1i128.bit`, `init 1 done 1`; board version 12, 16 slices per block, 128 code words, 16 data words):** first-read test B C A B A (healthy). Shared RAM block 0, 256 words: patterns A to D 0 wrong, "host path OK". Far RAM 0x2000 and 0x8000, 512 words each: 0 wrong, both banks. `ladder_1_one_job` (--blocks 4 --lw 0): 1 of 1 correct, 9,238 clocks (hh4s16u16n1: 9,216; unit-less 4 x 16: 9,209). `ladder_6_eight_jobs`: 8 of 8 correct (1600, 244, 5950, 600, 13080, 1012, 23590, 1480). `ladder_3_more_data --blocks 4 --unit-lw 4 --lw N`, N = 0 to 4: 4 of 4 correct in all five runs. Clocks, blocks 0 / 1 / 2 / 3: lw 0: 94,472 / 94,482 / 87,239 / 66,923; lw 1: 48,750 / 48,756 / 46,161 / 35,435; lw 2: 26,724 / 26,721 / 25,658 / 19,741; lw 3: 16,480 / 16,482 / 14,784 / 11,426; lw 4: 11,344 / 11,359 / 9,338 / 7,244.
**Computed, speedup over lw 0 (lw 1, 2, 3, 4):** block 0: 1.94, 3.54, 5.73, 8.33; block 1: 1.94, 3.54, 5.73, 8.32; block 2: 1.89, 3.40, 5.90, 9.34; block 3: 1.89, 3.39, 5.86, 9.24. Earlier build hh4s16u16n1 (#1512): 1.87 / 3.33 / 5.20 / 8.26 (blocks 0, 1), 1.89 / 3.39 / 5.41 / 8.17 (block 2), 1.88 / 3.38 / 5.37 / 8.08 (block 3). So lw 3 is about 10 % better (5.20 to 5.73 on block 0), lw 4 about 1 % better on blocks 0 and 1 and 14 % on blocks 2 and 3 (8.17 to 9.34, 8.08 to 9.24); lw 1 and lw 2 are within 1 to 6 %. (Single runs; lw 0 clocks match the earlier build within 0.1 %.)
**Reading, not tested:** a single small job costs 9,238 clocks at lw 0 (#1498) and the jobs here take 7,000 to 11,000 clocks even at lw 4, so a fixed part of that size per job (loading, result copy, wait) caps the speedup near 8 to 9 times; the fetch limit of #1516 matters for compute-heavy programs (sum100, crc32 in the simulation: 4 times to 17 times at lw 5), which these vector jobs are not. The card test does not yet show the large gain predicted by the pool simulation (#1517).
**What this settles:** the v21 dispatcher with unit clock stealing and block-RAM code store works on the card at 4 x 16 with a 16-lane unit, all widths, no wrong answers; the extra fetch latency costs 22 clocks on a single job (9,216 to 9,238).
**Not done:** `hj4s16u16n1i1024` and `hj8s32u32n1i1024` on the card; any test with a program above 128 code words; a compute-heavy job on the card to show the fetch fix; whether the code store is in block RAM.

**#1528 (27 Nov) -- card test of `hj4s16u16n1i1024` (same as i128 but a 1,024-word code store): everything correct, speedups and clocks the same as with 128 words; the host reports 1,024 code words but the job planner still packs for 128 unless given `--imem`.**
**Measured (Alan's card output, 27 Nov 01:05; first load attempt failed with "Open file FAIL" because the path was wrong, before touching the card; second load `init 1 done 1`; board version 12, 16 slices per block, `imem_words` 1024):** first-read test B C A B A; shared RAM 256 words and both far RAM banks (512 words each): 0 wrong; `ladder_1_one_job` 1 of 1 correct, 9,225 clocks (i128: 9,238); `ladder_6_eight_jobs` 8 of 8 correct; `ladder_3_more_data --unit-lw 4 --lw N`, N = 0 to 4: 4 of 4 correct in all five runs. Clocks per block (0 / 1 / 2 / 3): lw 0 94,477 / 94,481 / 87,247 / 66,923; lw 1 48,758 / 48,748 / 46,158 / 35,434; lw 2 26,721 / 26,719 / 25,654 / 19,736; lw 3 16,482 / 16,487 / 14,777 / 11,421; lw 4 11,348 / 11,358 / 9,326 / 7,256.
**Computed:** speedup over lw 0 (lw 1 to 4), block 0: 1.94, 3.54, 5.73, 8.33; block 1: 1.94, 3.54, 5.72, 8.32; block 2: 1.89, 3.40, 5.90, 9.36; block 3: 1.89, 3.39, 5.86, 9.22. Against i128 (#1527) every clock count is within 0.2 % (largest gap: block 3 lw 4, 7,256 against 7,244), so the larger store changes nothing in speed on these jobs.
**Read from the source (scblocks.py line 245):** `--imem` has default 128 and is not asked from the board (unlike `--dsh`, which is), so the planner prints "code 46/128 words" even though the board says 1,024; to pack jobs for 1,024 words the option `--imem 1024` must be given. Not run on the card.
**What this settles:** a 1,024-word code store (block RAM, v21) works on the card at 4 x 16 for the existing jobs. **Not shown:** that any program above 128 words runs (no such job was run; it needs `--imem 1024` and a program larger than 128 words), and that the memory is in block RAM.
**Not done:** `hj8s32u32n1i1024` on the card.

**#1529 (27 Nov) -- card test of `hj8s32u32n1i1024` (8 blocks of 32 slices, 1,024 code words, 32-lane unit per block): design runs, shared RAM, far RAM, one job and eight jobs on eight blocks all correct; the fusion run was made at 4 blocks and `--unit-lw 4` (16 lanes), so the 32-lane unit itself is NOT yet tested on the card. Correction to #1528.**
**Measured (Alan's card output, 27 Nov 01:08; the paste starts at the first-read test, the load lines are not in it; board version 12, `S` 32, `imem_words` 1024, which identifies the 8 x 32 build):** first-read test B C A B A; shared RAM 256 words and both far RAM banks 512 words: 0 wrong. `ladder_1_one_job --blocks 8 --lw 0`: 1 of 1 correct, 9,455 clocks (hh8s32u1n1: 9,464). `ladder_6_eight_jobs --blocks 8 --lw 0`: 8 of 8 correct, one job per block on 8 blocks (87,647 / 69,116 / 73,192 / 60,082 / 58,760 / 51,043 / 42,021 / 44,292 clocks); all eight answers match. `ladder_3_more_data --blocks 4 --unit-lw 4 --lw N`, N = 0 to 4: 4 of 4 correct in all five runs. Clocks per block (0 / 1 / 2 / 3): lw 0 96,236 / 96,249 / 88,660 / 68,036; lw 1 48,771 / 48,782 / 47,449 / 36,409; lw 2 28,360 / 28,347 / 25,704 / 19,781; lw 3 18,113 / 18,101 / 14,844 / 11,484; lw 4 11,403 / 11,396 / 9,368 / 7,306.
**Computed, speedup over lw 0 (lw 1 to 4):** block 0: 1.97, 3.39, 5.31, 8.44; block 1: 1.97, 3.40, 5.32, 8.45; block 2: 1.87, 3.45, 5.97, 9.46; block 3: 1.87, 3.44, 5.92, 9.31. Against the 4 x 16 build (#1528): lw 0 is 2 to 7 % slower with 32 slices per block (96,236 against 94,477 on block 0), lw 2 and lw 3 are 6 to 10 % slower on blocks 0 and 1 (28,360 against 26,721; 18,113 against 16,482), lw 4 is the same within 0.5 %.
**What was and was not tested:** the fusion loop used the 4 x 16 command (`--blocks 4 --unit-lw 4`), so only 4 of 8 blocks ran fused jobs, at 16 lanes inside a 32-lane unit's block, and lw 5 was not run. The 32-lane width (`--unit-lw 5`, `--lw 5`) has not been run on the card. Whether the host accepts `--lw 5` has not been checked.
**Correction to #1528:** its text gives the block 1 lw 3 speedup as 5.72; by the same clocks (94,481 / 16,487) it is 5.73. No other figure changes.
**Not done:** the 32-lane runs on the card: `ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 8 --unit-lw 5 --lw N` for N = 0 to 5, and `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json` with `--unit-lw 5 --lw 5` (eight jobs, one per block); a compute-heavy job; programs above 128 words (`--imem 1024`).

**#1530 (27 Nov) -- the 32-lane unit on the card (hj8s32u32n1i1024, `--unit-lw 5`): lw 0 to 4 correct, but lw 5 fails the host's check on every job (status 1, n_out 0, want 1); the jobs finish, and much faster, so the unit runs; a standalone simulation of private-memory store and load at 32 lanes passes; cause not found.**
**Measured (Alan's card output, 27 Nov 01:11, `ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 8 --unit-lw 5 --lw N`, N = 0 to 5, 4 jobs on blocks 0 to 3):** lw 0 to 4: 4 of 4 correct each. Clocks per block (0 / 1 / 2 / 3): lw 0 96,257 / 96,250 / 88,677 / 68,040; lw 1 48,774 / 48,763 / 47,442 / 36,398; lw 2 28,365 / 28,370 / 25,729 / 19,788; lw 3 18,109 / 18,089 / 14,860 / 11,480; lw 4 11,398 / 11,420 / 9,365 / 7,279; lw 5 8,057 / 8,042 / 6,606 / 5,178. **lw 5: "0 of 4 jobs correct"; each job reports `status 1 n_out 0 (want 1, 1)`** (the host's verify: status word 1, number of outputs 0 against 1 wanted). `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json --blocks 8 --unit-lw 5 --lw 5`, one job per block, 8 blocks (6,534 / 5,870 / 5,539 / 5,171 / 4,543 / 4,443 / 3,712 / 3,528 clocks): "0 of 8 jobs correct", the same message on all eight; `ladder_show.py` on that file printed `8 of 8 jobs match` (all eight output values correct in far RAM).
**Computed, speedup over lw 0 (lw 1 to 5):** block 0: 1.97, 3.39, 5.32, 8.45, 11.95; block 1: 1.97, 3.38, 5.32, 8.43, 11.97; block 2: 1.87, 3.45, 5.97, 9.47, 13.42; block 3: 1.87, 3.44, 5.93, 9.35, 13.14 (lw 5 figures are of runs the host calls wrong).
**Read, not settled:** (1) `ladder_show` reads the output words from far RAM; the 8-block lw 5 run used the same far-RAM layout and addresses as the earlier lw 0 eight-block run (#1529), so those output words may be left over from that run (the card was not reloaded in between); the match therefore does not prove the lw 5 run wrote them. (2) The finish is real (the sequencer saw all blocks end, with clocks that fall steadily from lw 4), and the status word reads 1, but the output count n_out reads 0; where those two words come from (written by the job at its end, or left over) was not checked.
**Measured in simulation (new test_sys21p.py, S 32, ULW 5, one task alone at lw 0 to 5):** a program that stores three words to private data memory and loads them back gives the right a0 at every width including lw 5 (clocks 2,377 / 1,225 / 649 / 361 / 217 / 145): RESULT PASS. So a plain store-then-load round trip at 32 lanes is fine; the real job end (status and n_out words, then the copy of results to far RAM) is not covered by it.
**Not done / next:** (a) reload the 8 x 32 bitstream and run the lw 5 job first, then lw 4, to see whether lw 5 writes output at all from a clean state; (b) run the real job code at 32 lanes in simulation and compare the words it leaves in data memory with the reference (the pool testbench only reads a0 and shared RAM); (c) read what the job writes for n_out and status and which path copies it.

**#1531 (27 Nov) -- cause of the 32-lane failure found: back-to-back shared-RAM stores from a fused unit lose the earlier store (the dispatcher lets a second store overwrite the first while it waits for the slice's slot); reproduced in simulation; fixed in sc_sys_v22 (the unit waits); new build line `hk` made, not built.**
**Measured (card, Alan, 27 Nov 01:15, hj8s32u32n1i1024 freshly loaded, first-read test B C A B A, lw 5 run first):** `ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 8 --unit-lw 5 --lw 5`: 8,037 / 8,043 / 6,601 / 5,175 clocks, "0 of 4 jobs correct", each job `status 1 n_out 0 (want 1, 1)`; the lw 4 run straight after: 4 of 4 correct (11,423 / 11,400 / 9,384 / 7,302). `ladder_show.py` on the lw 5 results file h5.json: `4 of 4 jobs match` (5050, 134650, 242, 9649). So from a clean card the 32-lane run wrote the correct output words; only n_out (and not status) was wrong. This settles the doubt of #1530: the earlier match was not leftover data.
**Read from the source (jobio/lib_io.s, jobio/vsum.s):** a job ends with three stores to its shared window in quick succession: the output word, then `sw a0,20(t0)` (n_out, word 5), then `sw t1,16(t0)` (status, word 4), with only a few instructions between. The dispatcher's shared-store handling (sc_sys_v21.v lines 626 to 633) keeps one pending word (`dacc`, `rq_a`, flag `dpend`) until the slice's slot comes round (up to S = 32 clocks); a fused unit's store (`u_d_wwe`) writes `dacc` and `rq_a` again even when `dpend` is still set, and only loads wait (`stall_d = need_rd`). That fits what the card showed: output landed, n_out lost (overwritten by the status store), status landed.
**Measured in simulation (test_sys21p.py, S 32, ULW 5; new program `sstore`: three consecutive stores to shared words 20, 21, 22, then loads them back, expected a0 = 187):** v21 gives the right answer at lw 0, 1, 2 and the WRONG answer at lw 3 (0x8f), lw 4 (0x8f) and lw 5 (0x84): FAIL, so the bug is reproduced and it starts at 8 lanes at S 32. (The vector jobs on the card passed at lw 3 and 4 because their stores were spaced further apart; lw 5 is faster than the slot period.) Whether v16 to v20 have the same defect was not run; the code is the same, reading says yes.
**Fix (new files; v21 untouched): `sc_sys_v22.v`:** `stall_w = fz && dact && !dldr && sh_sel && !dviol && dpend` added to the stall (a fused unit that reaches a shared store while the previous shared store still waits for its slot waits too, like a load does). **Measured:** test_sys22p.py (S 32, ULW 5): sstore correct at lw 0 to 5 (clocks 2,337 / 1,249 / 673 / 401 / 297 / 277); full pool tests test_sys22.py all RESULT PASS with no FAIL: S 32 ULW 5 NU 1, S 16 ULW 4 NU 2, S 8 ULW 3 NU 2, S 4 ULW 2 NU 1.
**New build line `hk` (nothing built):** `fpga/sc_block_v6.v` (sc_block_v5 with sc_sys_v22), `fpga/sc_top_k7_v18.v`, `k7/make_mb_v13.py` (names `hk<NB>s<S>u<W>n<NU>i<IMEM>`), `k7/stage_mb_v12.sh`, `make_manifest_v2.py` (line hk), build files `hk4s16u16n1i1024` and `hk8s32u32n1i1024` with manifests. iverilog elaboration of the 8 x 32 top: only BSCANE2 missing (expected).
**Not done:** Vivado builds of hk (sizes and slack unknown; the added stall is one gate per slice, estimate only); card test of hk; whether v16 to v20 fail the same sstore test in simulation; whether the non-fused core can ever store twice before service (the existing message at the `dpend && dw_now` line guards it in simulation, not in hardware).

**#1532 (27 Nov) -- hk builds finished, both met timing: hk4s16u16n1i1024 70,077 LUTs (23.5 %) +3.699 / +0.039 ns; hk8s32u32n1i1024 246,006 LUTs (82.4 %) +0.493 / +0.026 ns. Not yet on the card.**
**Measured (Alan's run_builds_v1.sh summary, builds 27 Nov 01:51 to 03:18, pasted 10:04; vivado exit 0 for both, Synth 8-9873 count 0, ERROR lines 0, no failing endpoints):** hk4s16u16n1i1024: Slice LUTs 70,077, registers 45,100, setup WNS +3.699, hold WHS +0.039, build time 22 min 40 s. hk8s32u32n1i1024: LUTs 246,006 (82.39 %), registers 150,655 (25.23 %), WNS +0.493, WHS +0.026, build time 1 h 5 min (02:13:54 to 03:18:43).
**Computed (differences against the hj builds with the same shape, single builds):** the store-wait fix of sc_sys_v22 costs +740 LUTs at 4 x 16 (70,077 against 69,337, +1.1 %) and +2,589 LUTs at 8 x 32 (246,006 against 243,417, +1.1 %): about 10 to 12 LUTs per slice, more than the "one gate per slice" I expected in #1531 (that was an estimate; the cause of the larger figure is not looked into; Vivado re-partitions logic from build to build, so part may be noise). Registers: +13 / +5.
**Computed, correction to #1526 and the to-do list:** setup slack at 4 x 16 was +2.987 (hh4s16u16n1), +0.358 (hj i128), +0.574 (hj i1024), +3.699 (hk i1024). The designs differ only by the dispatcher changes, so run-to-run spread of the single-build slack at this size is at least 3 ns. The fall of 2.6 ns that #1526 read as a cost of the v21 dispatcher is therefore NOT shown to be one; it may be build variation. The to-do item "why slack fell with v21" is withdrawn; slack at 4 x 16 should be read as +0.4 to +3.7 ns, and at 8 x 32 as +0.16 (hh), +0.60 (hj), +0.49 (hk).
**Meaning:** both hk builds are cleared to load (timing met, no errors). 8 x 32 with 32-lane units is now 246,006 LUTs, 82.4 %, over the 80 % working target but under the part's 100 %; the 32-lane unit estimate of 5,000 to 5,300 LUTs (#1526) stays about right (the model gives 241,000 to 243,000 plus the +2,589).
**Recorded:** builds_measured_v1.json (both builds), manifests for both (hk8s32u32n1i1024 verdict on the Kintex card: tight, 82.4 %), K7_BUILDS_AND_LOADING table rows, to-do list. **Not done:** card tests of hk4s16u16n1i1024 and hk8s32u32n1i1024 (the 32-lane width, lw 5, is the one that failed on hj8s32u32n1i1024); the shared-store test of v16 to v20 in simulation.

**#1533 (27 Nov) -- the 32-lane unit works on the card: `hk8s32u32n1i1024` (8 x 32, 256 slices, a 32-lane unit per block, store-wait fix) passes every test, including lw 5 on four blocks and eight different jobs at once on eight blocks, all correct. Confirms the cause and fix of #1531.**
**Measured (Alan's card output, 27 Nov 10:09; load `Done`, `init 1 done 1` after earlier USB write errors cleared by a retry; board version 12, `S` 32, `imem_words` 1024):** first-read test B C A B A (first thing after the load). `ladder_3_more_data.json --blocks 8 --unit-lw 5 --lw 5` (run before anything else): **4 of 4 jobs correct** (clocks 8,042 / 8,038 / 6,633 / 5,178; on hj this run said 0 of 4, n_out 0). `ladder_6_eight_jobs.json --blocks 8 --unit-lw 5 --lw 5`, eight jobs on eight blocks each with its own 32-lane unit: **8 of 8 jobs correct**, `ladder_show` 8 of 8 match (clocks per block 6,565 / 5,882 / 5,553 / 5,165 / 4,522 / 4,456 / 3,719 / 3,539). `ladder_3_more_data --lw N`, N = 0 to 4: 4 of 4 correct in all five runs; clocks (blocks 0 / 1 / 2 / 3): lw 0 96,233 / 96,259 / 88,669 / 68,023; lw 1 48,785 / 48,772 / 47,457 / 36,390; lw 2 28,366 / 28,352 / 25,711 / 19,801; lw 3 18,111 / 18,103 / 14,837 / 11,492; lw 4 11,398 / 11,414 / 9,385 / 7,291.
**Computed, speedup over lw 0 (lw 1 to 5):** block 0: 1.97, 3.39, 5.31, 8.44, 11.97; block 1: 1.97, 3.40, 5.32, 8.43, 11.98; block 2: 1.87, 3.45, 5.98, 9.45, 13.37; block 3: 1.87, 3.44, 5.92, 9.33, 13.14. lw 5 against lw 4: 1.42 times (blocks 0, 1), 1.41 times (block 2). Eight-job run at lw 5 against the hj lw 0 eight-job run of #1529 (same jobs, same layout; different bitstream, lw 0 clocks 87,647 / 69,116 / 73,192 / 60,082 / 58,760 / 51,043 / 42,021 / 44,292): 13.35, 11.75, 13.18, 11.63, 12.99, 11.45, 11.30, 12.52 times, 12.34 over the summed clocks. lw 0 to 4 clocks agree with the hj build within 0.3 %.

(3) The design reached is 246,006 LUTs (82.4 %), setup slack +0.493 ns, hold +0.026 ns (#1532).
**Not shown:** the vector jobs are small, so the speedup stays near 12 to 13 times at 32 lanes (fixed per-job cost, #1527 reading); a compute-heavy program on the card (to show the fetch fix, #1517, at its full size); programs above 128 code words (`--imem 1024`); hk4s16u16n1i1024 on the card; the same sstore test against v16 to v20 (older builds) in simulation; several fused units in one block (NU above 1); the other job kinds at 32 lanes.
**Note on the USB errors:** loading failed with "usb bulk write failed" earlier (before the load that worked); a retry after the adapter was reconnected cleared it. Not examined further.

**#1534 (27 Nov) -- front end, first working version: `score_shell_v2.py` (the existing S-Core shell plus a build store) and `score_store_v1.py`: commands store, builds, target, fit, use, load; passes its tests with a fake loader and fake card; not yet tried on the real card.**
**What it does (new files; score_shell_v1.py, test_score_shell_v1.py and SHELL.md are untouched and still pass):** `store FOLDER` remembers (in ~/.score_shell.json) the folder holding generated Verilog, manifests, bitstreams and timing reports; the k7 folder of the checkout is always searched too. `builds` lists every build found (manifest files `sc_k7_<name>.manifest.json`, plus the measured ones in builds_measured_v1.json): blocks x slices, lanes, code words, LUTs (measured, or ~predicted), setup slack (from the build's timing_summary.txt, else from the measured file), the verdict for the Kintex card (recommended / tight / no / unknown), whether the bitstream is in the store, and what the card showed. `target` shows the boards (Kintex and Tang Nano 20K, Tang marked "no S-Core pool built, fit unknown"). `fit BLOCKS SLICES ULW UNITS [IMEM]` prices a design size without a build and says where it fits, and says "not known" where a unit size was never measured. `use BUILD` sets blocks, slices, unit-lw and the new setting `imem` (passed to the planner as --imem, which closes the "planner assumes 128" point of #1528) from the build. `load BUILD [confirm]` finds the bitstream, refuses a build whose timing failed, asks for the word `confirm` when timing is unknown, runs the loader as exactly `openFPGALoader -c ft232 FILE` (SRAM only, no flash option anywhere), then runs the first-read test and checks the board's slices per block and code words against the build, reporting any mismatch or an unhealthy test; a USB write failure is reported with the advice to reconnect.
**Measured (here, no card):** test_score_shell_v2.py (the v1 tests copied plus 27 new checks of the store, timing parser, fit, use, and load with a fake loader and fake card): ALL PASSED; test_score_shell_v1.py: ALL PASSED. Typed against the real repo: `builds` lists the eight known builds with their measured sizes and slack, `fit 8 32 5 1 1024` gives tight / 82 % and `fit 8 4 2 1` says the unit size is not known.
**Not done:** a run of `load` against the real card (needs the shell started inside `nix shell nixpkgs#openocd --command python3 score_shell_v2.py`); the user\'s own-build route (generate Verilog from a size, then build elsewhere, then import the bitstream) beyond reading manifests and bitstreams from the store folder; the extra fields in the board\'s V reply (blocks, lanes, units; the check uses slices per block and code words only); writing a manifest for a bitstream built outside (timing from timing_summary.txt is read when it sits next to the bitstream); ULW 5 in the `lw` setting is allowed by the planner but the shell help for `lw` now says 0..5 untested through the shell; SHELL_v2.md is generated by `python3 score_shell_v2.py --write-help`.

**#1535 (27 Nov) -- correction to #1534: first real `load` through score_shell_v2 failed on the card side with "Unknown device with IDCODE 0x23751093"; cause and fix.**
**Seen (Alan, card, first try):** `load hk8s32u32n1i1024` -> loader exit 1, "JTAG init failed with: Unknown device with IDCODE: 0x23751093 (Xilinx, part 0x1b)". 0x23751093 is the XC7K480T, which the nix openFPGALoader 1.1.1 used by hand all session accepts. **Reading (not yet confirmed on his machine):** score_store_v1 looked for `openFPGALoader` on PATH before the nix one, so a different (older) loader that does not know this chip was probably run. It was my ordering in #1534; the tests used a fake loader so could not show it. **Changed (new test coverage, no card):** score_store_v1.find_loader order is now the `loader` setting, then the nix 1.1.1 path, then PATH; new shell setting `loader`; a failed load prints the loader path used and, for an unknown-IDCODE failure, says to set the nix one. The "set loader PATH" text in #1534 named a setting that did not exist; it does now. test_score_shell_v2.py: ALL PASSED (2 new checks). **Open:** whether this fixes it on his machine; a fresh `load` on the card.

**#1536 (27 Nov) -- first real `load` through score_shell_v2 on the card: worked (confirms the #1535 loader fix).**
**Measured (Alan, 10:18, card):** started `nix shell nixpkgs#openocd --command python3 score_shell_v2.py` in s-core; `load hk8s32u32n1i1024` -> "loaded ... (setup +0.493 ns, hold +0.026 ns, failing endpoints 0 (timing_summary.txt))", then "first-read test healthy; the board reports 32 slices per block and 1024 code words, as the build says"; settings became blocks 8, unit-lw 5, imem 1024. So the store lookup, timing check from the build's own timing_summary.txt, SRAM load with the nix loader, first-read test and board-vs-build check all ran on real hardware. The earlier failure (#1535) was the loader choice. **Not yet tried through the shell:** `run` of jobs on the card, a refused (failed-timing) load on real files, the Tang card.

**#1537 (27 Nov) -- first job run through score_shell_v2 on the real card (hk8s32u32n1i1024 loaded by the shell): correct.**
**Measured (Alan, 10:19, card):** `add vsum 1 2 3 4`, `add vmax 7 99 42 13`, `set lw 5`, `run` -> board reports version 12, 32 slices per block, 1024 code words, 8 blocks asked for; shared RAM 256 words/block read from the board; 2 jobs on blocks 0 and 1 (code 46 and 49 of 1024 words); 201 words loaded to far RAM in 0.7 s; both blocks finished in 0.08 s, 10 steps, 1144 and 1155 clocks; "2 of 2 jobs correct", and the shell's own comparison: job 0 vsum [10] MATCH, job 1 vmax [99] MATCH. Scope: two tiny jobs at width lw 5 (32-lane fusion), one job per block, not a speed test (the fixed per-job cost dominates at this size, #1528). So the whole shell path (load, check, plan, run, compare) now works on the card. **Not tried:** a larger mix of jobs through the shell, the `save`/`open` of a job set on the card, the Tang card.

**#1538 (27 Nov) -- the browser window carries the build store: new `scwin_v3.py` (clone of scwin_v2.py on score_shell_v2) with a BUILDS panel; tested with fakes, not yet seen in a browser on the card.**
**What changed (new files; scwin_v2.py, scwin.py and their tests untouched, test_scwin_v2.py still passes):** `scwin_v3.py` is scwin_v2.py with `score_shell_v2` as its shell, so `store`, `builds`, `target`, `fit`, `use`, `load` and the `imem`/`loader` settings all work in the window's prompt. New BUILDS panel (above the cores): the store folder, the chosen build, and a table of the builds with size, lanes, code words, LUTs (measured or ~predicted), setup slack (red when timing failed, yellow when unknown), the Kintex verdict, whether the bitstream is found, and the card note as a hover text; each row has `use` and `load` buttons that send exactly the commands `use NAME` / `load NAME` through the same one-command-at-a-time path as the prompt, so the refusals (failed timing, unknown timing needs `confirm`, no bitstream) and the post-load board check apply unchanged; the `load` button is disabled when no bitstream is found. The panel is read from folders at most every 3 s. **Measured (here):** test_scwin_v3.py (the v2 checks copied plus 6 new: state has the repo builds; `store` makes a store's builds appear with slack and bit flags and flags the failed one; `use` sets the settings; `load` of a failed-timing build is refused with the loader not called; `load` of a good one runs the loader as `-c ft232 FILE` and the first-read check; the page text has the panel and buttons): ALL PASSED; test_scwin_v2.py: ALL PASSED. **Not done:** nobody has looked at the panel in a real browser (page text and API tested only); `load` from the window on the real card; a bigger job set through shell or window on the card (commands given to Alan, result pending).

**#1539 (27 Nov) -- `use`/`load` accept a short build name that fits exactly one build.**
**Seen (Alan, 11:29):** `use hk8s32u32n1` -> "no build ...; similar: hk8s32u32n1i1024" (the shell wanted the full name; he had cut it short). **Changed:** score_store_v1.get_build now takes a unique substring (e.g. hk8s32u32n1 gives hk8s32u32n1i1024); a name fitting two or more builds (e.g. hj4s) is still refused with the list; a name fitting none is refused as before. **#1540 (27 Nov) -- eight-job ladder through the shell/window on hk8s32u32n1i1024: 8 of 8 correct at lw 0 and lw 5; whole-run speedup 13.4 times (slowest block); window fixes in `scwin_v4.py`.**
**Measured (Alan, 11:33, card; `use hk8s32u32n1i1024`, `open ladder_6_eight_jobs.json`, 8 blocks, one job per block, vsum/vmax over 37 to 79 words):** lw 0: 8 of 8 correct, per block clocks 87652, 69142, 73196, 60081, 58754, 51063, 42010, 44296 (slowest 87652 = 1.75 ms, the card's own figure). lw 5 (32 lanes fused): 8 of 8 correct, 6548, 5907, 5548, 5187, 4533, 4456, 3743, 3544 (slowest 6548 = 0.13 ms). Per-block ratio 11.2 to 13.4 times; sum of clocks 486,194 to 39,466 = 12.3 times; slowest block 87,652 to 6,548 = 13.4 times (ratios computed from the printed clocks, not a separate measurement). Matches the 12 to 13 times of #1533. Host wall time was 0.13 s for both runs and is dominated by the polling and loading (1,224 words in 2.0 s), so it says nothing about the card speed. Each setting was run once (not a repeat; #1417 says a first run after a load can be slower). Both fixes are tested only as page text (no browser here), so Alan needs to look once. scwin_v3.py is left as it was (it works from the command line input one line at a time). **#1541 (27 Nov) -- batch jobs in the shell and the window (new score_shell_v3.py, scwin_v5.py), and the cores panel redrawn as squares only. Tested with fakes; not yet run on the card or seen in a browser.**
**Asked (Alan, 11:39):** (1) the cores panel only updates when a short run has finished and is too big for a large design: show only squares, one per core, coloured by what it is doing; (2) let both the graphical front end and the command line accept batch jobs, so a user can design and run in one go, for the larger jobs that come next. **Cores panel (scwin_v5.py):** one small square per slice, no text; colours: dark = idle, yellow = taken and waiting, green = running, dim green = was in use at some poll during the run (new: the window remembers which slices were seen busy), red = the block\'s sequencer reported an error; the slices of a fused group touch; hover shows block, slice, state, class, task, fused width; square size is picked from the panel width so 16 blocks of 64 or 8 of 32 fit. The picture is still a snapshot four times a second, so it only shows what a poll catches; the 22 to 25 ms first poll of #1540 is why the eight-job runs showed an empty picture (their cards runs last 0.1 to 1.8 ms), a longer job is needed to see it live. **Batch (score_shell_v3.py, scwin_v5.py; v1/v2/v3/v4 untouched):** new shell command `batch FILE [continue]` runs a file of the same commands you type (make, add, open, set, use, load, run...), one per line or separated by ;, # comments; it prints each command as it starts, stops at the first command that reports an error unless `continue` is given (a run that does not finish cleanly counts as an error), may call another batch up to 4 deep, and ends with a summary listing every run (jobs correct, slowest block clocks, lw) and one line of commands/runs/errors (green only if no error and every run fully correct). Command line: `-f FILE` and `-c CMDS` use the same runner, stop at the first error and exit with code 1 (`-k` goes on); piped input too. Window: `batch FILE` at the prompt, and a new collapsible Batch box (text area, run button, keep-going tick, file chooser) that posts to `/api/batch` (same one-command-at-a-time lock and X-Score guard); the window clears its plan/verdict/clocks panels at the start of every command inside a batch via a new hook, so the panels follow the last run. Example file `batches/ladder6_compare.score` (the #1540 comparison, each width twice). **Measured (here, no card, no browser):** test_score_shell_v3.py (v2 checks plus 15 batch checks: file, comments, ;, summary with slowest/lw, stop and continue, unreadable file, usage, not-all-correct flag, nesting and depth limit, -f/-k exit codes, piped input, completion): ALL PASSED; test_scwin_v5.py (v4 checks minus the two on the old box labels, plus 11: empty/refused/busy batch, batch run and log, panels follow the last run, `batch FILE` at the prompt, keep-going, a seen slice shows as used, page text for squares and Batch box): ALL PASSED; v2, v3, v4 window tests still pass. **Found while testing:** on the fake 4-slice board lw 5 is refused by the planner ("needs 32 slices") and the batch summary flags that run; that is the intended behaviour. **Not done:** a batch on the real card; the Batch box and squares in a real browser (page text only); a batch step that waits or repeats N times; large-design runs (the next target).

**#1542 (27 Nov) -- cores panel flicker and right-hand column shaking (seen by Alan in scwin_v5.py): new scwin_v6.py. Fix tested as page text only; not yet seen in a browser.**
**Seen (Alan, 11:49):** batch selector and commands run well; the cores display shrinks to blank, then opens again at a new size, over and over, and every panel on the right resizes with it. **Reading (from the page code, not reproduced in a browser here):** two causes in my #1541 page. (1) every poll (about 3 per second while busy) replaced the innerHTML of the Builds, Cores, Jobs, Plan and Settings panels even when nothing had changed, so each panel was emptied and refilled and the column re-laid out; (2) the square size was worked out from the width of the Cores box itself, and that box has a height limit with a scrollbar, so a scrollbar appearing shrank the width, which shrank the squares, which removed the scrollbar, and so on. **Changed (scwin_v6.py, v5 untouched):** `setIf` redraws a panel only when its html differs from the last one; square size comes from the width of the column, not the box; `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on the boxes. test_scwin_v6.py: ALL PASSED (v5 checks plus one page-text check). The status read took 3.5 to 21 ms per read, which is why a run of 0.13 to 1.75 ms is never caught running. **Reading (not confirmed): the log is from scwin_v6.py** (it came about a minute after scwin_v7.py was pushed, and v6 is the version that clears the picture at the start of each run, the collapse he describes); v7 is meant to fix exactly that and he has not yet reported on it. **Changed anyway, so the panel cannot collapse whichever way the picture is redrawn (scwin_v8.py, v7 untouched):** the panel has a fixed pixel height (rows x (square size + 4)) and every row a fixed height; each row now has a left bar that is green when that block\'s sequencer reported done with steps above zero in the last run (red for a sequencer error), measured from the card\'s own status, so the blocks that ran show even when every square is dark; the squares stay per-slice and still only show what a poll catches. **Not claimed:** that the bar tells which slices ran (it is per block); that v7 or v8 cures the collapse in his browser (page text and state tests only, no browser here). test_scwin_v8.py: ALL PASSED (v7 checks plus 1; the v5 check on the crow class made less exact).

**#1547 (27 Nov) -- large designs in waves: scwaves_v1.py, score_shell_v4.py (`run` in waves, `waves`, `many`, `add ... from=J argfrom=J`), scwin_v9.py. Tested with a fake card and the real planner; not yet run on the real card.**
**Asked (Alan, 12:01, answers to my two questions):** a large design that is split and fed in parts with results gathered between; all three cases ("all 3 are very tempting"): many independent jobs, one huge data set in rounds, a staged pipeline; the front end decides the split. **What it does (new files; v1 to v3 of the shell, v1 to v8 of the window untouched):** `run` first tries the whole design as one run exactly as before; if the planner reports that it does not fit the far RAM ("far-RAM area ends"), or if any job reads the answers of earlier jobs, it runs the design in waves: the jobs are cut into the fewest consecutive waves that each fit (the planner of scblocks is asked on the host, by doubling then binary search); a wave is an ordinary run of its jobs; the answers are read back after each wave; if the card refuses a wave as too big, that wave is halved and the parts go on; at the end the answers of all jobs go into one result file (job numbers of the whole design), and the existing comparison (`check`) and long-job combining (`check_big`) run over all of them. (1) Many independent jobs: consecutive waves. (2) One huge data set: `addbig` pieces are jobs, carried in waves, combined at the end across waves. (3) Pipeline: `add KIND from=J` takes its input words from the answers of job J, `argfrom=J` takes its arg from the first answer word of J (J must be an earlier job; opened files are checked for missing jobs and circles); jobs are put in stages (one more than the highest stage they read from); a wave never mixes stages; a later stage is fed the card\'s OWN answers from the earlier wave, while the expected answers come from the Python reference chain, so a wrong stage 1 shows as DIFFERENT at stage 1 and in what is built on it. `waves` shows the wave plan without the card (blocks, imem, dsh, far-words, slices, lw from the settings); `many N KIND seq START COUNT STEP [stride=K]` adds N jobs in one checked step (1000 jobs in seconds); saved job files keep `from`/`argfrom` with no literal inputs (a plain scbatch run of such a file fails loudly, it does not run empty). The batch summary names the waves ("run 1: ..., 3 waves"); the run log records one run per `run` with the summed slowest-block clocks. The window (scwin_v9.py) runs the shell of v4; during waves the log has one line per wave and the cores picture is the last wave; the Plan panel is not filled for wave runs (the condensed wave lines are printed instead). Examples: `batches/waves_demo.score` (300 jobs with a small far-words setting so waves are needed; then a three-stage pipeline). **Measured (here, no card):** test_score_waves_v1.py: ALL PASSED (engine sizes 1 to 100, levels and their errors, 40 jobs in 3 waves from the real planner with a fake card, auto fallback and halving when the card refuses, addbig 3000 words across waves with the combined sum 4501500, pipeline stage by stage with the real stage-1 answer fed on, a wrong stage 1 shown as DIFFERENT, a wave that fails stops the run, save/open round trip, `many`); test_score_shell_v4.py, test_score_shell_v3.py, test_scwin_v9.py: ALL PASSED. **Not done / limits:** nothing run on the real card; the host plan uses the settings (not what the board reports) so the first wave can be refused and halved (it is handled, and costs one failed attempt); `from=` input is limited by what one job can hold (247 words); a stage cannot yet read from several jobs at once (concatenate) or feed a long job; no per-wave timing summary beyond the slowest block; the window\'s Plan panel is empty during waves; reload cases and results kept only in memory if a wave fails (earlier answers are not written until the end).

**#1548 (27 Nov) -- waves demo run on the real card: 300 jobs in 7 waves and a three-stage pipeline, all correct; one duplicated log line traced to a polling bug in the page; fixed in scwin_v10.py.**
**Measured (Alan, 12:12, card, window scwin_v9.py, build hk8s32u32n1i1024, `batches/waves_demo.score` pasted into the Batch box, 0 errors):** Part 1, `many 300 vsum seq 1 40 1`, lw 5, far-words held at 0x3000 on purpose: the host plan was 7 waves (43, 43, 43, 43, 43, 43, 42 jobs); the run went straight into waves ("does not fit the far RAM in one run"); every wave correct (43 of 43 ... 42 of 42), slowest block per wave 21,397, 21,395, 21,398, 21,391, 21,377, 21,386, 21,386 clocks (spread 0.1 %); 300 of 300 correct, all 300 answers MATCH the Python answers over the merged result file (spot-read job 299: 479,220); summed slowest-block clocks 149,730 over the seven waves. Each wave\'s card run was 0.43 ms. The waves were forced by the setting (the card has 65,536 words of far RAM); this shows the mechanism and the combining on real hardware, not that the card was out of room. Part 2, pipeline: stage 1 `vsum` of 1 to 100 gave 5050 (96,252 clocks at lw 0), stage 2 `vscale from=0 arg=3` was fed that card answer and gave 10101 (7,287 clocks), stage 3 `vsum from=1` was fed 10101 and gave 10101 (6,766 clocks); 3 of 3 correct and MATCH against the reference chain. The batch summary read "run 1: 300 of 300 jobs correct, slowest block 149730 clocks, lw 5, 7 waves" and "run 2: 3 of 3 ... 3 waves". Not measured: wall-clock time per wave (each wave also loads far RAM, about 2 s in earlier runs, so 7 waves took about as long again in host time as in card time); a design that really exceeds the card\'s far RAM; the auto-halving path on a real card (not triggered).
**Seen in the paste, a bug:** the line "waves: 300 jobs in 7 waves (...)" appears twice (the pipeline run\'s line appears once). Not in the shell (the fake-card test prints it once, count checked). Cause found in the window page: `tick()` (the page\'s polling loop) schedules its own next call, and the Enter and `run batch` handlers also call `tick()`, so every press started an additional permanent polling loop; two loops can fetch the same log lines before either has moved its position, and the page then shows a line twice (and each press added polling load). This is my fault from scwin_v2 on (the Enter handler was already like that) and #1538/#1541 added the second handler. Reading, not reproduced in a browser. **Changed (scwin_v10.py, v9 untouched):** one polling loop only (a tick in progress is not started again, one timer), and a log line is shown only if its number is newer than the last shown. test_scwin_v10.py: ALL PASSED (page-text check). Needs a look by Alan: after several runs of the batch no line should repeat.

**#1549 (27 Nov) -- cores panel after short runs: all squares stayed black (Alan, waves demo); squares now show "used" after a run for blocks that ran; scwin_v11.py.**
**Measured (Alan, card, scwin_v9.py):** the 300-job and pipeline runs gave only the green bar at the left of each row (block finished, steps > 0); all squares stayed dark. **Cause (reading, consistent with the measured timing lines):** a square turned dim green only if a poll saw that slice allocated or leader, and runs of 0.14 to 0.43 ms are finished long before the first poll (22 to 24 ms later; "polls that found it busy: 0" in every wave). The card does not keep which slices a finished run used. **Changed (scwin_v11.py, v10 untouched):** when a run has finished, every square of a block whose sequencer reported steps > 0 (and no error) is shown as used; a block with 0 steps stays idle; during a run nothing changes (idle until seen busy). This is block-level knowledge: it does NOT say which cores inside the block did the work (a lw 0 run on a 32-slice block also shows 32 used squares). test_scwin_v11.py: ALL PASSED (state only; not seen in a browser). **Not solved:** seeing which cores run needs a run long enough for the polls to catch it, or a latch on the card that records slices used (hardware change, not made).

**#1550 (27 Nov) -- first full S-Core manual: `s-core/S_CORE_MANUAL_v1.md` (DRAFT v1, from start-up to builds, loading, running, waves, batch files, the window, the assembly language, limits).**
**Done:** one document in 15 chapters that joins the older guides (README, LADDER, PROGRAMS, BATCH_FORMAT, K7_BUILDS_AND_LOADING, SHELL_v4) into one story for the Kintex card over JTAG, the `hk` line, `score_shell_v4.py` and `scwin_v11.py`; it labels every number as measured (with its ledger entry) or read. It covers the language as it exists (RV32I assembly, the five-part program shape, the rules, `make` recipes, `register`, the instruction-set simulator `jobiss.py`) and says plainly that no interpreter or compiler for a higher-level language exists (chapter 11; the `make` recipe language is the nearest thing). **Not done / caveats:** not read through by Alan; the build commands (chapter 4.4) follow the scripts but were not re-run for this manual; the "used squares" description is from page-state tests, not a browser; PCIe is described only as a difference in what the JTAG numbers mean, no PCIe code exists. S-Core still has no manual generator of its own (the UniCell `docs/build_manual.py` was not touched), so this is a plain markdown file in `s-core/`. The older gap list (`USAGE_MANUAL_AND_SCALE_TESTS_PLAN_v1.md`, 26 Nov) said no single usage manual existed; this is the first version of it.

**#1551 (27 Nov) -- Alan asked that the gaps named in the manual go on the to-do list: new section F in `s-core/S_CORE_TODO.md`.**
**Added (all open, none designed):** multiply and divide (the core has none; software routine in the code library, or hardware in the fusion unit; measure the shift-add cost first), byte and halfword loads and stores (not available; shared RAM is word-wide), a table of exactly which instructions the core runs today taken from the RTL (the manual lists the rules but not an RTL-checked instruction list; I wrote the rules from PROGRAMS.md and the `jobiss.py` header, so this is a real gap), the interpreter or higher-level language side (grow `make` recipes, `from=` pipelines; check Onion for prior art), and keeping the manual in step. No code changed.

**#1552 (27 Nov) -- v11 seen working in the window (no duplicate line); the shell did not know what was on the card (use and load both just set `build`); new score_shell_v5.py and scwin_v12.py; compute-heavy batch written.**
**Measured (Alan, window v11, same waves demo):** "waves: 300 jobs in 7 waves" now appears once (was twice in v9), 300 of 300 and 3 of 3 correct again, repeat clocks within 0.1 % (149,763 against 149,730 and 149,733 earlier). Not yet reported by him: whether the "used" squares showed. **Alan's question:** after `load` there should be a notice that the design on the card changed; `use` changes the design in use but is it on the card? **Answer, from the code (score_shell_v2 do_use / do_load, copied into v4):** `use` only sets blocks, slices, unit-lw and imem on the host; only `load` touches the card. Both set the same `self.build`, so the shell could not tell a chosen build from a loaded one. After `load A` then `use B`, `run` planned for B (blocks, unit-lw) while the card held A. The host asks the board for slices and shared RAM size but not for the block count, so a wrong block count was possible. **Changed (clones, v4 and v11 untouched):** `score_shell_v5.py`: remembers `card_build` (set only by a successful `load`; cleared if the first-read test or the board check fails); `use` now says "host settings only" and what the card holds (or that nothing was loaded in this session); new command `card`; `load` ends with "now on the card: NAME (loaded in this session)"; `run` is refused when a build was loaded in this session and the chosen build is a different one, naming both and the two ways out. A build loaded by hand (nothing loaded in this session) never blocks `run`: the shell cannot know. `SHELL_v5.md` generated. `scwin_v12.py` (runs shell v5): the Builds panel shows "chosen (host settings)" and "on the card" separately, marks the row `[on card]`, and says `use` does not touch the card. test_score_shell_v5.py ALL PASSED (10 new checks with the fake loader and fake card), test_scwin_v12.py ALL PASSED (state and page text only; not seen in a browser). **Limit:** the shell only knows what it loaded itself; after a restart it does not know. Reading the card's own build identity would need the card to report one (the host reply has slices and code words but no blocks or build name, TODO #1520).
**Compute-heavy batch (`s-core/batches/compute_heavy.score`, not run):** eight `vstress` jobs (one per block, 100 words each, add and store repeated `arg` times), arg 50 then 150, at lw 0 and lw 5. Sizes from the instruction-set simulator (35,337 steps at arg 50; 635 steps for vsum 100 which measured 96,250 clocks, about 150 clocks per step): ESTIMATED 0.1 s and 0.3 s at lw 0, about 13 times shorter at lw 5. The card stops waiting for a batch at about 1.3 s (wait 0xFFFF x 1024 clocks, read from `jobcomp_v2.compile`), so arg is kept to 150; the batch stops at the first error. Whether the polls catch cores running is the thing to see.

**#1554 (27 Nov) -- on the real card: the "what is on the card" guard caught a wrong design, and the compute-heavy batch ran 8 of 8 correct four times with the status polls catching cores busy at lw 0.**
**Measured (Alan, 13:05, window with shell v5, card; hh16s16u1n1 was on the card from an earlier `load`):** (1) The batch started with `use hk8s32u32n1i1024`: the shell said "host settings only: the card still holds hh16s16u1n1", `card` showed they differ, and `run` was refused ("the card holds hh16s16u1n1 but the settings are for hk8s32u32n1i1024 ... `load` first"), nothing reached the card; the batch stopped at line 16 with 1 error. Alan then ran `load hk8s32u32n1i1024` ("now on the card: hk8s32u32n1i1024 (loaded in this session)") and the same batch, where `use` now said "this build is on the card". This is the case #1552 was written for (a batch that picked a build other than the loaded one); it showed on hardware that the guard works. (2) `batches/compute_heavy.score`, 14 commands, 4 runs, 0 errors, every run 8 of 8 correct on 8 blocks: vstress over 100 words, arg 50 at lw 0: 5,488,5xx clocks on every block (109.77 ms), at lw 5: 483,2xx (9.67 ms); arg 150 at lw 0: 16,451,7xx (329.03 ms), at lw 5: 1,446,4xx (28.93 ms). Clocks are the same on all eight blocks to within 0.0003 %. lw 0 to lw 5 speedup 11.36 times (arg 50) and 11.37 times (arg 150) on this job, against 13.4 times on the ladder-6 set (#1540); the vstress loop is mostly shared-RAM loads and stores, which I read as the likely reason, not tested. My estimates (#1552) were close: 0.1 s and 0.3 s at lw 0 (measured 0.110 and 0.329 s); about 155 clocks per instruction step (5,488,523 clocks over 35,337 simulator steps); lw 5 I had put at "about 13 times shorter", measured 11.4. (3) Status polls: arg 50 at lw 0: 16 polls, first at 22 ms found it busy, 8 polls busy; arg 150 at lw 0: 40 polls, 32 busy; both lw 5 runs (9.67 and 28.93 ms): 0 polls busy (first poll at 23 and 29 ms), as I had expected. The host-side time "all 8 blocks finished in" was 0.21 s, 0.11 s, 0.44 s, 0.13 s, longer than the card's own time because of the polling; loading far RAM took 2.0 to 2.1 s every run. **Not reported by Alan:** whether the Cores panel showed running squares during the lw 0 runs (it should, the polls caught cores busy). **Limit:** this shows the heavy job runs correctly and is catchable; it does not show per-core placement.

He judged the runs "a bit quick" and expects the live view to improve with a PCIe link (status reads of 3 to 9 ms over JTAG are the limit, #1548).
