**#1556 (27 Nov) -- the card can be asked what is loaded: a LABEL the shell writes in far RAM after `load`, and a block-count PROBE; card_label_v1.py, score_shell_v6.py, scwin_v14.py. FAKE-CARD TESTS ONLY; two assumptions about the real card are not measured.**
**Question (Alan):** can a stored word in far RAM, reachable over JTAG, hold the design label? **Answer and what was built:** yes, with no new build. **Facts read from code (not measured on the card):** the V reply (host_sc.dec_V) is 8 bytes (b'SC', version 12 on this whole line, S, imem words, dmem words) with no spare field; Z gives shared words; nothing reports blocks, lanes, units or a name. host_bridge_v13.v line 217: `O k` selects block k and a k at or above NB is ignored. Far RAM is written and read through block 0 with the 'w' and 'r' commands (as far_ram_check_v1.py does); the planner starts at 0x2000 and nothing in batch_seq.v or the host code uses addresses below it. **Built:** (1) `card_label_v1.py`: `encode`/`decode` of a 19-word record at far RAM 0x0100 (marker 'SCLB', format 1, name up to 32 characters, blocks, slices, lanes, units, code words, shared words, time, checksum); `probe_blocks` (write 0x5C000000 + k into one shared RAM word of "block k" for k = 0..15 in order, read back in order, the first block that does not hold its own marker is the last real one); commands `read`, `write`, `probe`, `test` (far RAM 0x0000-0x01FF with two patterns, then a TEST label, then the instruction for step 2: load any build by hand and `read`, expect "LABEL none: empty"). (2) `score_shell_v6.py` (clone of v5): `load` writes the label after the first-read test and compares the probe with the build's block count (a difference is an error and the shell claims nothing); `card` reads board, probe and label, trusts the label only if it agrees with the board (slices, code words, blocks), and learns the build name from it; `card stamp BUILD` writes the label for a hand-loaded build after the board matches; `run` learns the label once per session when nothing was loaded, refuses when the label names another build than the chosen one, and refuses when `blocks` is larger than the card has (fewer is allowed: it is a valid use). A failed read (no card link) never blocks `run`. SHELL_v6.md generated. (3) `scwin_v14.py`: the Builds panel shows what the card reports (blocks, slices, code words), the label, "not trusted" and whether the knowledge comes from this session or the label. **Tests (no card):** test_card_label_v1.py ALL PASSED (record round trip, every decode failure, probe on fake cards of 1 to 16 blocks, write/read/test through the program, no-card exit code); test_score_shell_v6.py ALL PASSED (a new shell learns the label, a restart still refuses a wrong chosen build, a reload by hand clears the knowledge, probe mismatch at load, stamp ok and refused, blocks guard, no card link, a label that disagrees with the board); test_scwin_v14.py ALL PASSED including node --check of the page script and a node run of the new `builds()` function on four sample states (this is the check that was missing in #1553). **NOT MEASURED, to do on the card (Alan):** (a) that far RAM words 0x0000-0x01FF store and return data (`card_label_v1.py test`); (b) that loading a bitstream clears far RAM, so a stale label cannot survive a reload (step 2 of `test`: load any build by hand, then `card_label_v1.py read`, expect LABEL none: empty); (c) that the block probe counts 8 on hk8s32u32n1i1024 (it depends on the `O k` ignore rule above, read from the RTL, not tested). If (b) fails the label is not trustworthy on its own (the shell still cross-checks it with the board: slices, code words, blocks, which would not tell hj from hk). The label is the HOST'S record that it loaded this build, not something the hardware asserts; a new `I` command or the JTAG user code at the next build change would. The DDR3 builds (not this line) may keep far RAM across a reload. S_CORE_MANUAL_v1.md updated (chapter 5 and the file map, versions v6 and v14).

**#1557 (27 Nov) -- card label measured on the real card (Alan, 13:23): low far RAM stores data, the block probe counts 8, label written and read back by shell v6. Reload-clears-label still NOT measured.**
**Measured (Alan's output):** `card_label_v1.py test`: far RAM 0x0000-0x01FF, pattern address 0 bad words, pattern complement 0 bad words; TEST label written and read back ok. Shell v6 on hk8s32u32n1i1024: `load` ok (setup +0.493 ns, hold +0.026 ns, 0 failing endpoints), first-read healthy (32 slices, 1024 code words), "label written on the card (far RAM 0x0100): hk8s32u32n1i1024, the card has 8 blocks"; `card`: version 12, 8 blocks, 32 slices per block, 1024 code words, shared RAM 256 words; label hk8s32u32n1i1024 written 13:23:08 (8 blocks, 32 slices, 32 lanes, 1024 code words); chosen build agrees. **Closes from #1556:** (a) low far RAM stores data; (c) the block probe counted 8 on this card, so the `O k` ignore rule held here (one build, one card: not tested on other block counts). **Still open:** (b) whether a reload clears the label. The shell `load` wrote a new label over the TEST one, so this run does not test it. Needed: `card_label_v1.py test`, then a load by hand (not the shell), then `card_label_v1.py read` (expect LABEL none: empty). Not yet tried: scwin_v14 in the browser.

**#1558 (27 Nov) -- MEASURED: a reload clears the label (Alan, 13:33). Closes #1556 item (b).**
**Measured (Alan's output):** `card_label_v1.py test` ok (low far RAM 0 bad words both patterns, TEST label written and read back); then a hand load with openFPGALoader -c ft232 of k7/vivado_out_hj8s32u32n1i1024/hj8s32u32n1i1024.bit (SRAM load, Done); then `card_label_v1.py read`: BOARD version=12 S=32 imem=1024 shared=256, PROBE blocks=8, "LABEL none: empty". **So:** loading a bitstream by hand zeroed far RAM word 0x0100 area; the label cannot outlive a reload on this card. **Limits:** one hand load, of an hj build (same geometry as hk: 8 blocks, 32 slices, 1024 code words, so the board data did not tell hj from hk, only the empty label did); the DDR3 builds are not tested and may keep far RAM. The probe also counted 8 on the hj build (second build, same block count). Not yet tried: scwin_v14 in the browser. #1556 items (a), (b), (c) are now all measured.

**#1562 (27 Nov) -- guard rails on the card, part 1: stamp and the wrong-build refusal PASS; then two link failures on the hj build, cause NOT known (Alan, 13:46 to 13:59).**
**Measured (Alan's output):** hand load of hj8s32u32n1i1024, new shell v6: `card` -> no label; `card stamp hj8s32u32n1i1024` -> "label written ... the card has 8 blocks", "now on the card: hj... (stamped; the board matches the build)"; `card` -> label hj (13:46:33), chosen hj. Then `use hk8s32u32n1i1024` and `run` -> REFUSED: "the card holds hj8s32u32n1i1024 but the settings are for hk8s32u32n1i1024" (twice; the second time through waves_demo.score, which has `use hk...` on its own line 5, so my positive control was wrong: logged here as my test error). `use hj`, `set lw 5`, run 300 x vsum 40 words: wave 1 "0 of 43 jobs correct", status 1 n_out 0 (want 1, 1) on every job = the lw 5 defect of the hj build already measured in #1530 (fixed in the hk builds), not a guard or label effect. `set lw 4`, run: wave 1 "43 of 43 jobs correct, slowest block 49208 clocks", then "error: LinkError: unexpected drscan answer: ['drscan:', 'jtag', 'execute', 'failed']"; five more `run` all gave the same LinkError. `lsusb` still listed the FT232H (0403:6014); pgrep showed only the shell and its one openocd (plus defunct openocd children): no window or second program was running. After quitting and restarting the shell, `card` read the board fine (version 12, 8 blocks, 32 slices, 1024, shared 256) but said **no label on the card (empty)**, with no reload by hand in between (the label had been written at 13:46); the next `run` (hj, lw 4) failed at once with the same LinkError. Alan then powered the card off, reloaded, and reports it fine. **Not known:** why the JTAG scan failed on hj after wave 1; why the label was empty with the design still answering. Both happened on hj8s32u32n1i1024 (81.5 % of the chip); the hk build has run many waves without this. Hypotheses only (power or heat under sustained load; a card reset that wipes far RAM but leaves the design); neither is tested. **Next:** repeat the guard tests on hk8s32u32n1i1024, which is what the shell is normally used with, and see whether the link stays up.
